# AUD-037 — Full pre-release audit after the Codex and Copilot rework: bugs, the coding guide and the agent instructions

## Record metadata

- **Audit number:** 37.
- **Completed (UTC):** 2026-10-19.
- **Reviewer:** Claude Code coordinator with nine scoped reviewer agents, an adversarial verifier for every candidate, a second lens for the medium and release-blocking ones, and a completeness critic.
- **Model:** claude-opus-5-5 for the coordinator and every agent.
- **Reasoning effort:** max for the coordinator's planning and dispatch; later coordinator turns at a lower setting shown as 40 in the session metadata; agents unknown.
- **Reviewer phases:** Waves of reviewers (security, core recovery, SSKR; command line, API and architecture, rule sweep; documents, build and process; design quality), then verification of all 66 candidates; see JSON reviewerPhases.
- **Reviewed commit:** `f8d9a933ba9bde8290da39d42fa3a380de78385a` (main = origin/main). Started at `4978a620d43a8571b89ccfc01f3b5a58b3a8c421`; three concurrent Codex commits moved main during the review (abf7729, 7facfcc, f8d9a93), and every finding is bound to f8d9a93.
- **Working tree:** Clean apart from the untracked `docs/audits/AUD-037-harnesses/`.
- **Source fingerprint:** `a36bb290b44ac835b98aa84d01524ab144c3268221d87ccc72a74dda53134468` over the 1,157 tracked files at f8d9a93.
- **Artifacts:** Fresh dist/ built from f8d9a93; a fresh Linux x64 executable `mnemocode-0.1.0-linux-x64`, SHA-256 `f3a35d61000a7231bad3e0edd974c03e63479e5fdffbba91ec47d6be3166c634` (local build, not published); the SSKR WASM rebuilt without cache in its compile stage, byte-identical to the committed `ca60c5db…a87e`.
- **Verdict:** **FAIL for release acceptance**: 61 confirmed findings (7 medium, 54 low), 3 release blocking (AUD-037-UI001, AUD-037-BLD001, AUD-037-BLD002). Every automatic gate passes (check, format, build, 3,001 tests, self-test, PTY on source and executable, uncached WASM compile); the defects are in behaviour the gates do not see, in rules checked by review, and in the release process.

## Review evidence

### Scope and methodology

A full pre-release audit of MnemoCode 0.1.0 (no release exists yet; 135 unreleased commits) requested by the owner after Codex and Copilot reworked the code between 2026-10-14 and 2026-10-19 (+11,321/−4,577 lines in src/, +9,886 in test/ since 44f7c73). The owner asked for real bugs and for compliance with the workspace agent instructions and every rule of the coding guide. As a first release with substantial recovery, format and trust-boundary changes, the expanded workflow of the audit instructions applied: all 32 procedure checks assigned, reviewers in waves, an independent skeptic per candidate, and release-level local execution.

Scope: the whole repository at f8d9a93 — library (src/core, src/sskr, src/export, entry points), command line (src/cli), the SSKR Rust/WebAssembly engine with its vendored bc-shamir 0.13.0 and sskr 0.12.0, scripts and gates, tests, documents, audit records AUD-001 to AUD-036 and docs/REMEDIATION.md, CI workflows and the release process; the browser host in multi-chain-wallet-tools only for feature parity and the synced copy (read-only). Not in scope: changing anything during the baseline; Windows and macOS runtime (only CI results were read); browser runtime acceptance; GitHub settings beyond reading CI runs.

Method: nine reviewers with distinct scopes read the code and ran bounded probes against dist/ built from the snapshot (public data only: the abandon … about phrase, published vectors, zero or counting entropy). Their 66 candidates went through an adversarial verification workflow: one verifier per candidate told to refute it, reproduce it at f8d9a93 and check earlier records for duplicates; a second verifier for the 11 medium or release-blocking candidates; and a completeness critic, which added one finding. Seven candidates were refuted and are kept below as observations. The coordinator ran every command once, one heavy check at a time under a memory budget (about 4 GB free, 2 GB reserve), and reran the changed-code checks on f8d9a93.

Earlier findings were treated as a regression checklist: verifiers searched AUD-001 to AUD-036 and REMEDIATION.md for each candidate; regressions and missed variants of fixed items keep their original IDs under relatedFindings.

Concurrency: another agent (Codex) edited documents in the working tree at 15:45–15:50 UTC and committed and pushed abf7729, 7facfcc and f8d9a93 during the review; the review was stopped and resumed on f8d9a93. After the verification Codex also committed e28d81e and 61e163b; the latter fixes three candidates it read from the coordinator's unpublished working list (see Remediation).

### Coding-guide and agent-instruction conformance

The owner asked whether the code follows the coding guide (workspace .github/instructions/) and the agent instructions (workspace AGENTS.md). Every gate passes: pnpm check (toolchain pins, Markdown lists, audit privacy and package, no 50-token copies, code-rules, oxlint type-aware, clippy, rustfmt, tsc for source, tests and cards) and the Prettier check. The table judges every rule, including those checked only in review. Outcomes: pass; fail (with the findings); partly; n/a (not applicable, with the reason). Rules whose Check names a gate are judged on substance too, not only on the gate result.

In short: the mechanical rules hold because the gates hold them; many review-only rules do not. The largest gaps are the stability and single source of error codes (CORE-23/TS-06, AUD-037-API009 and the observation on generated code names), semantic duplicates (CORE-10), stopped and failed results reported as outcomes (CORE-22), secret residue (CORE-24/TS-07), screens that drop the step's information (CORE-39), documents that disagree with the code (DOC-06/07), and the unreleased history (AGENTS.md Commits).

CORE-15 was changed by the owner during the audit (2026-10-19): only lines of code count, comment-only and blank lines are left out. The repository's gates still count every line (AUD-037-ARC003); under the new counting joint-solve.ts has 616 lines of code.

| Rule | Outcome | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| CORE-01 Logic in library modules | partly | The colour-card plan rule (one plan per export) lives in the command line (src/cli/encode-export.ts) and again in the browser host (AUD-037-ARC002). |
| CORE-02 Modules are objects | pass | Private #fields throughout; exported tables mutable at run time are an observation (refuted as a finding). |
| CORE-03 Classes where they pay off | pass | Owner classes for secrets and plans; createCardSession keeps state in a closure (observation). |
| CORE-04 Independent modules | pass | Parts import independently; index.ts and sskr/index.ts are documented Node entry barrels; src/cli/sskr-command.ts is a pass-through barrel (AUD-037-ARC001 context). |
| CORE-05 Single responsibility | partly | wallet-evidence.ts and joint-solve.ts hold several jobs; split by job recommended (AUD-037-ARC003). |
| CORE-06 Open for extension | partly | templates.ts switches over the card family in three places plus a table (R5). |
| CORE-07 Substitution | pass | No library code checks which host implementation it has; one shape difference (synchronous fingerprintOf in ShareRestore) noted by the critic. |
| CORE-08 Small interfaces | pass | ShareCombiner, ShareRepairPlatform, ShareSplitPlatform are narrow. |

### Findings

#### SEC

##### AUD-037-SEC001 — Medium — On Windows, PNG/JPEG export and its readiness probe run a pdftocairo.exe/.com from the start folder before PATH (untrusted search path)

- **Category:** SEC
- **Severity:** medium
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: No mandatory security, integrity or recovery guarantee of 0.1.0 depends on how the external renderer is found. SECURITY.md says that a command which draws images may start other programs, and that the Windows job object is not applied then. PNG/JPEG export is optional: PDF is the default and needs no renderer. The documented main path, decoding by the heirs (HEIRS.md double-click start), never asks for image cards. The pdftocairo -v readiness probe is a capability check, not a documented safety check. No legal obligation of distribution is involved, and no workspace rule makes absolute-path resolution of subprocesses an explicit release precondition. The defect should be fixed before release, but it does not meet the release-blocking definition.
- **Rules:** CORE-24, CORE-49, DOC-07
- **Affected files and builds:** src/export/image-export.ts:44,49,59-70,113-149 (bare-name execFile for the -v probe and for each page, no cwd or absolute path); src/cli/card-questions.ts:103-112,182 (menu readiness probe, run in the menu process before any secret); src/cli/output-saving.ts:60 (command preflight probe); src/cli/protection.ts:111-116 (menu children spawned without cwd), 163,180 (runsPrograms true for drawing and for the menu); src/cli/sandbox-windows.ts:121-127 (no job object when runsPrograms; network left to the permission model only); SECURITY.md:48
- **Reproduction:** HEAD has moved since the audit snapshot to 61e163b. `git diff --stat f8d9a93 HEAD` touches only .git-blame-ignore-revs, docs/REMEDIATION.md, docs/audits/README.md, package.json and vitest.config.ts, so src/, dist/ and SECURITY.md are unchanged and the defect is present at both commits. In src/export/image-export.ts, `execute = promisify(execFile)` is called with IMAGE_RENDERER = "pdftocairo" and options {timeout, maxBuffer} only, both for ["-v"] (line 60) and for each page (line 120). There is no absolute path, cwd or env, and dist/export/image-export.js lines 26, 36 and 90 match. grep for chdir, cwd and NoDefaultCurrentDirectoryInExePath in src/ found nothing. runInOwnProcess calls spawn(process.execPath, args, {stdio:"inherit"}), so the children inherit the start folder. In sandbox-windows.ts restrict(), the job object is added only when !needs.runsPrograms, and it records "network is refused by the permission model only". The menu asks the card questions, which include missingImageRenderer, before it builds the --ask-secrets command for a child process, so the probe runs before any secret. Own bounded probe under /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-SEC001-lens/: a logging stub `plant/pdftocairo`, and probe.mjs, which imports dist/export/image-export.js and calls assertImageRenderer() with cwd=plant. (1) With the default PATH it printed "resolved" and the stub did not run; the real /usr/bin/pdftocairo answered. (2) With PATH=".:$PATH", modelling the Windows order that checks the working folder first, the stub log shows "STUB RAN: -v". I read the first verifier's probe (verify/AUD-037-SEC001/probe.mjs and stub-ran-render.log) and found it sound: in render mode the stub also received the private temporary document.pdf path. Windows was not run, and no libuv Windows source or Windows node.exe exists on this machine. The Windows claim rests on libuv src/win/process.c. uv_spawn uses options.cwd, or the inherited current folder when none is given. search_path() checks that folder before the PATH entries when NeedCurrentDirectoryForExePathW(L"") is true, which is the default. For a name with no extension it tries only "<name>.com", then "<name>.exe"; the literal name is tried first only when it has an extension. A search of docs/REMEDIATION.md and AUD-001 to AUD-036 for pdftocairo/Poppler resolution, CWE-427, planting and untrusted search paths found no tracked item. Earlier audits record the PATH-selected local Poppler only as a reviewed boundary.
- **Expected behaviour:** Image export and its readiness probe run only a pdftocairo resolved from absolute PATH entries, never an executable from the process's working folder or from relative or empty PATH entries. SECURITY.md:48 says which pdftocairo is run, so that its advice "Use a trusted local installation of Poppler" is enough.
- **Observed behaviour:** The renderer is started by the bare name "pdftocairo" with no cwd. MnemoCode never sets its own working folder, and menu children inherit it. On Linux with a normal PATH, a planted stub in the working folder is not run. With the working folder first in the search order, which is libuv's default on Windows, the stub runs at the "-v" probe and then receives the private temporary card PDF. On Windows, the image-drawing process has no job object and only the Node.js permission model for the network, which does not bind a non-Node child.
- **Impact:** On Windows, a pdftocairo.exe or pdftocairo.com in the folder MnemoCode was started from runs with the user's rights as soon as PNG/JPEG cards are chosen in the menu (encode, shares or sample cards) or an --images-dir command passes preflight. This happens before any secret is typed. The planted program then gets the private temporary PDF, which can restore the phrase, and as a same-user process it can watch the later secret entry and use the network. The plant also turns the normal "requires pdftocairo" refusal into success, so the attack works even when Poppler is not installed, which is common on Windows. The prerequisites are an attacker-controlled .exe or .com file in the start folder, and the user choosing image export. When the executable is double-clicked, the start folder is the executable's own folder, typically Downloads. README tells owners to run it from a terminal, where the folder is the user's choice, and HEIRS.md documents the double-click start only for decoding, which does not reach image export. A Downloads plant partly overlaps with planting files beside the executable itself, and SECURITY.md already says it cannot protect against a compromised system or programs. The Windows execution was not demonstrated.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R1; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json). A second, independent verifier checked severity and release blocking: agreed.
- **Recommended fix:** Resolve the renderer once to an absolute file: walk only the absolute entries of process.env.PATH, skipping empty and relative entries and never the working folder. On Windows try pdftocairo.exe (and .com if that is wanted). Pass the absolute path to execFile for both the "-v" probe and every page. When none is found, fail with the existing "png-jpeg-export-requires-local-poppler-pdftocairo-program" refusal. As defence in depth, pass cwd set to the fresh private mkdtemp folder for the render calls, since libuv searches options.cwd when it is given, and set NoDefaultCurrentDirectoryInExePath in process.env at start on Windows. Update SECURITY.md:48 to say that MnemoCode runs the pdftocairo found in an absolute PATH folder, never one from the current folder, and optionally name the resolved path in the readiness message or the self-test.
- **Required verification:** 1. A unit test places a stub pdftocairo (and on Windows pdftocairo.exe) in the working folder, in a relative PATH entry and in an absolute PATH folder. It shows that only the absolute-PATH stub runs, both for assertImageRenderer and for writeRenderedDocument, and that the refusal appears when only the working-folder or relative-entry stubs exist.
2. On a Windows runner, a harmless marker-writing pdftocairo.exe in the start folder of the built executable is not run by the menu's PNG choice or by `encode --images-dir`, while a PATH-installed Poppler or stub is.
3. The existing image-export and executable acceptance tests still pass.
4. If a Windows run before the fix shows the planted .exe receiving the temporary PDF, record that evidence and reassess the severity as high, since it would then be a reproduced defect with serious security impact.

##### AUD-037-SEC002 — Medium — Ctrl+C during an image export on the private screen (Linux/macOS) exits at once, leaving the raster card PDF and staged card images and printing no cancellation line

- **Category:** SEC
- **Severity:** medium
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: No mandatory guarantee in the release definition is broken. SECURITY.md (export bullet, line 47) already tells the person that hidden staging folders and a mnemocode-raster folder can remain, that each can hold a complete private copy, and that they should delete it. The leftover files are owner-only (0600 files in 0700 folders), and SECURITY.md says export precautions do not isolate against processes of the same account. A leftover copy is not a documented safety check that gets bypassed (checksum, self-test, protection, swap warning). No legal distribution obligation is involved. No workspace rule (AGENTS.md, AUDIT_STANDARD) makes a release depend on this behaviour. The path is a supported main path: the menu offers PNG/JPEG when pdftocairo is installed (src/cli/card-questions.ts:163-182), and the menu runs each entry in a child process that gets the same SIGINT. So it should be fixed before or soon after 0.1.0, but it does not meet the release-blocking test.
- **Rules:** CORE-24, CORE-22, DOC-07
- **Related:** AUD-026-BLD003, AUD-008, AUD-020
- **Affected files and builds:** src/cli/private-screen.ts:93-110 (SIGINT handler leaves the alternate screen and calls process.exit(130), skipping the try/finally); src/cli/terminal-input.ts:279-288 (keepsRawMode is false off Windows, so Ctrl+C while the CLI works arrives as SIGINT); src/cli/command-line.ts:136-158 (every secret command runs inside withPrivateScreen); src/cli/protection.ts:75-115 (relaunch parent and menu parent ignore SIGINT, so the child that exports gets it); src/export/image-export.ts:90-100,114-117,166-168 (raster folder in /dev/shm or tmpdir() holding document.pdf, removed only in finally); src/export/individual-cards.ts:41-50 (cards written one by one into .card-collection- staging); src/export/private-file.ts:77-99 (withStagedFile), 169-197 (publishNewFolder). Contract text: CHANGELOG.md:43 (one cancellation line, exit 130), CHANGELOG.md:117 (clears its temporary copies of the phrase data after use), docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:262, SECURITY.md:47 (residual-risk warning), README.md:127.
- **Reproduction:** Source check: in the current checkout, HEAD 61e163b is two commits after f8d9a93. `git diff --stat f8d9a93 HEAD` touches only docs/REMEDIATION.md, docs/audits/README.md, .git-blame-ignore-revs, package.json and vitest.config.ts, so no src/ file changed. private-screen.ts:93-97 registers `process.once("SIGINT", interrupted)`, which writes LEAVE_ALTERNATE_SCREEN and calls process.exit(CANCELLED_EXIT_CODE). The only cleanup that runs is an 'exit' listener in terminal-input.ts:321 that restores raw mode. No listener removes temporary or staging folders. I reviewed the first verifier's AUD-037-SEC002/drive.py and its stub. They are sound: a real 0x03 byte on the PTY master, and a node process under protection-flags.json with no relaunch parent. relaunchProtected and runInOwnProcess ignore SIGINT in the parent, so the child behaves the same on the npm/menu path.

Independent probes: /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-SEC002-lens/drive.py with stub bin/pdftocairo. The stub answers -v from the real Poppler. It renders with the real /usr/bin/pdftocairo until a finished file exists in a staging folder; after that, or in wait-first mode, it execs `sleep 8`. Public phrase 'abandon … about'.

(1) `python3 -I drive.py <repo> images wait-first encode --mode direct --mnemonic-file phrase.txt --format colors --images-dir out-images/imgs` gave run-images.json: exit_code 130, printed_Cancelled false; the tail ends `Rendering the cards…`, then ^C, ESC[2J ESC[H ESC[?1049l. Left behind: /dev/shm/mnemocode-raster-Qk1PDr/document.pdf (0600, 1,413,975 bytes, %PDF-1.7; pdftotext shows the card codes 01 000064, 02 1EAB91, 03 3D0964 …) and an empty out-images/.mnemocode-images-E6uGwD (0700).

(2) New variant the first verifier did not run: `… cards real-first encode … --template business-it --cards-dir out-cards/cards --image-format png` gave run-cards.json: exit_code 130, printed_Cancelled false. Left behind: out-cards/.card-collection-lJERkG/01-000064.png (0600, 1,005,808 bytes) on disk beside the output, plus /dev/shm/mnemocode-raster-Lug0vf/document.pdf (0600, 230,344 bytes, card 02). Peak RSS was about 278 MB in each run; there were no stray sleep processes. I removed my own /dev/shm leftovers after recording their hashes.
- **Expected behaviour:** On Linux and macOS, Ctrl+C during an export on the private screen should end the run the way Ctrl+C at a question or during a search does. Every owner's finally cleanup should run, removing the mnemocode-raster folder and every hidden .mnemocode-images-/.card-collection-/.sskr-cards-/.mnemocode-export- staging folder. The command should then print the single 'Cancelled.' line and exit 130 (CHANGELOG 0.1.0 line 43, docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:262), keeping the promise that temporary copies of the phrase data are cleared after use (CHANGELOG line 117). Any copy that cannot be removed should be named to the person, as LeftStagingCopyError does for a failed cleanup.
- **Observed behaviour:** On Linux, a Ctrl+C typed while the CLI renders an image export arrives as SIGINT. The handler exits at once with 130 and prints nothing after leaving the alternate screen. No finally block of the export runs.

Single-document exports (--images-dir): the complete card PDF stays in /dev/shm/mnemocode-raster-*/document.pdf until reboot. On macOS, or wherever /dev/shm cannot be written, it stays in the system temporary folder, which is usually on disk (from source; not run). The .mnemocode-images- staging folder stays too, but it is empty during rendering.

Multi-document exports (--cards-dir with PNG/JPEG, run here): the cards already rendered stay as owner-only PNG files in the hidden .card-collection- folder beside the chosen output on disk, and the PDF of the card being rendered stays in /dev/shm. A Ctrl+C late in a run leaves almost every card. SSKR card images follow the same pattern (from source).

Windows is not affected, because raw mode is kept there and the key is read at the next question, after cleanup.
- **Impact:** A routine cancellation, using the key the program itself names for cancelling, leaves secret backup material outside the destination the person chose. Depending on when Ctrl+C is pressed, this is a complete card PDF in /dev/shm or the temporary folder, or finished card images beside the output on disk. The folders are hidden, have random names, and the person is not told about them. The run also ends without the documented cancellation line, unlike every other Ctrl+C path.

Mitigations: the files are owner-only, /dev/shm is cleared on reboot, and SECURITY.md warns in general that such folders can remain. No disclosure to another account or direct compromise is shown, so the severity stays at medium, not high.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R1; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json). A second, independent verifier checked severity and release blocking: agreed.
- **Recommended fix:** Do not end the process from the SIGINT handler while an export owns temporary files. Turn SIGINT on the private screen into an AbortSignal that the export passes to execFile (its signal option) and checks between pages and cards. The renderer then stops, the existing finally blocks remove the raster and staging folders, withPrivateScreen returns CANCELLED, and the entry point prints 'Cancelled.' once with exit code 130.

Keep a bounded forced exit, for example on a second Ctrl+C. Before exiting, it should synchronously remove the registered raster and staging folders (rmSync in a registry flushed by an 'exit' listener) and name any folder it cannot remove. If any residual case remains, adjust CHANGELOG lines 43 and 117 and SECURITY.md to match.
- **Required verification:** Add real-PTY regressions that type 0x03 while a stub renderer waits. Cover encode --images-dir (single document), --cards-dir with PNG after the first card is staged, and SSKR card images. Each should expect exit 130, exactly one 'Cancelled.' line on the main screen, the alternate screen left, no new /dev/shm/mnemocode-raster-* (or tmpdir) folder, and no .mnemocode-images-/.card-collection-/.sskr-cards-/.mnemocode-export- folder beside the output. Rerun the renderer-failure control (cleanup before the retry question), the existing question and date-search Ctrl+C PTY checks, the menu child-process path, and the Windows raw-mode cancellation tests unchanged.

##### AUD-037-SEC003 — Low — ShareSplit silently accepts a randomness callback that leaves the split seed unfilled, so any one share reveals the phrase

- **Category:** SEC
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: This does not affect the 0.1.0 main path. Both production constructions (src/sskr/shares.ts:43,50, which the CLI reaches through splitSskrMnemonic and splitSskrBackup) pass nodeFillRandom, which calls randomFillSync. My probe shows those shares are non-repeating and resist the attack. No shipped host or workspace host builds ShareSplit with any other callback; a workspace grep outside mnemocode src, test and dist found none. The documented split self-check (restore of every share) runs on the main path and works. No legal obligation or workspace release precondition is involved. The failure needs a future third-party host that breaks the documented "fills a buffer with secure random bytes" contract.
- **Rules:** CORE-46, CORE-48, CHECK-SEC-004
- **Affected files and builds:** src/sskr/split.ts:3-5,63-70,88-94; src/sskr/index.ts:27,31 (public export of ShareSplit from mnemocode/sskr); src/sskr/share-platform.ts:52-63,75; sskr-wasm/rust/src/lib.rs:186-189 (checks the seed length only); README.md:278 and docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:155 (hosts supply their own randomness); test/share-platform-injection.test.ts:123 (a test that passes fill(0))
- **Reproduction:** Source tree: HEAD is now 61e163b. `git diff f8d9a93 HEAD -- src sskr-wasm` is empty, and dist/sskr/split.js (built 17:13Z, after f8d9a93 at 17:06Z) contains the same unchecked `this.#fillRandom(seed)` followed by `createShares(..., seed)`.

I wrote /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-SEC003-lens/probe.mjs and ran `node probe.mjs` against dist. It uses only public data: the BIP39 vector "vessel ladder ... valve picture" (entropy f30f8c1da665478f49b001d94c5fc452) and the all-"abandon" phrase. Output:
- "no-op split accepted, shares: 3 no-op == fresh-array: true"
- "nodeFillRandom repeats: false"
- The attacker holds only victim share #0, below the 2-of-3 threshold. He makes the reference split himself with the public library and an all-zero seed. Result: "recovered bytes 4..15: a665478f49b001d94c5fc452 match: true".
- The share's own 4 digest bytes satisfy share[j] = a*HMAC(R,S)[j] ^ b*S[j], where R is recovered from the reference. A reduced search over bytes 0..1 found "digest-check candidates over 2^16: 1 first: f30f8c1da665478f49b001d94c5fc452", equal to the truth. The full 2^32 search over 4 unknown bytes is a few minutes of HMAC on a CPU.
- The same attack on a production share gives "match: false".

Source checks:
- The library validates every other host output: createShares (count, split identity, restore) and the decoy randomInt range (RANDOM_REFUSAL in src/export/card-decoys.ts). The split seed has no check.
- No test vector uses an all-zero seed (the only testOnlySeed is fffefd...e0). Only test/share-platform-injection.test.ts:123 uses fill(0).
- No earlier finding in AUD-001..036 or docs/REMEDIATION.md covers a seed callback that leaves the seed unfilled.
- **Expected behaviour:** ShareSplit is a public API (mnemocode/sskr, README.md:278, ARCHITECTURE.md:155) whose host supplies the secret-path randomness. Under CHECK-SEC-004 ("injected callback type/length, repeated/short/oversized responses") and the guide's public-API rule ("wrong result shape ... Reject rather than guessing"), it should refuse a callback whose failure it can detect cheaply and soundly. A seed still all zero after the callback cannot come from a working CSPRNG (chance 2^-256). That is the case for a no-op callback and for a getRandomValues-style callback that returns a new array. ShareSplit should refuse this before createShares, as it already refuses bad createShares and decoy-random output, and wipe entropy and seed on refusal. A deterministic non-zero test source (fill(7), testOnlySeed) remains allowed, as CORE-46 permits.
- **Observed behaviour:** ShareSplit stores fillRandom unchecked and passes the 32-byte seed straight to createShares. The Rust engine checks only the length. The two callbacks I tested both leave the seed all zero and produce identical shares for identical phrases. Every check still passes (share count, split identity, restore of every share), so the backup looks sound.

The impact under that misuse is worse than the candidate states. An all-zero seed is public knowledge, so an attacker needs no victim-side reference split. One share below the threshold gives 12 of 16 entropy bytes directly (28 of 32 for 24 words). Its 4 digest bytes then pin the rest in about 2^32 HMAC evaluations, which recovers the full phrase.

The returns-new-array form type-checks, because `(bytes: Uint8Array) => void` accepts a function that returns a value. So TypeScript does not catch it. Passing `crypto.getRandomValues` unbound, or a non-function, fails closed.
- **Impact:** Under the trigger, the SSKR threshold guarantee fails silently and completely: any single share reveals the seed phrase. The trigger is narrow. It needs a host integration of the exported host-neutral API that breaks the documented callback contract with a no-op or a returns-new-array callback. Neither the CLI nor the Node wrappers do this, and no workspace host exists for 0.1.0. A fixed non-zero source, such as fill(7), leaks the same way and cannot be detected on a single call. The proposed check therefore covers the plausible API-shape mistakes, not every bad source. This is a misuse-resistance defect in a public secret-path API, with no demonstrated effect on supported behaviour.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R5 (R3 O9 similar); verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json). A second, independent verifier checked severity and release blocking: first verifier refuted info, second confirmed-with-changes low; the coordinator kept the second.
- **Recommended fix:** 1. In ShareSplit.split, after this.#fillRandom(seed), refuse with a cataloged error (for example "the host's randomness left the split seed unfilled") when every seed byte is zero. Refuse before createShares, inside the existing try, so the finally block wipes entropy and seed.
2. Document on the constructor (JSDoc @param) that fillRandom must fill the given buffer in place from a cryptographic source and that its return value is ignored.
3. Change test/share-platform-injection.test.ts:123 from fill(0) to a non-zero deterministic source such as fill(7).
Optionally, apply the same check in the Node bridge or in Rust create(), so that a host calling createShares directly with an all-zero seed is refused too.
- **Required verification:** - A unit test: ShareSplit refuses a no-op callback and a callback that returns a new array, with the stable error, and createShares is never called.
- Entropy and seed are zero after the refusal.
- fill(7) and the published testOnlySeed vectors still produce their expected shares.
- nodeFillRandom splits are unchanged.
- pnpm check and the existing SSKR vector tests still pass.

##### AUD-037-SEC004 — Low — Date searches, the marked-code search and the masked SSKR restore drop the fingerprint filter warning (missed variant of AUD-020-SEC001)

- **Category:** SEC
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: Only the result wording loses the caveat. The wallet check itself works. The --master-fingerprint help, docs/CLI.md and the README all say that a fingerprint is a 32-bit filter, not proof. No secret is exposed, no wrong phrase is accepted for encoding, and no mandatory integrity, isolation or recovery guarantee is broken.
- **Rules:** DOC-07, AUD-020-SEC001
- **Related:** AUD-020-SEC001
- **Affected files and builds:** src/core/date-search.ts:389-401 (DateMatch carries only `evidence`, with no warning), 415-433 (DateSearchResult has no warning), 660-683 (the run loop reads match.path and drops match.warning); src/cli/date-search.ts:295-311 (reportDateMatches), 344-359 (dateMatchNoteLines prints only 'Each displayed candidate matched the requested ... locally.'); src/cli/recover-date-command.ts:189-195, 298-305; src/cli/decode-command.ts:590-591; src/core/missing-codes-search.ts:88-95 (MissingCodesFound has no warning), 524-541, 599-641; src/cli/missing-codes-search.ts:115-119 (reportFound). Additional variant, from source only: src/sskr/share-unmasking.ts:54-61, 199-205 (UnmaskedPhrase.matchedAt only) and src/cli/sskr-restore-command.ts:708-714 ('matches the master fingerprint'). Compare src/cli/recover-word-command.ts:509-513 and src/cli/re-encode-command.ts, which do print the warning.
- **Reproduction:** All runs at HEAD f8d9a93, with dist/ built from HEAD.
(1) `node tmp/aud037/r2/probe_fp_warning.mjs`. The probe is sound. The wallet check answers {"matched":true,"path":"m","warning":"A master fingerprint is only 32 bits and is a filter, not proof of recovery.",...}. The DateSearch result keys are combinations,checksumValid,matchCount,shown,kept,checked,stopped. shown[0] keys are dates,allDates,mnemonic,evidence, so the warning is gone.
(2) `node dist/mnemocode.js recover-date --mode seedshift --input-file stored.txt --format 1 --dates-file dates.txt --master-fingerprint 73c5da0a`, run in tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-SEC004/. stored.txt is the public 'wool abuse actual ... congress' phrase and dates.txt is '??-09-2026'. Exit 0. stdout: '23-09-2026<TAB>abandon x11 about<TAB>matched at m'. stderr: 'Recovery search contains 30 date combinations.', 'Checked 30/30 date combinations; found 1 match.', 'Each displayed candidate matched the requested master fingerprint locally.' There is no filter warning.
(3) For comparison, `recover-word --mnemonic-file word.txt --master-fingerprint 73c5da0a` (abandon x11 ?) prints on stderr 'Matched 1 of 128 candidates against the requested master fingerprint locally.' followed by 'A master fingerprint is only 32 bits and is a filter, not proof of recovery.'
(4) PTY run of `decode --ask-secrets --mode seedshift --format 1 --master-fingerprint 73c5da0a` with the full codes and dates '??-09-2026' (tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-SEC004/decode-date-pty.txt). The output is the same: 'matched at m' and 'Each displayed candidate matched the requested master fingerprint locally.', with no warning.
(5) PTY run of the same decode with code 12 typed as '?' and date 23-09-2026 (tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-SEC004/marked-pty.txt). Output: '✓ Code 12 found: wool ... congress', 'Correct the written copy; then check the wallet that comes back.', then the phrase and its fingerprint. There is no FINGERPRINT_WARNING, and also no 'matched ... locally' line.
(6) History: `git log -S "Each displayed candidate matched"` gives be33cc4, which predates the AUD-020-SEC001 fix (8aa9b25, 2026-10-14). That fix touched only re-encode. docs/REMEDIATION.md does not track AUD-020-SEC001, and AUD-020 records it as fixed.
- **Expected behaviour:** When a master fingerprint is the only wallet check, each search result tells the person what the check's own answer says: the fingerprint is a 32-bit filter, not proof. Re-encode and recover-word already do this after AUD-020-SEC001, as WalletMatch.warning ('What the person should know of a match') and MasterFingerprintCheck ('A match remains a filter carrying FINGERPRINT_WARNING, not proof') intend.
- **Observed behaviour:** DateSearch copies only match.path into DateMatch.evidence and drops match.warning. recover-date and decode's date search therefore print 'Each displayed candidate matched the requested master fingerprint locally.' with no caveat. The marked-code search (MissingCodesFound) also drops the warning. It does not print 'matched ... locally' as the claim says: it prints 'Code N found' and the hint 'Correct the written copy; then check the wallet that comes back.' Reading the source, the SSKR restore of masked shares (UnmaskedPhrase.matchedAt) has the same omission.
- **Impact:** The person loses some assurance about the recovery, and the omission matters most where the fingerprint is the only filter. In Seedshift mode every date gives a valid phrase, and a date search may try up to 2^31 combinations. At that size a chance 32-bit match is no longer negligible (about 0.5 false matches expected at the hard maximum), yet one match is reported without the caveat. The help, CLI.md and the README do state it, and a wrong phrase would show up as the wrong wallet. No secret disclosure, theft or acceptance for re-encoding was demonstrated.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R2+R1; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Carry the check's warning through the search results. Options: a `warning` field on DateSearchResult (the first warning, as MatchTally does for the word search) or on DateMatch, propagated through MissingCodesFound and UnmaskedPhrase. Then have dateMatchNoteLines, reportFound and the SSKR restore wallet fact print it after the match line, as recover-word does. An alternative is to derive the line from the evidence kind in the CLI, but forwarding the library's warning also serves other hosts.
- **Required verification:** Run recover-date and decode's date search with --master-fingerprint 73c5da0a on the public stored phrase and check that FINGERPRINT_WARNING is printed after the match note. Check that the address and public-key checks print no fingerprint warning. Check that the marked-code search by wallet and the SSKR masked-share restore by fingerprint show the warning. Add a library test asserting that a DateSearch run with a fingerprint check exposes the warning. Keep the AUD-020 re-encode and recover-word tests passing.

##### AUD-037-SEC005 — Low — Every digest-checked SSKR recovery, including a refused failed-digest one, leaves the reconstructed entropy in the unwiped HMAC state on the WASM shadow stack

- **Category:** SEC
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: This is a best-effort memory-hygiene gap inside the process's own WASM linear memory, which core-dump and ptrace hardening already protect. No secret leaves the process and recovery stays correct. The recovered phrase becomes an unerasable JS string on success anyway. CORE-24, RUST-13 and DOC-07 are not stated as release preconditions, and the sibling AUD-035-SEC001 was also non-blocking.
- **Rules:** RUST-13, CORE-24, DOC-07
- **Related:** AUD-035-SEC001, AUD-012-SEC001
- **Affected files and builds:** sskr-wasm/rust/vendor/bc-shamir-0.13.0/src/shamir.rs:12-14 (create_digest) and :204 (called with RecoverySecret bytes before the digest result is known); registry bc-crypto-0.14.0/src/hash.rs:61-69 (hmac_sha256: Hmac<Sha256> state with no wipe; hmac 0.12.1, digest 0.10.7, block-buffer 0.10.4 and sha2 0.10.9 have no zeroize or Drop); sskr-wasm/rust/src/lib.rs:1-4 (doc claim), :210-224 (combine); CHANGELOG.md:30 ("clears creation and recovery intermediates") and :17 ("Retire reconstructed native entropy when its SSKR digest fails"); CHANGELOG.md:29 is a TS-owner caveat, not the claim.
- **Reproduction:** Source at f8d9a93 (HEAD e28d81e changes only docs/REMEDIATION.md and docs/audits/README.md). The generated WASM and glue match sskr-wasm/integrity.json (wasm ca60c5db...). recover_secret calls create_digest(&digest[4..], secret.bytes()), which is hmac_sha256(key=random digest tail, message=entropy). The 16- or 32-byte message stays in the HMAC inner BlockBuffer, and none of the hash crates wipes it.
Probe 1: `node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-SEC005/probe.mjs` (output in probe.out). It uses a fresh pinned instance per case, starting with SP=1048576.
- 2-of-3 recovery of counting secrets, L=16 and L=32: one copy at 1047544 (belowSp true). Before the recovery there were none, after the probe zeroed the dead stack.
- An unrelated later 2-of-2 split removes that copy. The next digest-checked recovery puts its own secret at the same 1047544.
- 1-of-1 control (no digest, no HMAC): no stack copy after recovery. The only copies are heap ones at 1252301/1253933.
- AUD-035 failed-digest construction: refused with sskr.engine.combine, and the exact published entropy is at 1047544. The healthy control recovers the entropy.
Probe 2: `node .../verify/AUD-037-SEC005/attribute.mjs` (attribute.out). Using the dist gf256 helpers I rebuilt the digest share and HMAC key of the deterministic 2-of-3 vector, then computed the SHA-256 ipad/opad midstates by hand. The bytes around the residue are, in order:
- inner midstate at 1047464 and block_len=1;
- outer midstate at 1047504 and block_len=1;
- the 64-byte buffer at 1047544 (entropy followed by zeros);
- pos=0x10 at 1047608.
This is exactly an Hmac<Sha256> state that was updated but never finalized or wiped.
Reviewer probes rerun:
- r3/p11b: entropy at 1047544 after splitSskrMnemonic (which verifies by recovering) and again after restoreShareSet.
- r3/p14: entropy at 1047544 after the failed-digest refusal.
- r1/a-c5-digest: 1-of-1 leaves 0 copies after recovery, 2-of-2 leaves 1.
- **Expected behaviour:** The engine doc says its copies of a secret are wiped once it is done, and CHANGELOG says the native engine clears recovery intermediates and retires reconstructed entropy when the digest fails. So no plain copy of reconstructed entropy should stay in the engine's WASM linear memory after recover_sskr_shares returns, whether the digest passes or fails.
- **Observed behaviour:** Every recovery with member or group threshold of 2 or more leaves one plain copy of the reconstructed secret in the dead WASM shadow stack at a fixed address (1047544, 1032 bytes below the stack top). That copy is the unwiped HMAC-SHA256 message buffer from bc-shamir's digest check. It is also left when the digest fails and the call is refused. The copy stays until a later engine call reuses that stack depth. The product split path leaves it too, because it verifies by recovering. Threshold-1 recoveries, which skip the HMAC, leave no stack copy.
- **Impact:** The process holds one extra copy of the wallet entropy in memory, inside the long-lived engine instance, after every restore and after a refused failed-digest restore, until the next engine call overwrites it. Nothing is exported and no other process can read it; on success the phrase also exists as an unerasable JS string. The defect is a bounded gap in local secret hygiene, and the engine's own source comment and the CHANGELOG overstate the cleanup (RUST-13, DOC-07).
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R3+R1; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Remove the dependency's stack copy, with owner approval and a canonical rebuild. Option 1: in the vendored bc-shamir, call an #[inline(never)] stack-scrub right after each create_digest, writing zeroes with zeroize over a fixed-size stack array (a few KiB) that covers the hmac_sha256 frame. Option 2: run the same scrub in the bridge after sskr_combine and sskr_generate_using return. Option 3: compute the HMAC with a state type that zeroizes. Also apply the scrub to split_secret's create_digest for symmetry. If no code fix is accepted, narrow sskr-wasm/rust/src/lib.rs:1-4 and CHANGELOG.md:17/30 to the buffers the engine owns, and state that dependency stack copies in WASM memory are not guaranteed erased.
- **Required verification:** After the canonical rebuild and pin update, add a Node regression on a fresh integrity-pinned engine instance. It should recover a public 2-of-3 deterministic vector and the AUD-035 failed-digest construction, then assert the exact entropy bytes occur 0 times in memory.buffer after return. Keep the healthy-recovery and 1-of-1 controls and the splitSskrMnemonic verification path. Native tests and Clippy must pass; wire outputs and published vectors must stay unchanged.

##### AUD-037-SEC006 — Low — A successful SSKR create leaves a quorum of share payloads from its CBOR/UR encoding copies in freed WASM heap

- **Category:** SEC
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: This is a best-effort cleanup gap. The copies sit in freed memory of the same instance and are overwritten by the next engine call. The caller already holds the same shares as JavaScript strings that cannot be wiped. No mandatory integrity, isolation or recovery guarantee fails, and share output stays correct. The project's precedents for equivalent WASM residues (AUD-028-SEC001, AUD-035-SEC001) are low and not release blocking.
- **Rules:** RUST-13, CORE-24, DOC-07
- **Related:** AUD-012-SEC001, AUD-028-SEC001, AUD-035-SEC001, AUD-026-SEC004, G1-001
- **Affected files and builds:** sskr-wasm/rust/src/lib.rs:136-144 (encode_share), sskr-wasm/rust/src/lib.rs:199-202 (records built from encode_share), sskr-wasm/rust/src/lib.rs:1-4 (module doc says share copies are wiped); dependency copies: dcbor 0.25.2 src/conveniences.rs:61-62 (CBOR::to_byte_string copies into a plain Vec), bc-ur 0.19.2 src/ur.rs:41-43 and src/ur_encodable.rs:8-21 (untagged_cbor, to_cbor_data); reached through the exported src/sskr/share-platform-node.ts:21-26 (nodeSharePlatform.createShares)
- **Reproduction:** Read-only, at audit HEAD f8d9a93. The pinned glue and WASM hashes match integrity.json, and lib.rs and the generated files are unchanged at the later docs-only commit e28d81e. (1) Reran `node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/r3/p07-wasm-residue.mjs`: after a raw CompactUr 2-of-3 create, each of the 3 share payloads occurs once in linear memory, and the secret and seed occur 0 times. `p07b-where.mjs`: payloads at 1252728, 1253304 and 1253880; after another create in the same instance, none remain. `p07d-bytewords.mjs` (Bytewords 3-of-5, 32-byte secret): shares 0 and 1 are absent, and shares 2, 3 and 4 each occur twice. `p12-seed-residue.mjs`: the seed is absent. (2) Own probe `node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-SEC006/residue.mjs` uses a fresh instance per case and prints the 4 bytes before each hit. CompactUr 2-of-3: all 3 members, each preceded by allocator header 22000000 (an exact-size Vec). Bytewords 2-of-3: members 1 and 2, each twice, once after header 22000000 and once after d99d7555, which is tag 40309 plus the byte-string head, i.e. the tagged CBOR serialization. Bytewords 3-of-5: members 2, 3 and 4 twice. CompactUr 3-of-5: all 5 once. Every case leaves at least a member-threshold quorum. The full share text and UR body are not found. A following create overwrites every hit, which shows the hits are freed, reused allocations. The hits are spaced about 576 to 712 bytes apart, with raw and CBOR copies interleaved per share. This matches the encoding step's temporary copies and not the zeroized UrShare buffers, which are allocated together at generation. (3) Own probe `node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-SEC006/platform.mjs` goes through the public package entry ./sskr (nodeSharePlatform.createShares, CheckedSskrBridge, integrity-checked engine). Output: share 0, 1 and 2 payloads at 1252728, 1253304 and 1253880, secret absent. (4) `node r3/p11-cli-flow-residue.mjs` and `p11b`: after the library's own splitSskrMnemonic, no share payload remains, because the verifying restores overwrite them.
- **Expected behaviour:** CORE-24 and RUST-13 say a share lives in an owner that wipes it when its work ends. The engine doc says it wipes the share copies it keeps once done. So the temporary share serializations made while formatting create output should be held in zeroizing owners and wiped before release, as the UrShare buffers and the output string already are.
- **Observed behaviour:** encode_share passes each share through dcbor and bc-ur. untagged_cbor and tagged_cbor copy the payload into a dcbor ByteString, a plain Vec. to_cbor_data makes a second plain Vec with the CBOR head, and for Bytewords also the tag. Both are dropped without wiping. After a successful create through the raw engine, or through the exported nodeSharePlatform.createShares, a full quorum of raw share payloads stays in freed WASM heap until a later engine call reuses the memory. CompactUr leaves every member once. Bytewords leaves the later members twice, while the earliest members' copies are already reused. The ur/bytewords encoders themselves only iterate and make no raw copy.
- **Impact:** A host or library consumer that creates shares and makes no further engine call leaves enough share material to restore the secret in the engine's exported memory for the rest of the instance's life. Same-realm code and process memory captures can read it. Exposure gained is small: the caller already holds the same shares as JavaScript strings that cannot be wiped. MnemoCode's own CLI split flow overwrites the copies with its verifying restores. No secret compromise, exfiltration or wrong output is shown.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R3; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** In encode_share, stop sending share bytes through dcbor values that cannot be wiped. Write the deterministic CBOR bytes (optional tag d9 9d 75, byte-string head, payload) into a bridge-owned Zeroizing Vec. Encode it with bc_ur::bytewords::encode in Minimal or Standard style, add the ur:sskr/ prefix, and zeroize the intermediate body String. Alternatively, apply a reviewed vendored patch that reclaims and zeroizes the ByteString and serialized buffers. Keep output byte-identical to the SSKR vectors, respect the G1-001 ownership boundary, check decode_share's similar dependency copies at the same time, and rebuild through the canonical process with refreshed integrity pins. If any copy cannot be removed, narrow the module doc comment to what is actually wiped (DOC-07).
- **Required verification:** Extend the AUD-012-SEC001 fresh-instance memory test, which today covers only the refused-encoding path, to the success path. Use create_sskr_shares, both formatted encodings, and 2-of-3 and 3-of-5 sets. After one create, assert that no public share payload or tagged serialization occurs in a fresh instance. Check that the test fails on the current engine, and keep the known-answer and SSKR vector byte equality. Run the native cargo tests and Clippy, then do the canonical rebuild and integrity-pin comparison.

##### AUD-037-SEC007 — Low — A save that fails before publication and whose staging cleanup also fails loses the original cause, and the screen does not mention the hidden private copy left behind

- **Category:** SEC
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: This needs two failures in a row: the save fails before publication, then removing the staging folder also fails. The leftover copy is owner-only (0600 inside a 0700 folder) and sits beside the destination the person had already chosen. SECURITY.md already says that a hidden .mnemocode-export-…/.card-collection-…/.sskr-cards-…/.mnemocode-images-… folder can remain, may hold a complete private copy and should be deleted. No mandatory integrity, isolation or recovery guarantee is broken, and no direct secret compromise is shown.
- **Rules:** CORE-24, CORE-22
- **Related:** AUD-014 adjudication "Cleanup can fail after file publication" (fixed as adjudicated; pre-publication variant missed), AUD-015-API003
- **Affected files and builds:** src/export/private-file.ts:80-100 (withStagedFile; catch at 90-94 rethrows the rm error over the publish error); src/export/private-file.ts:187-195 (publishNewFolder catch, same pattern); src/cli/save-retry.ts:20-30 (SAVE_FAILURES), 47-52 (saveFailure), 111-114 (private-screen notice); src/mnemocode.ts:35-37 (off-screen raw message)
- **Reproduction:** Code is identical at f8d9a93 and the current HEAD e28d81e, which changes docs only; dist matches the source. R1's probe (r1/f4-probe.mjs) calls only publishNewPrivateFile with link=EIO and rm=EROFS injected and never shows the screen text, so I wrote my own. Command: node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-SEC007/probe.mjs (log probe.log). It drives the real dist saveWithRetry on the private screen (PrivateSteps.open), replacing only fs.promises.link and, in cases B and C, rm.
Case A: link throws EIO after the staging folder is made 0500, and the real rm fails with EACCES. The screen shows "The record could not be saved as …/out/A/record.txt: there is no permission to write there." and .mnemocode-export-g1a5wB/document (mode 600, test phrase) is left. The staging path is not named and there is no copy wording.
Case B: rm returns EROFS. The screen says "…: the disk can only be read." The full copy is left and is not mentioned.
Case C: rm returns EBUSY, an unmapped code. The raw message names the staging path but does not say it holds a copy.
Command: node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-SEC007/probe-folder.mjs (log probe-folder.log). The writer of the folder publisher writes a card, makes its staging folder 0500 and throws EIO. The screen shows "The card files could not be saved as …/out/F/cards: there is no permission to write there." and .card-collection-u0WLiV/card-1.txt (test phrase) is left.
Command: node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-SEC007/probe-raw.mjs gives code EACCES, message "EACCES: permission denied, unlink '…/.mnemocode-export-nr0woL/document'", cause undefined. The original EIO is lost.
Audit history: grep of AUD-001..036 and REMEDIATION.md found only the AUD-014 adjudication "Cleanup can fail after file publication" ("do not hide a private staging-copy warning"; fixed for the post-publication case only) and AUD-015-API003 (QR sidecars; fixed). No test covers a publish failure followed by a cleanup failure (test/aud-014-staging.test.ts, test/private-file.test.ts).
- **Expected behaviour:** When a save fails and its private staging folder cannot be removed, the person should be told the original reason for the failure. They should also be told that the named hidden staging folder may hold a complete private copy and should be deleted. This follows the AUD-014 adjudication, the withStagedFile comment that a failed removal is reported because the folder may hold a copy, and CORE-22 (no error is swallowed).
- **Observed behaviour:** The removal error replaces the original failure, with no cause attached. On the private screen, known codes become generic words such as "there is no permission to write there" for an EIO link failure, or "the disk can only be read". Only the destination is named, and a hidden staging folder with the complete test phrase remains unmentioned. The folder publisher behaves the same way.
- **Impact:** After a double failure, for example a failing USB stick remounted read-only or a Windows file lock, the person is shown a misleading reason. If they skip, they are told the result was not saved, while a complete owner-only copy of the secret export stays in a hidden folder beside the destination. They may hand over, sync or discard that medium without knowing the copy is there. This is bounded by needing two failures, by owner-only permissions, and by the general warning in SECURITY.md.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R1; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** When cleanup fails after a failed publication in withStagedFile and publishNewFolder, throw a typed error that carries the staging path and keeps the original failure as its cause. One option is a variant of LeftStagingCopyError with saved=false. In save-retry, the failure line should give the original reason and also name the leftover folder, saying it may hold a private copy and should be deleted. Do the same in the top-level report off the private screen. Add fault-injection tests for the file and folder publishers.
- **Required verification:** Rerun verify/AUD-037-SEC007/probe.mjs and probe-folder.mjs, or equivalent Vitest cases, for link failure, the FAT rename fallback and the folder writer, each combined with a cleanup failure. The screen line should give the original reason and name the staging folder with copy and delete wording. The original error should be kept as the cause. The existing aud-014-staging tests (published file with a leftover copy, QR sidecars, EEXIST stays a failure) should still pass.

##### AUD-037-SEC008 — Low — recover-word's codes-or-words check maps every marked token before it refuses the word count

- **Category:** SEC
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: The 1 MiB input cap still bounds the work, and the command ends with the correct refusal. No secret, integrity or recovery guarantee is affected. The cost is a delay of tens of seconds and a few hundred MiB of heap, only for a deliberately malformed local file.
- **Rules:** CORE-48, CORE-50
- **Related:** AUD-026-SEC001
- **Affected files and builds:** src/cli/recover-word-command.ts:124-129 (the refusal's catch calls BackupReading.isCodes); src/core/backup-reading.ts:254-257 (isCodes calls MissingCodes.formats when the text has ?, * or |); src/core/missing-codes.ts:269-278 (formats calls placesOf and only then checks isBip39WordCount); src/core/missing-codes.ts:167-196 (placesOf maps every token before any count check); src/core/candidates.ts:139-151 (wordPlaceOf filters all 2048 words for each prefix* token). src/core/missing-codes.ts:243-244 (the constructor's count check) is not on this path.
- **Reproduction:** HEAD is e28d81e. Its diff from f8d9a93 touches docs only (git diff --quiet f8d9a93 HEAD -- src passes), and dist/ has the same order (dist/core/missing-codes.js:185-189). Probes are in /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-SEC008/ (gen.mjs writes N space-separated "a*" tokens).

1) CLI runs of `/usr/bin/time node dist/mnemocode.js recover-word --mnemonic-file star-N.txt`:
- 13 tokens: 1.03 s, maxrss 217,020 KB.
- 20,000 tokens: 2.38 s, 220,976 KB.
- 50,000 tokens: 4.49 s, 254,064 KB.
- All three exit 1 with "Enter 12, 15, 18, 21 or 24 places: words, ?, a prefix with *, or a|b."
- 12 tokens give the 2^24 combinations refusal instead.

2) Core timing (`node core-probe.mjs star-50000.txt`):
- WordCandidateSearch.parse refuses the count in 5 ms.
- MissingCodes.formats takes 3,496 ms and returns [] (RSS 72 to 202 MiB).
- BackupReading.isCodes takes 3,523 ms and returns false.
- With 100,000 "z*" tokens, formats takes 6,783 ms.

3) Heap per token (`node --expose-gc heap-probe.mjs 10000|40000`):
- Each "a*" place holds 136 words.
- The mapping costs about 70 µs and about 1,185 bytes of retained heap per token (40,000 tokens: 2,798 ms, 45.1 MiB).

The 1 MiB file (about 349,525 tokens) was not run because it exceeds the probe limits. Extrapolated, it takes about 24 s and about 400 MiB of retained heap on top of the roughly 216 MB CLI baseline. A decode control with the same files refuses in about 1.1 s, so decode is not affected.
- **Expected behaviour:** CORE-48 asks for explicit limits on size and count where input enters, and CORE-50 says work that grows with input stops at its limit. recover-word's own parser follows this: parseUnknownWords counts the tokens before any per-token work and refuses at once. The codes-or-words check that runs after that refusal should apply the same 12–24 count before it computes a place for each token.
- **Observed behaviour:** When the phrase has a wrong count and contains ?, * or |, the catch calls BackupReading.isCodes and then MissingCodes.formats. In the English form, placesOf runs wordPlaceOf on every token and only then checks the count. For each prefix* token, that means a 2048-word filter and a retained array. 50,000 "a*" tokens add about 3.5 s. A 1 MiB file adds an extrapolated 24 s and about 400 MiB before the same "Enter 12, 15, 18, 21 or 24 places" message. R1's figure of 317 MB at 100,000 tokens is total process RSS and includes the roughly 216 MB the CLI uses at startup.
- **Impact:** A malformed local file or paste of up to 1 MiB makes recover-word, or the menu's word search, hang for about 25–35 s and hold a few hundred MiB of heap, then print the correct refusal. The work is synchronous, so the event loop is blocked meanwhile. The 1 MiB cap bounds it. No secret is exposed, no wrong result is shown, and no supported 12–24-word input is slowed.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R1; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** In MissingCodes.formats/placesOf, split the text into its units for each form first (English tokens, code tokens, hex units, color pairs). Refuse a unit count that is no BIP39 word count, or at least one above 24, before mapping any unit to its place. BackupReading.isCodes and markedFormats then inherit the cheap refusal. Keep the per-place messages for inputs of a valid count.
- **Required verification:** - Add a regression test: MissingCodes.formats and BackupReading.isCodes on about 1 MiB of "a* " return []/false within a small time budget, and recover-word --mnemonic-file prints the unchanged "Enter 12, 15, 18, 21 or 24 places" refusal without the extra time.
- Confirm that the existing detection tests (test/codes-apart-and-joined.test.ts, test/aud-021-recovery.test.ts) and the backup-reading self-test still find the same forms for valid marked inputs of 12–24 places.

##### AUD-037-SEC009 — Low — A WIF evidence match returns the derived private key as an unerasable WIF string

- **Category:** SEC
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: No shipped path prints, saves or sends the returned text: the CLI (re-encode, recover-word) uses only matched, path and warning, and the self-test prints derived only for coin addresses. The string is the same key the caller already passed in as an unerasable string. No mandatory security, integrity or recovery guarantee on a main path is broken, and TS-07/CORE-24 are review rules, not stated release preconditions.
- **Rules:** TS-07, CORE-24
- **Related:** AUD-002-SEC002
- **Affected files and builds:** src/core/wallet-evidence.ts:23-25 (module contract: "keeps nothing: every seed and private key it derives is wiped before it returns"); src/core/wallet-evidence.ts:169-172 (EvidenceMatch, "what was derived", with no warning that a WIF match carries a private key); src/core/wallet-evidence.ts:633-656 (WifEvidence and its derivedFrom, which returns WIF(...).encode(derivedKey)); src/core/address-scan.ts:65-69 (FoundKey.derived, "A key that matched, as shown"); src/bitcoin-evidence.ts:78-114 (matchWalletEvidence and walletCheckOf, exported from the package root through src/index.ts:3); behaviour pinned by test/wallet-evidence.test.ts:188 and test/wallet-check-lifetimes.test.ts:24,36,57,58
- **Reproduction:** I checked the code with git show f8d9a93:src/core/wallet-evidence.ts. At f8d9a93, WifEvidence.derivedFrom compares the derived bytes with the evidence key. On a match it returns WIF(networkParameters(network)).encode(derivedKey), then zeroes derivedKey. LocatedEvidence.compare spreads that string into the result as derived. dist/core/wallet-evidence.js has the same code, and the only change from f8d9a93 to the working HEAD e28d81e is in docs.
The reviewer probe is sound. I reran it with `node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/r1/b-f5-probe.mjs`. It printed "matched: true | result.derived is the private key as WIF text: true | typeof derived: string".
I also wrote my own probe and ran it with `node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-SEC009/probe.mjs`, using the public abandon...about phrase and m/84'/0'/0'/0/0. Output:
- (1) the package-root matchWalletEvidence returns {matched:true, kind:"wif", derived:<WIF of m/84'/0'/0'/0/0>, path:"m/84'/0'/0'/0/0"} and derived is a string.
- (2) with padded input "  WIF\n", derived equals the trimmed canonical WIF but is not the input string, so it is a new string the module creates.
- (3) walletCheckOf: after check.wipe() the check refuses, but the earlier result's derived still holds the WIF.
- (4) core WalletEvidenceCheck.match and matchAsync both return the WIF.
- (5) a 20-address scan returns the WIF.
- (6) a non-match has only the keys matched and kind.
- (7) an address match returns the public address.
Consumers: git grep at f8d9a93 finds no product code that reads .derived except src/core/coins/coin-self-test.ts:83, which handles coin addresses only. src/cli/recover-word-command.ts:466-468 prints only matched and path, but it keeps the match objects of the shown rows for the whole run.
Earlier audits: I grepped the AUD-001 to AUD-036 Markdown and JSON and docs/REMEDIATION.md for wallet-evidence.ts, WIF, derived, "keeps nothing" and WIF.encode. No record covers this defect. AUD-002-SEC002 (info) notes only that input WIF strings cannot be erased. The code dates from be33cc43 (2026-10-07).
- **Expected behaviour:** The module says that "every seed and private key it derives is wiped before it returns", and TS-07/CORE-24 require secrets to live only in wipeable Uint8Array owners. A WIF evidence match should therefore return only non-secret facts (matched, kind, path), or a public value such as the compressed public key or the address. It should not return a new string encoding of the derived private key.
- **Observed behaviour:** On a WIF match, every entry point returns the derived private key re-encoded as a canonical WIF JavaScript string in EvidenceMatch.derived. This covers matchWalletEvidence and matchBitcoinEvidence, WalletEvidenceCheck.match and matchAsync, prepared owners, walletCheckOf, and scanned ranges. The byte copy is zeroed, but the string cannot be erased and outlives the owner's wipe(). Neither EvidenceMatch nor FoundKey documents that this field can be a private key, and the tests assert this behaviour as intended.
- **Impact:** Impact is bounded. The returned key is the same key the caller supplied as evidence (already an unerasable string), so no new secret is disclosed and the shipped CLI never shows or saves it. Two things remain. The module's own secret-lifetime guarantee is wrong, and an extra unerasable copy of a spending key is created and kept as long as the result is (for example, recover-word's retained rows). A library caller who treats derived as the public "what was derived" value, as it is for every other kind, could log, display or serialize a spending key, against README's "keep them out of command arguments and reports".
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R1; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** In WifEvidence.derivedFrom, return a public value on a match, such as hexOfBytes(publicKey) (as PublicKeyEvidence does) or the matched path only, instead of WIF(...).encode(derivedKey). Update the EvidenceMatch, FoundKey and CoinAddressMatch doc comments to state that derived is never secret. Update test/wallet-evidence.test.ts:188 and test/wallet-check-lifetimes.test.ts so they assert that derived is not the WIF. If the WIF echo is deliberately kept instead, correct the module contract at lines 23-25 and document the field as secret for the wif kind.
- **Required verification:** Rerun /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-SEC009/probe.mjs against a rebuilt dist. The checks must report that derived is not the WIF (and holds no private-key text) for matchWalletEvidence, WalletEvidenceCheck.match and matchAsync, walletCheckOf and a scanned range. Add a regression test asserting that the serialized EvidenceMatch of a WIF match (JSON.stringify and util.inspect) does not contain the WIF or the hex private key. Confirm that the self-test, re-encode confirmation and recover-word output are unchanged.

#### FUN

##### AUD-037-FUN001 — Medium — Decode's joint search of unreadable codes and forgotten date digits reports 'found' when two date sets of one codes candidate match, and can show a wrong phrase

- **Category:** FUN
- **Severity:** medium
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: The defect only shows when a second date set of the right codes candidate passes the wallet check by chance. In practice only the 32-bit master fingerprint allows that; an address or public key would need a 2^-160 coincidence. README.md:238, docs/CLI.md:221 and help-pages.ts:279 all say a fingerprint is "a filter, not proof", so the release promises no correct result from a fingerprint match. No secret is exposed, no funds move, and the person recovers by searching again with an address. No legal obligation applies. The workspace rules (AGENTS.md, mnemocode/AGENTS.md) and docs/RELEASING.md do not require open medium findings to be fixed before a release.
- **Rules:** CORE-22, DOC-05, DOC-07, CORE-19
- **Related:** AUD-010-FUN001, AUD-012-FUN001, AUD-014-API001, AUD-020-SEC001
- **Affected files and builds:** src/core/missing-codes-search.ts:569-578 (#searchOf: maxShown 1; the comment at 569 says the date search "stops at its first match"); src/core/missing-codes-search.ts:599-641 (WalletAndDatesWay.run: line 617 takes result.shown[0], line 619 counts the codes candidate once, result.matchCount is never read, line 636 returns kind "found" with match.allDates/match.dates); src/core/date-search.ts:629-695 (DateSearch.run checks every combination; matchCount += 1 at 670; maxShown at 672 limits only what is kept); src/core/missing-codes-search.ts:238-254 (foundOne: the same chance match on another codes candidate gives "several"); src/cli/missing-codes-search.ts:94-98 (reportFound prints the green "Code N found:" and "Dates found:" lines) and 307-318 (outcomeOf); src/cli/decode-command.ts:428-443 and 530-544 (Decode then decodes and shows the phrase from those dates); src/cli/ask.ts:505-513 ("Fingerprint" is the first choice in the interactive wallet question).
- **Reproduction:** I checked the commit first. The checkout is at 61e163b, two commits after f8d9a93. `git diff --stat f8d9a93 61e163b` touches only .git-blame-ignore-revs, docs/REMEDIATION.md, docs/audits/README.md, package.json and vitest.config.ts. The four source files involved are byte-identical, so the defect is present at both commits. dist/core/missing-codes-search.js contains the same code at lines 483 (comment), 490 (maxShown: 1) and 544 (shown[0]).

I re-read the first verifier's probe (tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-FUN001/probe.mjs) and found it sound. I then wrote my own variant, /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-FUN001-lens/probe.mjs. It marks a middle word (code 6) instead of the last one and counts every wallet call. It sends a modelled collision through the production fingerprintWalletCheck: the fingerprint function returns 73c5da0a for the public phrase "abandon ×11 about" and for the chosen decoys, and 00000000 for every other phrase. Command: `timeout 60 node --max-old-space-size=256 probe.mjs` (log probe-61e163b.log). Output:

seedshift codes: 128 combos: 31
control: {"kind":"found","foundDates":"15-01-2020","phraseIsP":true,"keys":"kind,backup,dates,foundDates","walletCalls":4030}
early decoy: {"kind":"found","foundDates":"02-01-2020","phraseIsP":false,...,"walletCalls":4030}
late decoy: {"kind":"found","foundDates":"15-01-2020","phraseIsP":true,...}
both decoys: {"kind":"found","foundDates":"02-01-2020","phraseIsP":false,...}

The joint search makes the same number of wallet calls whether or not a match is found, which disproves the "stops at its first match" comment. When the collision comes first, the joint search returns "found" with the wrong phrase and wrong dates. The "found" result has no warning field.

I also read the source paths. DateSearch.run counts every match. The fingerprint check's FINGERPRINT_WARNING is not shown anywhere on this Decode path: in src/cli, `.warning` is read only by recover-word and re-encode. Nothing in docs/REMEDIATION.md or AUD-001 to AUD-036 tracks this defect. test/missing-codes-search.test.ts:161-175 covers only the codes-level "several" case.
- **Expected behaviour:** If more than one date set of one codes candidate passes the wallet check, the joint search should not report a single success. It should be treated like the existing codes-level chance match ("Several sets fit, which a fingerprint leaves only by chance"), with a message that names the matching sets of codes and dates. That is consistent with the plain Decode date search, which shows every match and the count. The #searchOf comment should describe what the code does.
- **Observed behaviour:** WalletAndDatesWay.run keeps result.shown[0], ignores result.matchCount, counts the candidate once and returns kind "found". Which date set wins depends only on the order of the search. If the chance match comes first, Decode prints "✓ Code N found" and "✓ Dates found: <wrong date>" and shows a phrase that is not the wallet's, with no fingerprint caveat. The same chance match on a different codes candidate returns "several". The comment "which stops at its first match" is false: the per-candidate DateSearch checks every combination.
- **Impact:** The affected path is supported and is the main interactive route for this feature: "Fingerprint" is the first choice in Decode's wallet question. There, a chance 32-bit match on another date set of the right codes candidate is shown as a confirmed recovery. It is the wrong phrase and dates roughly half the time, and nothing says the result was ambiguous. The person may write down wrong dates or restore an empty wallet, then has to search again with an address. There is no secret disclosure and no loss of funds, and an attacker cannot cause it.

The likelihood is lower than the first verifier estimated. The odds per run are about (distinct date phrases of the right candidate − 1)/2^32. That is about 7e-9 for a typical "??-01-2020" (31 combinations). For a wholly unknown date in years 1–9999, EarlierShifts skips dates 2,048 years apart, leaving about 0.75M distinct phrases, so about 1 in 5,700 rather than 1 in 1,200. A joint search that large (128 × ~3.65M combinations) would also take very long. A chance match on another codes candidate, which is already handled as "several", is about (candidates − 1) times more likely than this case.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R1; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json). A second, independent verifier checked severity and release blocking: agreed.
- **Recommended fix:** In WalletAndDatesWay.run, read result.matchCount. If any codes candidate has more than one matching date set, do not return "found". Add the matches to the total and return "several", with a message that does not say only "sets of codes" (for example "N sets of codes and dates fit; an address tells which is yours"). Alternatively, return a dedicated ambiguous kind that the CLI handles by asking for an address. Correct the #searchOf comment: the search keeps only one match, it does not stop at the first. Optionally pass a match limit so the date search stops after a second match; the reported count is then a lower bound and must be worded that way. Separately, consider whether a fingerprint-based "found" on this path should carry FINGERPRINT_WARNING, as re-encode does since AUD-020-SEC001. That is a distinct issue and should not be merged into AUD-037-FUN001.
- **Required verification:** Add unit tests in test/missing-codes-search.test.ts with a WalletCheck that passes two date sets of the same codes candidate. Cover both orders, the extra match before and after the true date set, and assert the result is never "found". Keep three existing behaviours: the single-match control returns "found" with all known and guessed dates (AUD-012-FUN001), the codes-level "several" test at line 161 passes, and equivalent dates that give the same phrase stay deduplicated, so they do not trigger "several" (AUD-010-FUN001). Rerun tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-FUN001/probe.mjs and tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-FUN001-lens/probe.mjs against a rebuilt dist: the "early decoy", "late decoy" and "both decoys" cases, and the first verifier's cases A and B, must no longer print kind "found".

##### AUD-037-FUN002 — Low — Decode offers 'List every candidate' for marked legacy-valid codes from a code-set estimate, so the list always overflows for 12-, 15- and 18-word codes

- **Category:** FUN
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: No secret is exposed and no wrong or truncated result is returned: the core refuses the overflow (the safety bound added after multi-chain-wallet-tools:AUD-034-FUN005 holds), and the encoded-fingerprint and wallet ways remain offered and work. The defect is a dead-end option in the marked-code menu. It breaks no mandatory security, integrity or recovery guarantee and no release precondition.
- **Rules:** CORE-14, DOC-07
- **Related:** multi-chain-wallet-tools:AUD-034-FUN005 (recorded in AUD-030; overflow refusal added, this is its untouched offer side, not a regression)
- **Affected files and builds:** src/core/missing-codes-search.ts:289-342 (unreadable, many, ways, fits/whyNot decide the list way from MissingCodes.expected, which counts code sets); src/core/missing-codes-search.ts:654-695 (ListWay: the constructor bound and plan count code sets, and run throws list-overflow at line 680/685 once the decoded phrases exceed 16); src/core/masking.ts:466-468 (checksPerDecoding, the mode-owned count of phrases per decoding that the list bound ignores); src/core/masking.ts:135-145 (seedshift-legacy-valid: givesSeveral, checks = lastWordEntropyValues); src/cli/missing-codes-search.ts:76-89,182-188,270-275 (menu and --list-candidates take the core's ways/whyNot, and the thrown refusal leaves decode); src/cli/help-pages.ts:199-204 (--list-candidates help)
- **Reproduction:** Checked at HEAD e28d81e. It differs from f8d9a93 only in docs/REMEDIATION.md and docs/audits/README.md, and dist/ matches the source (it has the same `if (!this.many)` and overflow lines). (1) I reran the reviewer's probe `node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/r2/probe_missing_codes.mjs`. For the 12-word legacy-valid code set with one digit unread, it printed `expected 1`, ways ["encoded","wallet","list"], `fits list true`, plan "1 candidate", then `core.missing-codes-search.list-overflow`. (2) My own probe `node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-FUN002/probe_list_way.mjs` encodes the public zero-entropy phrases with seedshift-legacy plus validLastWord and the date 23-09-2026, then marks the last digit of code 4 or the whole code 4.
