One digit unread (1 actual code set) gave the following. 12 words: perDecoding 128, list offered, list-overflow. 15 words: 64, offered, overflow. 18 words: 32, offered, overflow. 21 words: 16, offered, list of 16 rows, true phrase included. 24 words: 8, offered, list of 8 rows. Whole code 4 unread: 21 words gave expected 16 and 20 actual code sets, offered, overflow; 24 words gave expected 8 and 11 actual, offered, overflow; 12, 15 and 18 words were not offered. Control: MnemoCode Seedshift with 12 words and one digit unread listed 1 row. (3) CLI under a PTY (`python3 -I /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-FUN002/pty_drive.py` on `node dist/mnemocode.js decode --ask-secrets --input-file /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-FUN002/lv-marked.txt`, a file identical to r2/lv-marked.txt). The screen shows "Code 4 cannot be read: 10 combinations, about 1 candidate." and the menu offers "3  List every candidate  compare them yourself". I chose 3 and typed 23-09-2026. The command printed "mnemocode: More than 16 candidates fit these codes and dates. Narrow the codes or use a wallet check instead of listing them." and exited with status 1. Adding `--list-candidates` gives the same: no warning, the same refusal, EXIT 1.
- **Expected behaviour:** The list way should be offered, and --list-candidates accepted, only when the rows it would list fit the 16-row limit. Rows are code sets times phrases per decoding (Masking.checksPerDecoding: 128, 64, 32, 16 or 8 for legacy-valid 12 to 24 words). The README says a legacy-valid decoding needs a wallet reference to tell its last words apart, and the menu promises "List every candidate · compare them yourself".
- **Observed behaviour:** MissingCodesSearch.many, ways, whyNot("list") and the ListWay bound use only MissingCodes.expected, which counts code sets. For a seedshift-legacy-valid code set with one digit unread, the screen says "about 1 candidate" and offers "List every candidate". After the dates are asked, ListWay.run computes up to 16 fingerprints and then throws list-overflow, which ends decode with exit status 1. For 12-, 15- and 18-word codes this happens every time, because one code set already decodes to 128, 64 or 32 phrases. For 21- and 24-word codes the list succeeds only when one code set (or, for 24 words, up to two) actually passes; a whole unread code is still offered and overflows. The claim's "32-128 phrases" and "can never succeed" are therefore true only for 12/15/18 words; the full range is 8-128.
- **Impact:** A person decoding damaged legacy-valid codes is offered a way that cannot work for 12-, 15- and 18-word phrases. The tool asks for the dates, refuses with advice to "narrow the codes" that cannot help, and ends the whole decode command instead of returning to the way menu, so the codes must be given again. No result is wrong or truncated, nothing secret is shown, and the encoded-fingerprint and wallet ways still recover the phrase. The impact is a bounded usability defect. The other host that imports this core (the multi-chain-wallet-tools page) inherits the same ways decision.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R2; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Count listed rows as MissingCodes.expected × Masking.of(mode).checksPerDecoding(wordCount) (1 where the mode gives one phrase), asking Masking rather than deciding locally (CORE-14). Use this count in many, ways, whyNot("list"), the ListWay constructor and plan, and in the candidate count of `unreadable`/plan text, so that legacy-valid 12/15/18-word codes never get the list offered and --list-candidates warns with a reason. Keep the run-time overflow refusal as the completeness guard. Optionally, have the CLI treat list-overflow from a way it offered as an outcome that returns to the way menu instead of ending decode.
- **Required verification:** Add core tests. Legacy-valid 12-word codes with one digit unread must have ways without "list", a whyNot("list") reason that names the phrase count, and a ListWay constructor that refuses. 24-word codes with one digit unread must still offer the list and return 8 rows with the true phrase. A whole unread code in 21/24 words must not be offered. Keep the existing overflow tests in test/missing-codes-list-boundary.test.ts. Add a PTY or CLI test showing that --list-candidates on a 12-word legacy-valid marked record warns and asks for another way instead of exiting 1. Rerun the verify/AUD-037-FUN002 probe and confirm "list" is absent for 12/15/18 words.

##### AUD-037-FUN003 — Low — The written-backup check names word places and word counts as "codes" for colour backups

- **Category:** FUN
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: The check's verdict is still correct: a changed colour or a wrong colour count is reported as "differs" and never as "restores", so the written-backup safety check works on its main path. Only the place and count in the diagnostic line are wrong, and the person can still type the codes again.
- **Rules:** CORE-14, CORE-10
- **Related:** AUD-019 CHECK-API-001 (EncodedForm.placeName introduced for the missing-codes search; not applied to the backup check)
- **Affected files and builds:** src/core/backup-check.ts:374-396 (EncodedBackupCheck.compareCodes: count message and place message); src/core/backup-check.ts:67-72 (BackupFinding.places contract); src/core/backup-check.ts:259-264 (WrittenCodes holds word indexes); src/core/encoded-form.ts:253-265, 277-280 (placeName, elementCount, unit, which compareCodes does not use); compare src/core/missing-codes-search.ts:276-281 (the existing colour-aware place naming); shown by src/cli/backup-check.ts:98-106 (afterMismatch prints found.message)
- **Reproduction:** Source is unchanged between f8d9a93 and the working HEAD e28d81e, which touches only docs. dist/core/backup-check.js carries the same messages. R2's probes are sound and reproduce at HEAD: `node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/r2/probe_backup_check.mjs` printed `colors: color 8 changed {"verdict":"differs","places":[12],"message":"The code at place 12 differs from the result."}` and `colors: color 3 changed ... places [5]`. `node .../r2/probe_backup_check_count.mjs` printed `{"colorsTyped":10,"verdict":"differs","message":"The backup has 12 codes; these are 15."}`. Own probe: `node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-FUN003/probe.mjs` (zero-entropy phrases, date 23-09-2026). Results: for 12 words there are 8 colours typed, form.unit=codes, form.placeName=Word and elementCount=8. Changing the last colour (8) gives places [12] and "The code at place 12 differs from the result."; changing the first colour (1) gives places [2] and "The code at place 2 ...". For 24 words, 16 colours: changing colour 16 gives "The code at place 24 ...". Typing 10 colours for an 8-colour result gives "The backup has 12 codes; these are 15.". In Color Unicode, changing colour 8 also gives "The code at place 12 ...". All verdicts were "differs", and correct backups gave "restores". Source: compareCodes compares WrittenCodes.indexes, which are word indexes (one per word), with the original word indexes. It builds "The code at place N" and "The backup has W codes" with the literal "code" and word counts. It never asks EncodedForm for placeName, elementCount or unit. test/backup-check.test.ts covers the place message only for the indexes form. Audit search: no matching item in docs/audits AUD-001...AUD-036 or docs/REMEDIATION.md. AUD-019 CHECK-API-001 only reviewed EncodedForm.placeName when it was added for the missing-codes search.
- **Expected behaviour:** For a colour backup (colors or colors-unicode), a finding should name places and counts in the units of the typed codes, or explicitly as words. This follows the BackupFinding.places contract ("by their places among those typed") and the form's own placeName ("Word" for colours), as the missing-codes search already does. Changing colour 8 of an 8-colour backup should name colour 8, or "Word 12". Typing 10 colours for an 8-colour backup should report 8 and 10 codes.
- **Observed behaviour:** The check reports word places and word counts as if they were code places and code counts. Changing colour 8 of an 8-colour (12-word) backup gives "The code at place 12 differs from the result.", although only 8 codes exist. Changing colour 1 gives "The code at place 2 ...", which points at another colour that is correct. Changing colour 16 of a 24-word backup gives "place 24". Typing 10 colours for an 8-colour result gives "The backup has 12 codes; these are 15.". The same happens in Color Unicode.
- **Impact:** A person who checks a colour backup on the command line (or in a host that shows the finding's message) is sent to a code place that does not exist, or to the wrong colour, and is given code counts that do not match the paper. This makes a written error harder to find and may undermine trust in the check. The verdict stays correct ("differs"), no secret is shown, and the person can still type the whole backup again, so the impact is limited to a misleading diagnosis.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R2; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Have compareCodes ask the result's EncodedForm how to name places and counts instead of hard-coding "code" and word counts (CORE-10, CORE-14). For colour forms, either compare the colours (encode both index lists with the form and name the differing colours by their places) or name word places with placesNamed(form.placeName, places), as MissingCodesSearch.placesText does. Build the count message from form.elementCount(words) and form.unit. Keep BackupFinding.places consistent with the units its message names.
- **Required verification:** Add backup-check tests in colors and colors-unicode for 12 and 24 words. A changed first colour and a changed last colour must name that colour (or "Word N", following placeName). A colour count from another word length must report colour counts (8 versus 10). Keep the existing indexes-form tests passing. Rerun /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-FUN003/probe.mjs and check that no reported place is greater than the number of colours typed.

##### AUD-037-FUN004 — Medium — Marked-colour search silently assumes tag offset 0 and returns "none" for an accepted one-step-offset colour record whose top-tag colour is unread

- **Category:** FUN
- **Severity:** medium
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: The defect does not affect any record that MnemoCode or BIP39Colors writes, because both number tags from 0 (src/core/colors.ts:98-100). It only affects records whose tags start exactly one step up (2 for 8 colours, 1 for other sizes) when the single highest-tag colour is the unread one. Complete records still decode. The result is a false "none", not a wrong phrase accepted and not exposed material. No mandatory security, integrity or recovery guarantee breaks on the 0.1.0 main path, and no legal obligation or workspace release precondition is involved. It does not block the release.
- **Rules:** CORE-22, CORE-48
- **Related:** AUD-011-ARC020 (source-fixed in AUD-012 with the acceptance gap 'actual compatible nonzero offsets remain gaps'), AUD-028 (coverage observation: nonzero RGB offset tags)
- **Affected files and builds:** src/core/colors.ts:182-209 (wordPositionPatterns: the docstring at 185-186; `const offset = offsets.includes(0) ? 0 : offsets.length === 1 ? offsets[0] : undefined;` at 208). src/core/colors.ts:162-180 (colorsToIndexes accepts any uniform offset and prefers none). src/core/missing-codes.ts:159-192 (colorPlaces, and the placesOf cases colors and colors-unicode). src/core/missing-codes-search.ts:71 and 238-254 (noneByFingerprint; foundOne returns kind "none"). docs/MANUAL_RECOVERY.md:55-58 (the claim's top-level MANUAL_RECOVERY.md does not exist). README.md:199-218 (`?` stands for an unreadable colour, and Decode accepts marks in stored codes).
- **Reproduction:** The working tree is at 61e163b. `git diff f8d9a93 HEAD -- src` is empty, and dist/core/colors.js:174 has the same offset choice as the source.

(1) I wrote /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-FUN004-lens/probe.mjs, with positive controls, and ran `timeout 120 node probe.mjs` (5.3 s, 91 MB). For each case, the full record was the public phrase encoded with colors/direct, with k*1,000,000 added to every colour. For each one I checked candidates() in both the colors and colors-unicode forms. Unread colours are written `?` in the colors form and `??` in joined Unicode, where each ? stands for one whole code.
- Every complete offset record decodes to the phrase (true).
- Tag-0 controls (12w, 24w): with the first, middle or last colour unread, includesTruth is true in both forms.
- Records shifted by one step: 12w tags 2..16, 15w 1..10, 18w 1..12, 21w 1..14 and 24w 1..16. With the highest-tag colour unread, includesTruth is false in both forms, with 2611, 1324, 675, 331 and 151 candidates. A middle colour unread gives true. The first colour unread is refused with "The colors that can be read do not tell where the others belong."
- Larger offsets are refused honestly: 15w tags 3..12 with the last colour unread. Offset 1 on 8 colours (tags 1..15) works.
- Two unread colours are refused by the 16,777,216-combination bound.

(2) I wrote search.mjs in the same folder and ran `timeout 150 node search.mjs` (9.1 s, 116 MB). It runs byEncodedFingerprint end to end on the 15-word phrase in colors-unicode, last colour unread. Tags 0..9 gave `{"kind":"found"}`. Tags 1..10 gave `{"kind":"none","message":"No codes fit this fingerprint: check it, and the codes."}`.

(3) Records: grep shows no open item in docs/REMEDIATION.md or AUD-001..036. AUD-011-ARC020 is a layout-parameter duplication finding, marked source-fixed in AUD-012, which recorded the gap "actual compatible nonzero offsets remain gaps". AUD-028 lists nonzero RGB offset tags only as a coverage opportunity.
- **Expected behaviour:** docs/MANUAL_RECOVERY.md:56-57 says older records may have a common tag offset, and README.md says `?` marks an unreadable colour in stored codes. A marked copy of such a record should be searched in its true placement. If the readable tags fit more than one placement, the search should either try every fitting placement or say that the placement is ambiguous. It should never return a definite "No codes fit". CORE-22 says the unknown must stay apart from none.
- **Observed behaviour:** wordPositionPatterns picks offset 0 whenever 0 fits the readable tags, even when the record's real offset also fits. Offset 0 and a nonzero offset both fit exactly when the real offset is one tag step and the unread colour is the highest-tag one. In that case the unread colour's '????' digits are put in slot 0, every readable payload moves one slot later, and the true phrase is never a candidate. The fingerprint search then returns kind "none" with "No codes fit this fingerprint: check it, and the codes." This happens in all five sizes and in both the colors and colors-unicode forms. When the lowest or a middle colour is unread instead, the same records are either searched correctly or refused honestly.

Corrections to the claim: the preference is in wordPositionPatterns, not colorsToIndexes, and the document is docs/MANUAL_RECOVERY.md.

Correction to the first verifier: a tag-0 record is ambiguous in the same way when its FIRST colour is unread, not its last. Readable tags 2..14, or 1..n-1, fit both 0 and one step, and 0 is correctly chosen. With the last colour unread, the lowest readable tag is 0, so only offset 0 fits.
- **Impact:** Take an accepted record whose tags start one step above 0 and whose top-tag colour is unreadable. Recovering it gives a definite "no codes fit; check the fingerprint and the codes", although the true codes were never searched. A recoverable backup looks unrecoverable, and the user is pointed at the wrong cause. It is a false negative only: no secret is exposed and no wrong phrase is accepted. Experts have a workaround: decode the readable colours by hand into word numbers and search in the indexes form, or subtract the offset from every colour.

Scope limits:
- MnemoCode (first release 0.1.0) and BIP39Colors both write from tag 0, and I found no writer that produces offset records.
- For the BIP39Colors-compatible 12- and 24-word sizes, a one-step-offset record needs tag 16. It can therefore hold only phrases whose last payload is at most 777215, about 78% of them.
- Exactly one colour must be unread.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R2; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json). A second, independent verifier checked severity and release blocking: agreed.
- **Recommended fix:** In wordPositionPatterns, or in a placement-aware variant that MissingCodes uses, return every fitting offset instead of silently choosing 0. MissingCodes should search the union of the fitting placements without duplicates, inside the existing combination bound. At most two offsets can fit here, so this at most doubles the work. Another option is to search offset 0 first and, if nothing passes, return a distinct "other placements fit and were not searched" result instead of "none".

Do not turn every ambiguity into a refusal. A tag-0 record with its first colour unread hits the same two-offset ambiguity, and it must keep finding its codes. Update the docstring at colors.ts:182-187 to describe whatever behaviour you choose.
- **Required verification:** Add regression tests for all five sizes, in both the colors and colors-unicode forms:
- Records shifted by one step: 12w tags 2..16, and tags 1..n for 15, 18, 21 and 24 words.
- Larger valid offsets, such as 15w tags 3..12.
- In each record, the lowest, a middle and the highest colour unread.

Check that the true indexes are among MissingCodes.candidates(), and that byEncodedFingerprint and the wallet-reference search find the record instead of returning "none".

Keep tag-0 behaviour unchanged: the same candidates and combination counts, including the first-colour-unread case. If the union approach is used, check that the expected count and combinations reflect the larger search.

These tests also close the "actual compatible nonzero offsets" acceptance gap that AUD-012 recorded for AUD-011-ARC020.

##### AUD-037-FUN005 — Low — The startup and full self-tests never run decode's search of unreadable codes, the share split, the share-restore orchestration, the candidate-list writer or the card-PDF reader, although the documents say every feature has its self-test

- **Category:** FUN
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: No wrong result is shown; the features are covered by the suite and by their library parts' self-tests. Fix before tagging because the documents promise a self-test for every feature.
- **Rules:** CORE-26, CORE-27, CORE-30, CORE-28, DOC-07, DOC-06
- **Affected files and builds:** src/cli/self-test.ts:53-62 (FEATURES), 68-76 (assertCoreSelfTest), 182-202 (runSelfTest); src/core/self-test.ts:405-414 (FEATURE_STARTUP), 417-475 (featureChecks); src/core/missing-codes-search.ts:261 (MissingCodesSearch, used at src/cli/missing-codes-search.ts:163); src/sskr/split.ts:88,117 (ShareSplit.split/splitBackup, used at src/sskr/shares.ts:43,50); src/sskr/share-restore.ts:385 (ShareRestore, used at src/cli/share-repair.ts:86); src/core/candidate-list-writer.ts:52; src/export/card-drawing.ts:691 (CardPdfReader, readCardPages); CHANGELOG.md:77; docs/releases/v0.1.0.md:47; docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:190; src/core/self-test.ts:6-11
- **Reproduction:** Uses dist built from f8d9a93 (src/ is unchanged at 61e163b). (1) node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/completeness/selftest-closure.mjs prints the exported library parts outside the static import closure of src/cli/self-test.ts (218 modules): core/missing-codes-search, core/candidate-list-writer, core/backup-options, sskr/split, sskr/share-restore, export/card-drawing, export/sskr-keeping-text and export/card-preview, plus the barrels. (2) cd /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/completeness && TMPDIR=$PWD/tmp node --max-old-space-size=256 selftest-coverage.mjs wraps every static and prototype method of these classes with a counter, then runs assertCoreSelfTest, assertSskrSelfTest and the full self-test as runSelfTest composes it (without the process-protection rows). Output: during startup only ShareSplit.validateThreshold and MissingCodes.{marked,read,candidates,wipe,formats} are called. During the full self-test (25 rows, 5.0 s) only MissingCodes.* is called. MissingCodesSearch, ShareRestore, CandidateListWriter and CardPdfReader print 'NEVER CALLED by startup or full self-test', and ShareSplit.split and splitBackup are never called. (3) git grep shows that no *-self-test.ts module and no known-answers.ts imports missing-codes-search, split, share-restore, candidate-list-writer or card-drawing.
- **Expected behaviour:** CORE-26 says a feature brings its checks in the same change. CORE-27 says known answers run at every start, before the feature's controls open. CORE-30 says the self-test command runs every startup check plus the slower ones, with each new feature's checks added there, and CORE-28 says each check has a case it must refuse. The shipped documents promise exactly this: CHANGELOG.md:77 and docs/releases/v0.1.0.md:47 say 'Every feature has its self-test beside it: quick known answers before every command ... and its row in mnemocode self-test', and docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:190 says each feature keeps its checks in a -self-test module beside it.
- **Observed behaviour:** Several main-path features have no startup known answer and no full self-test row. (a) Decode's and menu entry 6's search of unreadable codes, MissingCodesSearch: the encoded, wallet and list ways, the MAX_LISTED bound and the nested joint search of codes and dates, which is where AUD-037-FUN001 and AUD-037-FUN002 live. (b) The mask-then-split pipeline of ShareSplit.split and splitBackup, used by encode --sskr, sskr-split and re-encode to shares. (c) The ShareRestore orchestration behind sskr-combine, decode's share input and sskr-export. (d) The candidate-list writer behind --candidate list files. (e) The host card-PDF reader exported for pages. Only the pieces underneath are checked: MissingCodes enumeration and the encoded fingerprint under 'Backup reading', DateSearch under 'Dates', JointRepair under 'Share repair', engine createShares with a fixed seed under 'SSKR integrity', and MNCL bytes and age under 'Candidate lists'. ShareSplit also recombines every split at run time (#assertRestores). So the gap is in how these parts are composed, and the shipped claim is stronger than the code. This is not tracked in AUD-001..AUD-036: AUD-035 rules[32] and [35] recorded CORE-27 and CORE-30 as supported-at-recorded-scope. Related: AUD-037-FUN001 and AUD-037-FUN002 (defects inside the unchecked MissingCodesSearch) and AUD-037-BLD007 (SSKR refusal checks). Release blocking: false. The engines' known answers still run before any secret on every command, and splits are verified by recombination, so no mandatory integrity, recovery or safety guarantee fails. What is wrong is a coverage gap plus an overstated document claim.
- **Impact:** A fault in these features' own orchestration (for example the ambiguity of AUD-037-FUN001 or the list bound of AUD-037-FUN002) is not caught by the startup or full self-test on a host, only by the repository's suite.
- **Evidence:** Found by the completeness critic of the verification workflow with an import-closure probe and a call-counting probe at f8d9a93; the coordinator accepted it on that evidence.
- **Recommended fix:** Add startup known answers or full self-test rows, each with a case that must be refused (CORE-28), for MissingCodesSearch (encoded, wallet and list ways), ShareSplit.split/splitBackup, ShareRestore, CandidateListWriter and CardPdfReader, or correct the documents that promise them.
- **Required verification:** A coverage probe like the critic's shows every exported feature class called by the startup or full self-test; each new row fails against an injected fault.

#### API

##### AUD-037-API001 — Low — ShareRestore.restore and repair publish phrases or shares after their ShareInput owner was disposed during a later awaited host step

- **Category:** API
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: Neither shipped host can reach the path. The CLI uses the step API and keeps its input live. The browser host (wallet-tools MnemoCodeShareOperation) scopes a private ShareInput with `using` around each awaited restore or repair, so it cannot dispose it mid-flight. The input's own storage is wiped correctly, and nothing leaves to another party. This is a late-publication safety net that fails only after a host breaks its own obligation to keep the input live, so it breaks no mandatory security, integrity or recovery guarantee on a main path.
- **Rules:** CORE-24, TS-07
- **Related:** AUD-029-SEC001, AUD-015-API001
- **Affected files and builds:** src/sskr/share-restore.ts:459-474 (search), 482-514 (restore: no input liveness check after #sets or phrases), 592-618 (repair publishes after restore), 641-694 (#sets); src/sskr/share-input.ts:538-551 (restoreComplete, the only awaited step that re-checks liveness); docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:270 (contract)
- **Reproduction:** Source at HEAD: the repository is checked out at e28d81e, a docs-only commit after f8d9a93; `git diff f8d9a93 HEAD -- src` is empty. dist/ is the build of that source.

R1's probe is sound. `node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/r1/a-c4-probe.mjs` printed "complete shares: refused with core.secret-value.wiped" and "marked shares: outcome kind phrases (1 phrase(s) published)".

Independent probe: `node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-API001/probe.mjs`. It uses public sskrSetVector colors, with one element of share 1 replaced by ?, and gates the first host combineShares call or an async wallet check. The input is disposed at the gate, then the gate is released. Output:
- "0. marked read(): kind=marked; combineShares calls during assessment=0", so the gate is reached only in the search, after read() returned.
- "1. complete restore (disposed at gate): input wiped (core.secret-value.wiped); refused core.secret-value.wiped" (control, guarded).
- "2. marked restore (disposed at gate): input wiped (core.secret-value.wiped); outcome kind=phrases; phrase equals public vector: true"
- "3. marked repair (disposed at gate): input wiped (core.secret-value.wiped); outcome kind=shares; 2 share(s) published"
- "4. complete restore, async wallet check (disposed at gate): input wiped (core.secret-value.wiped); outcome kind=phrases; phrase equals public vector: true"
- 5a and 5b: undisposed marked and complete restores return the public vector phrase.

Static checks:
- test/share-owner-lifetimes.test.ts:92 covers only ShareInput.restoreComplete.
- No test covers disposal during the marked search, the wallet check or repair.
- The ARCHITECTURE.md:270 sentence and the restoreComplete guard were added together in d4776f7 (AUD-029-SEC001 producer remediation).
- Host callers: src/cli/share-repair.ts and sskr-restore-command.ts use the step API. wallet-tools packages/recovery-backup/src/mnemocode-share-operation.ts owns the input per operation with `using`.
- **Expected behaviour:** ARCHITECTURE.md:270 says disposing the ShareInput owner during an awaited restoration prevents a later host result from being published. If the input is disposed while ShareRestore.restore or repair awaits any later host callback (the marked-share combiner, a wallet check, a date search), the call should be refused with core.secret-value.wiped and return no phrases or shares, as restoreComplete already does.
- **Observed behaviour:** Liveness is checked only inside restoreComplete and, for marked shares, at assessmentTrouble at the end of read(). After read() returns, restore() awaits the search, the date work and unmasking and the wallet check with no further input check. With the input disposed at those awaits:
- a marked-share restore returns kind "phrases" with the restored seed phrase;
- a marked-share repair returns kind "shares" with two rewritten shares;
- a complete-share restore with an asynchronous wallet check returns kind "phrases".
The claim is right but narrower than the defect: repair() and the complete-share wallet-check and date-search awaits are affected too.
- **Impact:** The documented late-publication guarantee for ShareInput holds only for the complete-share combiner await. A host that ends the input owner mid-operation still receives a seed phrase or rewritten shares, which conflicts with the stated contract and the AUD-029-SEC001 owner model. Limits:
- The disposed input's storage is wiped.
- The result goes only to the caller that started the operation.
- No wrong phrase is produced.
- No shipped host path (CLI step API, browser per-operation owner) can trigger it.
- The practical effect is a bounded API-contract and stale-result risk for a future or third-party host that binds one long-lived ShareInput to page fields.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R1; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Bind ShareRestore.restore and repair to the input's lifetime:
- Expose a liveness assertion on ShareInput.
- Call it after each awaited host step that follows read(): after the marked search in #sets, after ShareUnmasking.dateWork and unmask in restore, and before repair builds its export.
- On failure, release any late RepairedSet or plan material and refuse with core.secret-value.wiped.
- Keep the CLI step API behaviour.
If the guarantee is meant to cover only restoreComplete, narrow ARCHITECTURE.md:270 to say so instead. The code fix is preferred.
- **Required verification:** Add regressions that dispose the input at a gated host callback and expect rejection with core.secret-value.wiped and no published phrase or shares. Cover:
- marked restore during the share search;
- marked repair;
- complete restore during an asynchronous wallet check;
- a Seedshift date search.
Keep these controls:
- the existing restoreComplete late-result test;
- undisposed restores that return the public vector phrase;
- stopped (signal) outcomes staying distinct;
- the CLI step-API restore and repair flows.
Rerun the nearest share-restore and owner-lifetime suites, then have an independent review of the change.

##### AUD-037-API002 — Low — Split self-check and written-share check report operational combiner failures as share outcomes (missed caller variants of AUD-033-API003)

- **Category:** API
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: Both paths still fail safe: the split refuses to hand out shares, and the written-share check never reports "restores" for an engine fault. No secret is exposed and no mandatory integrity, isolation or recovery guarantee is broken. The defect is a misclassified diagnosis, and it can only be reached when the host combiner fails after the SSKR self-test has passed.
- **Rules:** CORE-22, CORE-23
- **Related:** AUD-033-API003
- **Affected files and builds:** src/sskr/split.ts:154-160 (#assertRestores catch-all); src/core/backup-check.ts:594-600 (restore wraps every combine Error as restore-refused-3); src/core/backup-check.ts:670-676 (check() maps restore failure to verdict unreadable); src/cli/backup-check.ts:312-316 (askRestoredShares re-asks on any restore failure); contract at src/sskr/share-platform.ts:11-18 (ShareCombiner) and src/sskr/complete-shares.ts:39-42 (isCompleteShareRefusal)
- **Reproduction:** I read the source at f8d9a93 with git show. The current HEAD e28d81e changes only docs/REMEDIATION.md and docs/audits/README.md, and dist/sskr/split.js and dist/core/backup-check.js contain the same catch and wrap. I reviewed the cited probes: the actual file is r3/p06-split.mjs, and r1/a-c2-probe.mjs, r1/b-f2-probe.mjs and r5/probe18.mjs are sound. Instead of rerunning them, I wrote one combined probe with controls.

Command: cd /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-API002 && timeout 60 node probe.mjs (output saved in probe.out).

Observed output:
- Split with an operational Error from the combiner: code=sskr.split.sskr-generated-shares-did-not-reconstruct-source-entropy, cause=none.
- Split with a non-Error throw ("raw string fault"): the same code, cause=none.
- Split with a declared ShareCombineRefusalError: the same code. The three cases cannot be told apart.
- Lower-layer control: ShareSet.restore with the same faulty combiner rethrows the original fault itself (code=SYNTHETIC_ENGINE_FAULT, same=true).
- ShareBackupCheck.check(): healthy engine gives verdict=restores. An operational Error gives verdict=unreadable, part=shares, kind=unreadable, message="synthetic engine unavailable". A declared refusal gives verdict=unreadable with "These shares do not restore a secret...". A real share of another set gives verdict=unreadable with "Shares 1 and 2 are of different sets...".
- restore() with an operational Error: code=core.backup-check.restore-refused-3, with the original message and the fault kept as cause.

I did not run the CLI loop, because it needs a private terminal screen. From source, askRestoredShares catches every error from check.restore and loops back to askShares with the message as the problem. checkShares runs assertSskrSelfTest first, so only faults that occur after the self-test reach this loop.
- **Expected behaviour:** The ShareCombiner contract (share-platform.ts) says that only an invalid quorum is a refusal and that "Other host/initialization failures propagate, never become failed candidates". CORE-22 says "Unknown stays apart from none" and that no error is swallowed, and refusal.ts says non-Error throws pass through as faults. So the split self-check should turn only a declared complete-share refusal or a wrong result into its "did not reconstruct" refusal, and propagate operational faults with their identity or cause. The written-share check should throw for an operational fault rather than return a finding about the backup, and the CLI should ask for the shares again only after a share refusal.
- **Observed behaviour:** When the combiner fails operationally, the split reports that the generated shares did not reconstruct the source entropy. It drops the original error and cause, and does the same for non-Error throws. The written-share check returns verdict "unreadable" for the shares part, using the host's raw error text as the finding line. The CLI shows that text and asks for the shares again; a persistent fault repeats this until the user cancels. Declared refusals and real share defects produce the same code or verdict, so callers cannot tell the cases apart.
- **Impact:** Diagnosis is wrong, but the outcome fails safe. A transient engine or host fault after the self-test is reported as defective generated shares (split) or as unreadable written shares (backup check). A user or host page may then distrust or re-type a correct backup, and is asked to type secret shares again for no reason. The operational failure channel that AUD-033-API003 restored in the lower layer is lost at these two callers. No false "restores" result, wrong phrase, or secret exposure occurs.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R3+R1+R5+R6; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** In ShareSplit.#assertRestores, catch only expected refusals (isCompleteShareRefusal or ShareCombineRefusalError.is) and turn them into the split refusal; rethrow every other value unchanged. In ShareBackupCheck.restore, wrap only the coded refusals of ShareSet.combine and the share modules (catalog code match, not message text) as restore-refused-3. Let operational failures and non-Error throws propagate, keeping assertLive's disposal precedence. check() should turn only those coded refusals into "unreadable" findings, and the CLI askRestoredShares should ask again only after them.
- **Required verification:** Add regressions that fail before the fix and pass after it:
- ShareSplit with a combiner that throws an operational Error, or a non-Error, rejects with that fault (identity or cause kept), not the did-not-reconstruct code.
- ShareBackupCheck.check and restore with the same combiner reject instead of returning verdict "unreadable".
- The CLI share check does not ask for the shares again on an operational fault.

Keep these controls passing: a combiner returning wrong entropy, a declared ShareCombineRefusalError, and a wrong surplus share still give "SSKR generated shares did not reconstruct the source entropy."; other-set and damaged shares still give verdict "unreadable"; disposal still takes precedence; entropy is still wiped. Then run test/aud-033-combiner-errors.test.ts, test/sskr-share-modules.test.ts, test/aud-014-shares.test.ts and the backup-check test files, plus pnpm check.

##### AUD-037-API003 — Low — Searching a joint repair plan that refused or was stopped reports a completed search that found nothing

- **Category:** API
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: No mandatory security, integrity or recovery guarantee is broken on a shipped main path. Every in-repo caller checks the verdict before it searches: ShareRestore.read returns "stopped" or a trouble for every refusal, ShareSet.combine throws on assessment.reason, and the self-tests only search plans that can be searched. No consumer of JointRepair, planJointRepair or searchOnce exists elsewhere in the workspace. Only a direct host of the public ./sskr/joint-repair entry that skips the verdict check is affected. Earlier audits set the same precedent: the open-Low CORE-22 items AUD-015-API001 and AUD-035-DOC001 were not release blocking.
- **Rules:** CORE-22
- **Related:** AUD-035-DOC001, AUD-015-API001
- **Affected files and builds:** src/sskr/joint-repair.ts:135-145 (JointPlan.search JSDoc), :168-170 (search returns NOTHING_SEARCHED when no platform is held), :274-292 (refusal() builds a plan with no platform), :295 (NOTHING_SEARCHED = { sets: [], stopped: false }), :633 (planOf drops the platform for a too-open plan); src/sskr/joint-results.ts:71-78 (RepairSearch has no "not searched" outcome); src/sskr/shares.ts:66-71 (planShareRepair: "The plan's search finds every phrase they can restore"); package.json exports "./sskr/joint-repair" (public entry); test/sskr-joint-repair.test.ts:154-163 (pins the empty result for a refused plan)
- **Reproduction:** Source read at f8d9a93. HEAD is now e28d81e, and only docs/REMEDIATION.md and docs/audits/README.md differ, so the source is identical. dist/sskr/joint-repair.js contains the same NOTHING_SEARCHED path.

The cited probe r3/p04.mjs does not exist; the real file is r3/p04-repair.mjs. Ran `node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/r3/p04-repair.mjs`. Result: stopped plan verdict "stopped", search() {"sets":[],"stopped":false}; wide plan verdict "too-uncertain" (2^96 combinations), search() {"sets":[],"stopped":false}.

Ran `node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/r1/a-c3-probe.mjs`. Result: verdict "stopped", searchOnce {"sets":0,"stopped":false}.

Own probe: `node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-API003/probe.mjs` (public deterministic vectors from vectors/sskr-v1.json), exit 0:
- Control, the same two shares not stopped: verdict "determined", search {"sets":1,"stopped":false}.
- Assessment stopped: verdict "stopped", search {"sets":0,"stopped":false}.
- Too-uncertain, 7.9e28 combinations: {"sets":0,"stopped":false}.
- Too-uncertain searched with an already-aborted signal: {"sets":0,"stopped":false}.
- not-enough (one marked share): {"sets":0,"stopped":false}.
- no-fit (shares of two sets): {"sets":0,"stopped":false}.

No earlier audit or docs/REMEDIATION.md item records this defect; I grepped for NOTHING_SEARCHED, joint-repair, too-uncertain, stopped plan and plan.search. The nearest items differ:
- AUD-035-DOC001 (verified) covers planShareRepair's comment about rejection on cancellation.
- AUD-015-API001 covers a candidate published after a stop during a running search.
- **Expected behaviour:** JointPlan.search should not report a search it never ran. Its JSDoc says it tries every open combination and reports whether the host stopped it, and planShareRepair says the search finds every phrase the shares can restore. CORE-22 adds that a refused operation fails and that an unfinished or partial search is a result of its own, never "not found". So searching a plan whose verdict is stopped, too-uncertain, not-enough or no-fit should either be refused with a stable code or return an outcome distinct from a completed search that found nothing.
- **Observed behaviour:** Every refusal plan is built without a platform, by refusal() and by planOf for too-open plans. Its search returns NOTHING_SEARCHED, { sets: [], stopped: false }, which has exactly the shape of a completed search that found no phrase. This happens for all four refusal verdicts, not only stopped and too-uncertain. A search signal that is already aborted is also ignored on these plans and still gives stopped: false. Two concrete cases:
- A valid 2-of-3 pair whose assessment was stopped reports zero sets, not stopped, although the same shares restore one phrase.
- A plan with 2^96 open combinations reports zero sets after trying none.
The project's own ShareRestore.search reads this exact shape as "none fits" (NO_PHRASE_FITS). This behaviour is not documented in any public JSDoc; only an internal constant comment and a test (S013) rely on it.
- **Impact:** A host of the public ./sskr/joint-repair API that follows the documented plan-then-search contract and does not check assessment.verdict would tell a person that no phrase fits their shares. For a stopped or too-uncertain assessment that is a false conclusion about recoverable shares, which is the failure CORE-22 exists to prevent. No secret is exposed. The CLI and the self-tests check the verdict first, so the shipped flows are not affected and the practical impact is bounded to direct API consumers.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R3+R1; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Make JointPlan.search on a plan without a searchable verdict either:
- reject with a stable refusal code that carries the verdict (for example sskr.joint-repair.plan-not-searchable), or
- return a distinct outcome (for example add a searched/refused discriminant to RepairSearch) instead of { sets: [], stopped: false }.

Honour an already-aborted signal in that path. State the behaviour in the JointPlan.search and planShareRepair JSDoc. Update the S013 test to assert the refusal verdict rather than an empty search.
- **Required verification:** Add a focused test that plans the stopped, too-uncertain, not-enough and no-fit refusals through JointRepair. Assert that search() and searchOnce() reject with the stable code, or return the distinct not-searched outcome, and still wipe the plan. Keep a determined control plan of the same shares that returns one set. Rerun /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-API003/probe.mjs and confirm that no refusal row prints {"sets":0,"stopped":false}. Also check that ShareRestore and ShareSet.combine behave as before.

##### AUD-037-API004 — Low — A restore whose groups all have member threshold 1 (no SSKR digest) is reported and documented as checked

- **Category:** API
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: MnemoCode never writes threshold-1 shares: split refuses them. The case arises only for third-party SSKR sets whose given groups all have member threshold 1 under group threshold 1. SSKR keeps no digest for such shares, so no implementation could detect the alteration. CRC32 still catches accidental corruption. The defect is in how the result is reported and documented. No mandatory integrity or recovery guarantee on a main path is broken.
- **Rules:** DOC-06, DOC-07
- **Related:** AUD-012-ARC012, AUD-008-FUN001
- **Affected files and builds:** src/sskr/complete-shares.ts:90-96 (unchecked reference, checked:false) and :164,238 (only topology.incomplete is returned, reference.checked is dropped); src/sskr/share-set.ts:141-151 and :243-246; src/sskr/shares.ts:92-103; src/cli/sskr-restore-command.ts:677 and src/sskr/share-restore.ts:415 (only uncheckedNote is shown); docs/SSKR.md:130,132,134; contrast src/sskr/share-layout.ts:119-125 and src/sskr/split.ts:23
- **Reproduction:** Source read at HEAD e28d81e. Only docs/REMEDIATION.md and docs/audits/README.md differ from f8d9a93, so src and dist are as audited. dist/sskr/complete-shares.js:72 contains 'checked: false'.

Probe: cd /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-API004 && node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-API004/probe.mjs. It builds 1-of-2 shares by hand (id 0x1234, GT=GC=1, MT=1, secret = 16 zero bytes, "abandon x11 about") and runs dist combineSskrShares and ShareSet.restore. Output:
(A) genuine share alone: combineSskrShares returns '...abandon abandon about'; ShareSet checked=true, unchecked=[], note=undefined.
(B) secret bit flipped, CRC recomputed: combineSskrShares returns '...abandon abandon account'; checked=true, unchecked=[], note=undefined.
(D) genuine member 1 + altered member 2: refused with "These shares give different secrets, and none of them can be checked on its own".
(G) control, 2-of-3 MnemoCode split pair with one altered secret byte and CRC recomputed: refused with "These shares do not restore a secret".

Reviewer probe p13 rerun from r3/: both lines print 'grace hill gadget' with checked true. It prints only the last three words, so its output does not show that the phrase changed. My r3-check.mjs on the same fixture and alteration printed 'same phrase: false | differing word positions: [ 4 ]'.
- **Expected behaviour:** A restore that no SSKR digest checked should not be reported or documented as checked. This applies when group threshold is 1 and every given complete group has member threshold 1. The project itself says such a group "a restore cannot check it" (share-layout.ts) and "with a threshold of 1 no share would be checked" (split.ts). docs/SSKR.md:130 should not claim an unconditional hash check. Either ShareSet/combineSskrShares should say that no digest checked the result, or the documents should state this limit for threshold-1 groups.
- **Observed behaviour:** referenceOf returns checked:false for such a set. restoreCompleteShares then returns only topology.incomplete and drops that flag. ShareSet.checked is therefore true and uncheckedNote is undefined. combineSskrShares, documented as "The phrase only, when every supplied share was checked", returns the phrase, and the CLI shows no note. A single 1-of-n share whose secret bit was flipped (CRC recomputed) restores a different valid phrase reported the same way. docs/SSKR.md:130 states unconditionally that the share library checks the restored secret against a stored hash. The code's own refusal for two disagreeing 1-of-n shares admits that none can be checked. ShareSet.checked matches its narrow doc comment ("took part ... settled"). docs/SSKR.md:132-134 define "unchecked" as members of an incomplete group, so line 134 holds only under that narrow meaning.
- **Impact:** A library caller or CLI user restoring a third-party SSKR set of member threshold 1 sees the result as fully checked. Nothing tells them that only a CRC32 guards it. An adversarially altered or CRC-colliding share then yields a different valid phrase with no warning. The overstated hash guarantee is the DOC-07 concern. The impact is bounded: MnemoCode never creates such shares, and the scheme makes such a share undetectable by any implementation.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R3; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Decide which side states the intended behaviour (DOC-06). Option 1: carry reference.checked out of restoreCompleteShares into RepairedSet/ShareSet, for example a digest-unchecked flag or note. ShareSet.checked would then be false, or combineSskrShares would refuse, and sskr-combine and the private screen would warn that no digest checked a member-threshold-1 restore. Option 2, at minimum: qualify docs/SSKR.md:130 and :134 and the combineSskrShares/ShareSet.checked doc comments. They should say that a group of member threshold 1 under group threshold 1 keeps no hash, and that its restore is checked only by each share's CRC32 and against the other given shares.
- **Required verification:** Add a regression with a hand-built third-party 1-of-2 share (group threshold 1). It should assert the chosen behaviour: a digest-unchecked indication, a refusal or note from combineSskrShares, and a CLI notice. The same test should keep a 2-of-3 control checked and keep the existing two-disagreeing-1-of-n refusal. Also check that docs/SSKR.md, the shares.ts/share-set.ts doc comments and the split refusal text use "checked" consistently for threshold-1 groups.

##### AUD-037-API005 — Low — readLine relabels any TypeError in its editing loop as "not valid UTF-8", defeating refusedLine's pass-through contract

- **Category:** API
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: No mandatory security, integrity or recovery guarantee is broken. No supported production path raises a non-UTF-8 TypeError inside readLine, so the defect only hides internal faults behind a misleading refusal. It is a bounded diagnostic and error-contract defect, not a release precondition.
- **Rules:** CORE-22, CORE-23
- **Related:** AUD-033-API003
- **Affected files and builds:** src/cli/terminal-input.ts:548-614 (the try block covers the whole editing loop; the catch is at 612-614); src/cli/terminal-input.ts:203-212 (refusedLine, documented as "foreign failures pass through unchanged"); src/cli/terminal-keys.ts:49-53 (UnusableAnswerError doc comment and class); src/cli/typed-answer.ts:191-193 (text(), the only TypeError the catch is meant for); compare src/cli/utf8.ts:53-62 (strictUtf8Text narrows the same conversion to the decode call)
- **Reproduction:** Checked the source at f8d9a93 with git show. The current HEAD e28d81e changes only docs/REMEDIATION.md and docs/audits/README.md, so src/ is the same. Reran the R6 probe: `node_modules/.bin/tsx /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/r6/probe/readline-typeerror.mts`. Its first case printed `UnusableAnswerError "unusable-answer" "The answer is not valid UTF-8 text." cause: none`. Its second case (next returns 256) printed `returned "\u0000"`, so it does not show the coded InputBytes TypeError that its comment claims. Wrote and ran my own probe from the mnemocode checkout: `timeout 30 node_modules/.bin/tsx /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-API005/probe.mts 2>/dev/null` (exit 0). Output: A, raw 0xff then Enter (the intended path): UnusableAnswerError NOT_UTF8. B, next() throws TypeError('reader bug'): UnusableAnswerError code="unusable-answer" msg="The answer is not valid UTF-8 text." cause=none. C, next() throws Error('reader io') (control): the original Error passes through. D, onBack throws TypeError: NOT_UTF8. E, hints.hint throws TypeError: NOT_UTF8. F1, refusedLine on the converted error from B returns the warning "The answer is not valid UTF-8 text." F2, refusedLine on the original TypeError (control) rethrows it unchanged.
- **Expected behaviour:** A failure that does not come from decoding the answer should reach the caller unchanged. This holds for failures thrown by the byte reader, the onBack callback, the word hints or an internal fault. refusedLine documents this ("foreign failures pass through unchanged") and CORE-22 requires it ("none is swallowed"). Only the decode in TypedAnswer.text() should become UnusableAnswerError(NOT_UTF8), as strictUtf8Text does in utf8.ts.
- **Observed behaviour:** The try block in readLine covers the whole editing loop, and its catch turns every TypeError into a new UnusableAnswerError(NOT_UTF8) with no cause. TypeErrors from next(), onBack and hints.hint are all reported as "The answer is not valid UTF-8 text.", and the original error is lost. refusedLine then accepts the converted error as an answer refusal and returns the warning, so the question is asked again instead of failing. A plain Error from the same callbacks passes through, which shows the conversion depends only on the error type.
- **Impact:** The two production callers (ask.ts readAnswer and terminal-choice.ts askLineOnce) pass bytes through validCharacters. So at HEAD the decode in readLine cannot fail on a supported path, and the catch can only fire on unexpected TypeErrors. These include a programming fault in TypedAnswer, ShownLine or the word hints, and a misused Node API. Such a fault would show the person a false "not valid UTF-8" warning and repeat the question, instead of surfacing as a MnemoCode fault, and its diagnostic is lost. No secret disclosure, wrong result or reachable trigger on a supported path was demonstrated.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R6; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Narrow the conversion to the decode. Wrap only the final answer.text() call in readLine, and convert a TypeError there to UnusableAnswerError(NOT_UTF8). Alternatively, make TypedAnswer.text() throw UnusableAnswerError itself. Either way, every other failure in the loop propagates unchanged. Optionally pass the decoder's TypeError as the cause; its message names no byte of the answer. Keep the existing NOT_UTF8 behaviour for raw invalid bytes.
- **Required verification:** Add a regression test that fails before the fix. readLine must reject with the identical TypeError when next(), onBack or hints.hint throws one, and refusedLine must rethrow it. Raw 0xff followed by Enter must still reject with NOT_UTF8. Rerun test/terminal-input.test.ts, test/ask.test.ts, test/menu-terminal.test.ts and test/terminal-choice-record.test.ts, plus scripts/verify-terminal-input.py in a real PTY.
