- ShareCardSet.renderShare with {}: 'NO-CODE TypeError'.
- Valid controls: 'Ref. 01 ...' and 'Subtitle  Prepared for ...'.
- Type-correct texts that resolveCardPresentation refuses were all drawn on a sheet: a soft hyphen (refused as card-non-empty-text-on-one-line), 120 'i' characters (refused as card-too-long-maximum-characters), a studio name of three spaces, and an untrimmed subtitle.

`node .../verify/AUD-037-API006/probe2.mjs`: a slogan with U+2028 and a footer with U+2029 were drawn (od showed the raw bytes 342 200 250 and 342 200 251 in the footer line); an LF footer was refused with a coded font error.

`node .../verify/AUD-037-API006/probe3.mjs`: in the same renderCards call, profile.company with U+2028 was REFUSED (export.card-settings.card-non-empty-text-on-one-line) and presentation.slogan with U+2028 was ACCEPTED.
- **Expected behaviour:** Every text that cards print should pass the single cardText rule (one line, no Cc/Cf/Zl/Zp, at most 100 code points, trimmed, NFC), whether resolveCardPresentation drew it or a host supplied it, just as a host-supplied profile is checked again. A malformed or incomplete presentation should be refused before drawing, with a stable coded error.
- **Observed behaviour:** A host-supplied presentation is frozen and printed as given.
- Missing fields print the literal word 'undefined' on separate cards and on sheet subtitles.
- A missing studio name on a sheet or share sheet throws an uncoded TypeError. ShareCardSet.create has already accepted the settings by then, so the failure comes at renderShare.
- Type-correct texts that cardText refuses are drawn: U+2028/U+2029, a soft hyphen, a studio name of spaces only, text over 100 code points, and untrimmed text.
- A host profile in the same settings object is refused for the same characters.
- **Impact:** Only a library host that supplies its own CardSettings.presentation to renderCards, renderIndividualCards, ShareCardSet or exportColorPalette is affected. Such a host can get decoy cards that print 'undefined' or text the project refuses elsewhere, or an uncoded TypeError instead of a coded refusal (CORE-23). It also bypasses the one-line and length rule that AUD-028-API003 recorded as fixed for presentation text. Backup codes, QR content, shares and recovery are unaffected; the CLI and card-session paths are unaffected.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R5; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Run a supplied presentation through the single text owner wherever an export settles it. For example, in ExportDetails.#settledFrom, check each COPY_FIELDS value of settings.presentation with cardText, keeping "" as the hidden-line value, or call resolveCardPresentation with the supplied object and refuse a missing or non-string field with a stable code. Apply the same check to a later job's content.presentation in renderCards. This settles presentation the same way createCardIdentity already settles the profile, while keeping the order of random choices for settings without a presentation.
- **Required verification:** Regression tests through renderCards, renderIndividualCards, ShareCardSet.create/renderShare and exportColorPalette should cover:
- presentation {}, a partial object and a non-string field: each refused with a coded export error before any page is drawn.
- Complete presentations containing U+2028/U+2029, LF, a soft hyphen, text of spaces only, and 100 and 101 code points: each refused or accepted exactly as resolveCardPresentation decides, with trimmed NFC output.
- An empty-string (hidden) line and the settled presentation of the CLI and card session: still printed unchanged.
- Fixed-seed card output for settings without a presentation: unchanged.

##### AUD-037-API007 — Low — shareKeepingNote states a recovery quorum for records of different sets and interpolates unknown packaging

- **Category:** API
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: This is wrong guidance text from a direct library entry point, and only when a caller passes input it should not. The only in-repo caller, the CLI saveShares path, gets one set from sskr-split, or shares that sskr-export has already set-checked with looseSetTrouble/setTrouble. No mandatory security, integrity or recovery guarantee on a main path is broken, no secret is exposed and no split or restore fails.
- **Rules:** CORE-48, CORE-14
- **Related:** AUD-030-API003
- **Affected files and builds:** src/export/sskr-keeping-text.ts:59-78 (quorum: the threshold and groupCount come from records[0], and members are counted only by SetTopology.memberOf with no set check); src/export/sskr-keeping-text.ts:84-91 (shareKeepingNote: the public doc comment does not state the one-set precondition or the refusals, and LAYOUTS[packaging] is not checked at run time). Context only, not a defect: src/sskr/set-topology.ts:65-67 (memberOf is group:member, which is correct for its documented one-set use inside firstConflict).
- **Reproduction:** HEAD is e28d81e. It differs from f8d9a93 only in docs/REMEDIATION.md and docs/audits/README.md, so the source is the same as the audit commit. dist/export/sskr-keeping-text.js matches the source logic.

1. Reran the reviewer probe: `node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/r5/probe3.mjs`. The shareKeepingNote section prints the outputs claimed. ShareCardSet.create on the same mixed input fails with sskr.transport.read-set-refused "SSKR shares belong to different sets."

2. Own probe: `cd /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-API007 && node probe.mjs`. It loads the public vectors from dist. S0/S1 and L0/L1 both have id 14188, gt=1, gc=1, mt=2; they differ in secret length (16 against 32 bytes), so SetTopology.setOf calls them different sets. Results:
- `Share.readSet([S0,L1], false)` -> FAIL sskr.transport.read-set-refused "SSKR shares belong to different sets."
- `shareKeepingNote([S0,L1],'files',false)` -> "All 2 shares are needed for recovery; each file is one share. Store the shares apart."
- `[S0,S1,L0,L1]` -> "All 2 distinct shares are needed for recovery; repeated copies count as one share; ..."
- `[L0,S1,S2]` -> "Any 2 of the 3 shares are enough for recovery; ..."
- `[S0,V0]` (V0 is from the 2-group vector) -> "These shares are fewer than the 2 needed for recovery; repeated copies count as one share; ..."
- packaging 'zip' -> "All 2 shares are needed for recovery; undefined. Store the shares apart."
- packaging 'toString' -> "...; function toString() { [native code] }. Store the shares apart."

3. Callers: grep shows the only caller is src/cli/sskr-output.ts:196. Its shares come from sskr-split (one set) or from sskr-export after ShareInput.looseSetTrouble/rewritten() set checks. The function is still published as the package export ./export/sskr-keeping-text (package.json:236, CHANGELOG).
- **Expected behaviour:** A public function that states a recovery quorum should state it only for records of one set: exact copies counted once, then the same set/member/threshold check that Share.readSet and ShareCardSet.create apply. It should refuse anything else with a coded error, as its siblings do. An unknown packaging value should be refused, not interpolated. The public doc comment should state the one-set precondition and the errors (CORE-20).
- **Observed behaviour:** For records of different sets, shareKeepingNote takes the threshold and group count from the first record and counts members only by group:member. It returns confident readiness text, for example "All 2 shares are needed for recovery" for two shares that restore nothing together. It also calls shares of another set "repeated copies". Share.readSet refuses the same input. A packaging value outside ShareCardPackaging prints "undefined", or native function text for prototype keys such as 'toString'. The one-set precondition is written only on the private quorum helper.
- **Impact:** A host that calls this published entry point with shares it has not set-checked can show the person a false statement that a backup is recoverable, or a wrong count of how many shares are needed. That could lead them to keep an unusable set of shares. The normal CLI path checks sets before calling, so exposure is limited to direct library callers. A bad packaging value only garbles one sentence.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R5; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** In shareKeepingNote/quorum:
- Read every record once.
- Drop exact copies with the existing copy rule in set-topology.ts.
- Check the distinct infos with SetTopology.firstConflict, or Share.readSet(distinct, false), and refuse with SET_CONFLICT_TEXTS[kind].refusal under a coded error.
- Take the threshold and groups from the checked set, through SetTopology, not from records[0].
- Check packaging with Object.hasOwn(LAYOUTS, packaging) and refuse an unknown value with a coded error.
- State the one-set precondition and both refusals in the public doc comment.
- **Required verification:** Add literal tests in test/share-keeping-text.test.ts:
- [sskrSetVector[0], sskrLongVector[1]] is refused as different sets.
- [S0,S1,L0,L1] and [L0,S1,S2] are refused.
- [S0,V0] is refused.
- Packaging 'zip' and 'toString' are refused.
- The existing [s0,s0], [s0,s1,s1], [s0,s1,s2,s2], masked-goal and multi-group cases keep their current text.
Rerun the CLI sskr-split/sskr-export save tests so the guarded path is unchanged.

##### AUD-037-API008 — Low — Public formatEncoded and EncodedForm.codes check BIP39 indexes only in the colour forms: the indexes form writes text that cannot be read back, and the word and Unicode forms report bad input as a fault of MnemoCode

- **Category:** API
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: This is reachable only by a direct library caller that passes indexes the documented precondition already rules out. Every production path (encodeMnemonic, representMnemonic, and EncodedBackup, whose constructor checks indexes under AUD-009-API002) passes checked indexes. No security, integrity or recovery guarantee breaks, and no documented safety check on a main path fails.
- **Rules:** CORE-10, CORE-23, CHECK-API-001
- **Related:** AUD-024-FUN001, AUD-009-API002, AUD-014-API007
- **Affected files and builds:** src/core/encoded-form.ts:104 (english write), :116 (indexes write), :128 (unicode write), :241-251 (write/codes, no guard); src/core/representations.ts:60-74 (formatEncoded, including the json branch through wordsOfIndexes); compare src/core/colors.ts:124-128 (indexesToColors copies with Array.from and calls assertPhraseIndexes, and its comment names the hole hazard); src/core/defined.ts:1-4 and :14-16 (a missing value is "a fault of MnemoCode, never of its input"). Public through package.json exports "." (formatEncoded, src/core.ts:52) and "./core/encoded-form" (package.json:252).
- **Reproduction:** I reran `node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/r5/probe5.mjs` against dist/. dist matches HEAD because src/ is unchanged between f8d9a93 and e28d81e, and the dist writers match the source. The output matches the claim. Index 2048 gives indexes=OK("... 2049"), while english and unicode are REFUSED(core.defined.missing-value) and colors and colors-unicode are REFUSED(core.bip39-sizes.assert-word-indexes-refused). Index 9999 gives "10000", index -1 gives "0" and index 1.5 gives "2.5". With 11, 13 or 0 indexes, english, indexes and unicode write and only the colour forms refuse (core.bip39-sizes.expected-word-indexes).

My own probe, `node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-API008/probe.mjs`, calls the public root formatEncoded with a caller-built EncodedResult. Index 2048, -1, 1.5 or NaN: indexes writes "...2049", "...0", "...2.5" or "...NaN", and parseInput then refuses each of those texts (core.words.indexes-use-seedshift-compatible-numbering-from or core.words.indexes-decimal-integers-from). In the same cases english, unicode and json throw core.defined.missing-value ("MnemoCode expected a value that is not there: this is a fault of MnemoCode."), and both colour forms throw "Every BIP39 index must be an integer from 0 through 2047." A sparse 12-slot array with a hole at slot 6 makes english, indexes and unicode write 11 codes without any error, while the colour forms refuse. The control 12-index vector writes and reads back in all five forms, and encodeMnemonic output is always in range (true).
- **Expected behaviour:** formatEncoded and EncodedForm.write/codes take BIP39 indexes. They are public exports, so like other independently callable boundaries (EncodedBackup under AUD-009-API002, checksumValid after AUD-014) they should refuse a non-integer, out-of-range, missing (hole) or wrongly counted index with the shared core.bip39-sizes input refusal in every form. CORE-22 requires wrong input to fail, and defined.ts reserves "fault of MnemoCode" for internal faults, never for input.
- **Observed behaviour:** Only the two colour forms apply assertPhraseIndexes. The indexes form returns text that its own reader refuses (2049, 0, 2.5, NaN). english, unicode and json report the caller's bad index as core.defined.missing-value, "a fault of MnemoCode". With a sparse array, english, indexes and unicode silently drop the missing code, the hazard the colour writer's comment names and guards against only there. The same public call therefore gives three different outcomes depending on the form.
- **Impact:** A library integrator who passes off-domain indexes, for example 1-based word numbers where the last one becomes 2049, or a sparse array, can write an indexes or Unicode backup that cannot be read back or is missing a code, with no error. In the other forms they get a misleading internal-fault message instead of an input refusal. The CLI and every internal path are unaffected, and no secret is exposed.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R5; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Apply the index rule once in the single owner, EncodedForm.codes. Copy the input with Array.from and call assertPhraseIndexes, or assertWordIndexes plus the count rule if a shorter list must stay writable, before dispatching to the variant writer. formatEncoded's json branch should check result.shiftedIndexes the same way. The colour writer can keep or delegate its existing guard. State the thrown refusal in the doc comments of codes, write and formatEncoded (CORE-20). Keep the output for valid indexes byte-for-byte unchanged.
- **Required verification:** Add a regression test through the public exports (formatEncoded for all five forms and json, and EncodedForm.of(f).codes). Cover indexes 2048, -1, 1.5 and NaN, a hole, and 11, 13 and 0 indexes, each expecting core.bip39-sizes.* refusals and never core.defined.missing-value or returned text. Add controls showing that valid 12- and 24-word vectors and the published vectors in vectors/mnemocode-v1.json still produce identical text in every form. Rerun the self-test and the representations and codes-apart tests.

##### AUD-037-API009 — Low — Several supplied-message error codes each stand for more than one condition, so a host cannot choose its wording from the code

- **Category:** API
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: No security, integrity or recovery guarantee is affected. The default English messages stay correct and distinct, and the shipped CLI shows those messages instead of branching on these codes. The broken promise is an optional host capability (rewording from the code), not a release precondition. Note that the codes become immutable once 0.1.0 ships, so splitting them is cheapest before the release.
- **Rules:** CORE-23, TS-06, CORE-10, CORE-16
- **Related:** AUD-025-ARC001
- **Affected files and builds:** src/core/error-catalog.ts:17-23 (suppliedMessage contract); docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:260; src/core/address-scan.ts:85-92; src/core/colors.ts:148-152; src/cli/output-questions.ts:84-88; src/sskr/repair.ts:301-307; src/sskr/share-set.ts:174-177; src/cli/sskr-export-command.ts:220,294; src/sskr/transport.ts:641 with src/sskr/set-topology.ts:27-35; src/core/backup-check.ts:45-51,586-599; src/cli/share-repair.ts:72,327 against src/sskr/share-input.ts:58,467
- **Reproduction:** The working tree's src equals f8d9a93; the only later commit, e28d81e, changes docs only (git diff --stat f8d9a93 -- src scripts is empty).

(1) The reviewer probes reproduce the counts:
- node r6/catalogs.mjs <repo> printed "catalogs 131, codes 520, supplied 55, numerically suffixed 12".
- r6/slug-src.mjs printed "codes with a literal default text: 420; slugs of that text: 363 (86%)".
- r6/supplied-sites.mjs printed "supplied codes 56; call sites 59" (this count includes scripts/).
- r6/catalog-dups.mjs printed the three shared namespaces and no full code defined twice.

(2) The verifier runtime probe, node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-API009/one-code-many-conditions.mjs <repo>, run against the built dist/:
- addressCount(5,-1) gives code "core.address-scan.address-count-refused" and "The first index must be an integer from 0 through 2147483647."
- addressCount(0,0) gives the same code and "The number of addresses compared must be an integer from 1 through 1000."
- parseColors("#12") gives "core.colors.parse-colors-refused" and "Colors must use #RRGGBB codes, separated or concatenated."
- parseColors("12345") gives the same code and "A continuous RGB stream must contain complete six-digit RRGGBB values."

(3) The verifier static probe, node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-API009/supplied-conflation.mjs <repo>, reported 9 of 55 src supplied codes with more than one create() site or a conditional literal text. Reading each site by hand, absent-path-refused (one condition at two sites) and assert-individual-template-refused (one condition, named or unnamed subject) are not conflation. restore-refused (too few shares, with or without copies left out) is borderline and is not counted. read-set-refused and restore-refused-2 forward several kinds of text. That leaves the eight codes listed in the claim.

(4) The shared namespaces carry "Keep the established ... codes" comments in share-repair.ts, word-candidate.ts and word-table.ts.

(5) Prior records: AUD-025-ARC001 (owned refusals lacked stable codes, so hosts depended on text) is recorded as fixed by introducing ErrorCatalog through an automatic migration. AUD-026 records a Rust-bridge variant under the same ID. Nothing in AUD-001 to AUD-036 or docs/REMEDIATION.md tracks code naming or supplied-code conflation as open.
- **Expected behaviour:** Each stable code identifies one refusal condition, so a host can choose its own wording from the code alone, as ARCHITECTURE.md states. Each code's English text comes from its catalog definition or from one shared rule (CORE-23 and the suppliedMessage doc comment), and one condition has one code (CORE-10).
- **Observed behaviour:** At least eight suppliedMessage codes are thrown for two or more distinct conditions, and the only difference between those conditions is in the message text. Several write those sentences at the throw site rather than taking them from a shared rule. The CLI copies the ShareInput place guard and its message under a second code. Slug-style names and value0 parameters are widespread but break no stated rule.
- **Impact:** A library host that rewords or localizes refusals by code, which ARCHITECTURE.md explicitly allows, cannot tell these conditions apart. For example, it could name the wrong input field for address-count-refused, or the wrong kind of set conflict for read-set-refused. Such a host would have to parse English text, which the coded-error design was meant to avoid. The default messages and all security, recovery and derivation results are unaffected.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R6+R5+R9; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Before 0.1.0 fixes the codes:
- Give each distinct condition its own catalog code, for example separate codes for the first index and the address count, one per set-conflict kind, and one per parse-colors form.
- Move sentences written at throw sites into catalog definitions.
- Keep suppliedMessage for text that truly comes from one shared rule describing one condition.
- Have the CLI call ShareInput's guard instead of copying it.
- Optionally rename the counter-suffix codes to describe their condition, and give message parameters descriptive names.
- **Required verification:** For every split code, a test triggers each former condition and asserts a distinct code and native kind while the default message stays unchanged. A static check, or a reviewed list, confirms that no suppliedMessage code has create() sites or conditional literals for different conditions. Rerunning the verify/AUD-037-API009 probes shows no conflated code. pnpm check and the error-catalog and code-rules tests pass.

#### BLD

##### AUD-037-BLD001 — Low — Unreleased 0.1.0 history is not regrouped: published fixup!, WIP, "temporary" and comment-only commits and multi-audit omnibus commits remain

- **Category:** BLD
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** true: Bullet 3 of the workspace Commits rule (/home/user/Documents/bip_tools/AGENTS.md:59) reads: "Before a release, regroup unreleased commits that do not [read as a short list of real changes]". That makes regrouping an explicit release precondition, and the rule has no exception clause. The repository has no tags, so all 135 commits at f8d9a93 (137 at the current origin/main 61e163b) are unreleased 0.1.0 history. Tagging v0.1.0 on this history would therefore break a workspace release precondition, which meets the given definition. The finding does not block on any security, integrity, recovery, legal or main-path safety ground; the rule precondition is the only reason. It stops blocking once the history is regrouped, or once the owner explicitly records a change or waiver of the rule. docs/REMEDIATION.md:237 anticipates "a regroup or an exception", but no exception is recorded.
- **Rules:** AGENTS.md Commits
- **Related:** AUD-024 informational observation 'History before the release' (docs/audits/AUD-024-2026-10-14.md:325, no ID), AUD-036 limitation on published fixup!/work-in-progress history (docs/audits/AUD-036-2026-10-19.md:209, no ID), docs/REMEDIATION.md:237 pending pre-release decision for ee4499e/8aa9b25 (no ID)
- **Affected files and builds:** Git history of main at f8d9a93 (135 commits, no tags; also on origin/main, which is now at 61e163b). Instances: ee4499e (fixup!, 1 line in docs/releases/v0.1.0.md), 8aa9b25 (title "Work in progress", body "To be regrouped into a few substantial commits before the release"), 45c9f86 (body "Temporary, unsigned: to be signed and regrouped before release"), 163f7bb (comment-only rewrap of 85 files). Omnibus commits: 8aa9b25 (185 files, +19577/-2221), f2d4cff (185, +8898/-1614), 0d7898f (193, +46899/-1163), d4776f7 (177, +11334/-2613), 4978a62 (265, +153138/-930). After the snapshot, 61e163b mixes a test-gate change with a correction of unreleased .git-blame-ignore-revs. Rule: /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/AGENTS.md lines 57-60 (Commits). Release procedure: /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/mnemocode/docs/RELEASING.md:26 checks signatures and dates but has no regroup step. Existing mentions under no finding ID: docs/audits/AUD-024-2026-10-14.md:325 (observation), docs/audits/AUD-036-2026-10-19.md:209 (limitation), docs/REMEDIATION.md:237 (pending decision).
- **Reproduction:** Read-only checks against /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/mnemocode.

(1) `git rev-parse HEAD origin/main` returned 61e163b for both. `git tag | wc -l` returned 0. `git rev-list --count f8d9a93` returned 135.

(2) `git log --oneline f8d9a93 | grep -iE "fixup|wip|work in progress|temporary"` listed ee4499e "fixup! QR images: at most 8 megapixels and 8 bits per sample" and 8aa9b25 "Work in progress: 0.1.0 changes, AUD-020 to AUD-022 remediation and audit records". The body of 8aa9b25 says "To be regrouped into a few substantial commits before the release". The body of 45c9f86 says "Temporary, unsigned: to be signed and regrouped before release". `git show --stat ee4499e` showed 1 file and 1 insertion. 163f7bb ("Comments within 100 columns", "No code changes") changed 85 files.

(3) `git show --shortstat` reproduced the omnibus sizes listed in Locations exactly.

(4) `git branch -r --contains ee4499e` listed origin/main, so the commits are published and a regroup needs a force-push.

(5) My probe tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-BLD001-lens/cited.sh counts main's 7-character hashes cited in the tracked docs/audits records, docs/REMEDIATION.md and .git-blame-ignore-revs. It printed "rev=f8d9a93 commits=135 cited=53" and "rev=61e163b commits=137 cited=55". The first verifier's 52 omitted .git-blame-ignore-revs.

(6) `git log --format='%G? %ad %cd'` showed +0000 for both dates of all 135 commits, so the "unsigned" wording in 45c9f86 is stale.

(7) `git show f8d9a93:.git-blame-ignore-revs` names 9df8ee6, which is not on main. At 61e163b the file names 0dbf39d, which is on main. That side issue was fixed after the snapshot.

(8) Searching the audit records for "regroup" found only the AUD-024 observation, the AUD-036 limitation and REMEDIATION.md:237, none with a finding ID. The other matches were "ShareGroup" identifiers.
- **Expected behaviour:** Per the workspace Commits rule, the unreleased 0.1.0 history should read as a short list of real changes before v0.1.0 is tagged. It should contain no fixup!, work-in-progress or "temporary" commits. Comment fixes and corrections of unreleased work should be folded into the commits they belong to. Each commit should be one coherent change with its tests and documentation. The final tree should be byte-identical to the pre-regroup tip, and every hash cited by audit records should be updated together with the rewrite. Alternatively, the owner records an explicit change or waiver of the rule.
- **Observed behaviour:** Main has 135 unreleased, published commits at f8d9a93 and 137 at the current 61e163b. They include:
- a one-line fixup! commit (ee4499e);
- a work-in-progress commit whose own body says it is to be regrouped before the release (8aa9b25);
- a commit whose body still says "Temporary, unsigned" (45c9f86);
- a standalone comment-only commit (163f7bb);
- five omnibus commits of 177 to 265 files that mix features, fixes from several audits, native or engine patches and the records of up to four audits.

The pattern continues after the snapshot: 61e163b folds a correction of unreleased .git-blame-ignore-revs into an unrelated test-gate commit. docs/RELEASING.md has no regroup step. The project has noted the item only as an observation, a limitation and a pending owner decision, never as a tracked finding.
- **Impact:** This is a process and maintainability defect only. It has no effect on the shipped program's behaviour, secrets, outputs or build composition. The 0.1.0 history would not read as the short list of real changes that the workspace requires, and bisecting, reviewing and reverting are harder because single commits mix unrelated fixes and audit records. Correcting it now requires a force-push of the published main, plus a coordinated update of about 53 to 55 hash references in the audit records, the register and .git-blame-ignore-revs. It is low severity, but it blocks the release under the workspace rule's explicit pre-release precondition.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R8; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json). A second, independent verifier checked severity and release blocking: agreed.
- **Recommended fix:** The owner decides before tagging; no agent may rewrite or force-push without explicit owner authorization (workspace AGENTS.md:52).

To regroup:
1. Rebuild the unreleased history non-interactively, with TZ=UTC and signing on, into a few coherent commits. Fold ee4499e, 163f7bb, 61e163b's .git-blame-ignore-revs correction and the other small follow-ups into the commits they belong to. Split the omnibus commits by meaning, and drop the WIP and "temporary" messages.
2. Check that the new tip's tree equals the old tip's tree.
3. In the same rewrite, update every cited hash in docs/audits, docs/REMEDIATION.md and .git-blame-ignore-revs, or record an old-to-new mapping. This resolves the conflict with REMEDIATION's statement that historical reports remain unchanged.
4. Force-push only with the owner's explicit approval, then cancel and delete the superseded CI runs.
5. Add a regroup check to docs/RELEASING.md.

If the owner keeps the history instead, record the waiver or the rule change explicitly. A pending-decision note is not enough.
- **Required verification:** - `git rev-parse <old-tip>^{tree}` equals `git rev-parse <new-tip>^{tree}`.
- `git log --format='%s%n%b' <new-tip>` contains no fixup!, squash!, work-in-progress or "temporary/unsigned" text, and the commit list reads as a short list of coherent changes.
- Every commit has a gpgsig header, and both of its dates are +0000.
- Every hash cited in the tracked audit records, docs/REMEDIATION.md and .git-blame-ignore-revs resolves to a commit on main, or has a recorded mapping. Rerunning the AUD-037-BLD001-lens cited probe on the new tip shows no dangling citations.
- origin/main equals the regrouped tip, and superseded CI runs are cancelled.
- docs/RELEASING.md includes the regroup check.
- If the owner waives the rule instead, the decision appears in the workspace rules or the register.

##### AUD-037-BLD002 — Low — The SSKR WASM in the 0.1.0 executables lacks the MIT copyright and permission notices of libsecp256k1, ur and phf_shared compiled into it

- **Category:** BLD
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** true: AUD-037 counts a breached legal obligation of distribution as release blocking. The 0.1.0 release job publishes three executables, each with its licenses file. Each executable embeds sskr-wasm/generated/recovery_sskr_wasm_bg.wasm (build-executable.mjs:69), and that WASM contains compiled code under the MIT licence only: the libsecp256k1 C library, ur 0.4.1 and phf_shared 0.11.3. The MIT licence requires its copyright notice and permission notice in every copy, and neither the release licenses file nor UPSTREAM_NOTICES.md carries them. The project's own executable-notices.mjs:2-4 says these notices must travel with a binary. AUD-005-BLD002 was not blocking, but it was judged under an older rule ("a legal-compliance gap, not a functional one"). This audit's rule applies instead. No workspace rule makes notices an explicit release precondition, so the blocking rests only on the legal-obligation clause.
- **Rules:** CHECK-BLD-003, CHECK-DOC-002
- **Related:** AUD-005-BLD002
- **Affected files and builds:** sskr-wasm/UPSTREAM_NOTICES.md:87-90 (phf rows), 111 (secp256k1-sys listed only as CC0-1.0), 134 (ur) and 161 (the only Copyright line, Blockchain Commons); scripts/executable-notices.mjs:2-4, 26-28 and 102 (the SSKR section is just LICENSE, NOTICE.md and UPSTREAM_NOTICES.md); scripts/build-executable.mjs:69 (embeds the WASM); scripts/verify-executable.mjs:145-152 (checks only that section headings exist); THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md:75
- **Reproduction:** I checked the evidence myself at HEAD 61e163b, which differs from f8d9a93 only in package.json for these paths (git diff --stat). Blob IDs match f8d9a93: the WASM is b4c729d1 and UPSTREAM_NOTICES.md is 77b4b4d7.
(1) Running `strings -n 8` on the WASM finds three kinds of evidence:
- the C ARG_CHECK string "ctx == NULL || rustsecp256k1_v0_11_context_is_proper(ctx)", which comes from depend/secp256k1/src/secp256k1.c:153. secp256k1-sys build.rs:52-55 compiles that file with cc::Build.
- the panic path ".../ur-0.4.1/src/bytewords.rs".
- the panic path ".../phf_shared-0.11.3/src/lib.rs".
(2) The upstream licence files in workingspace/cargo/registry are all MIT:
- secp256k1-sys-0.11.0/depend/secp256k1/COPYING: "Copyright (c) 2013 Pieter Wuille".
- ur-0.4.1/LICENSE: "Copyright (c) 2020 Dominik Spicher".
- phf_shared-0.11.3/LICENSE: "Copyright (c) 2014-2022 Steven Fackler, Yuki Okushi".
(3) In the coordinator's mnemocode-0.1.0-linux-x64-licenses.txt, the counts are Pieter Wuille 0, Spicher 0, Steven Fackler 0 and Okushi 0. "Blockchain Commons" appears 5 times.
(4) UPSTREAM_NOTICES.md has exactly one Copyright line (line 161, Blockchain Commons). Every other crate has only a table row.
(5) I read the first verifier's probe.sh and probe.out. Its logic is sound and agrees with my checks. Its comparison shows the SSKR section of the licenses file is exactly LICENSE, NOTICE.md and UPSTREAM_NOTICES.md joined together.
(6) In earlier audits, only AUD-005-BLD002 (low, fixed, not blocking) and AUD-010-BLD006 touch notices. Neither is open for the SSKR crates' MIT notices. AUD-005-decisions.md item 2 rated the broader gap low.
(7) package.json has "private": true. docs/RELEASING.md lists the release contents as three executables, their licence notices, SHA256SUMS and signatures.
- **Expected behaviour:** Every distributed copy of the SSKR WASM must carry the copyright and permission notices of the MIT-only code compiled into it: libsecp256k1 (Pieter Wuille), ur (Dominik Spicher) and phf/phf_shared (Steven Fackler, Yuki Okushi). The executable's licenses file is the main such copy. This is what the MIT licence requires and what executable-notices.mjs promises.
- **Observed behaviour:** UPSTREAM_NOTICES.md gives only a licence name for ur and phf_shared. It lists secp256k1-sys only as CC0-1.0, the crate's own declaration, and does not mention the MIT C library the crate bundles and compiles. It also states that it is "not a new upstream license audit". The executable licenses file copies that file as is, so it contains none of the three MIT notices. verify-executable.mjs checks only that the "SSKR engine" heading exists. The committed WASM in the repository and tag source archives has the same gap.
- **Impact:** The 0.1.0 executables, and the WASM committed to the source tree, would redistribute MIT-licensed code in binary form without the notices that licence requires. This is a compliance gap in distribution and affects only legal compliance: function, integrity and secrets are not affected.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R8; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json). A second, independent verifier checked severity and release blocking: agreed.
- **Recommended fix:** Add the full upstream licence texts with their copyright lines to UPSTREAM_NOTICES.md, or to a generated SSKR notices file that executable-notices.mjs reads, for every crate that is under MIT only and linked into the WASM:
- libsecp256k1, from secp256k1-sys/depend/secp256k1/COPYING. Add a note that secp256k1-sys bundles MIT C code, not only CC0 Rust.
- ur 0.4.1.
- phf and phf_shared 0.11.3.
Also check whether other non-dual crates are linked (crunchy and slab under MIT; curve25519-dalek, ed25519-dalek, x25519-dalek and subtle under BSD-3-Clause; minicbor under BlueOak), and add their texts if they are. For dual MIT/Apache-2.0 crates, state the licence elected. Generate this from the pinned crate sources, for example with a reviewed cargo-about run, not by hand. Extend verify-executable.mjs or a unit test so it fails when any required copyright holder is missing from the licenses file.
- **Required verification:** Rebuild the executable licenses file. Then confirm that "Pieter Wuille", "Dominik Spicher" and "Steven Fackler" each appear at least once, together with the MIT permission text, in UPSTREAM_NOTICES.md (or its replacement) and in the SSKR section of mnemocode-0.1.0-<platform>-licenses.txt on all three platforms. Rerun tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-BLD002/probe.sh and get non-zero counts. Show that a check fails when one of those notices is removed.

##### AUD-037-BLD003 — Low — .git-blame-ignore-revs names a formatting commit that no longer exists

- **Category:** BLD
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** verified
- **Release blocking:** false: This only affects how git blame and the GitHub blame view attribute lines. Shipped code, the build output and every security, integrity and recovery guarantee are unaffected. CORE-21 is a formatting and maintainability rule. It is not explicitly a release precondition.
- **Rules:** CORE-21
- **Related:** AUD-006 informational observation (line 156) asserting the file identifies formatting commits
- **Affected files and builds:** .git-blame-ignore-revs:5 at f8d9a93 (the file was added in c0f01b1). CORE-21 in the workspace coding guide, code-core.instructions.md.
- **Reproduction:** Step 1: ran `git show f8d9a93:.git-blame-ignore-revs`. Line 5 is 9df8ee6561efbe7878235da20ce360c088286642 and line 8 is 163f7bb5cf97f1883ad2e78dc514105f10e0aee6.

Step 2: ran `git cat-file -t 9df8ee6561efbe7878235da20ce360c088286642`. It printed "fatal: git cat-file: could not get object info" and exited 128. The hash also does not appear in `git rev-list --all` (735 commits), and the repository is not shallow.

Step 3: `git cat-file -t 0dbf39d3b6b0fbb3c08a46a5b1414cc20871a470` prints "commit". 0dbf39d is an ancestor of f8d9a93. Its subject, "Prettier: the workspace style, double quotes and 100 columns", matches the comment on line 4. It changes 123 files (+5139/-5140).

Step 4: ran the probe /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-BLD003/probe.sh, with output in probe.out beside it. It blames every file that 0dbf39d touched and that still exists at f8d9a93, once with the shipped ignore file and once with a corrected one. Each run starts with `--ignore-revs-file ""`, because the local clone sets blame.ignoreRevsFile to the working-tree file, which is already corrected at 61e163b. An empty `-c blame.ignoreRevsFile=` did not clear that setting when tested. Results: 2307 lines are blamed on 0dbf39d with the shipped file and 1 line with the corrected file. Git 2.53 skipped the unresolvable entry without any warning.

Step 5: AUD-006 line 156 (informational) only says the file "identifies formatting commits". AUD-008 to AUD-031 list the file in their inventories without flagging it. docs/REMEDIATION.md at f8d9a93 has no entry for it.

Step 6: commit 61e163b is later than the baseline and is now HEAD and origin/main. It replaces line 5 with 0dbf39d…a470 and records this in REMEDIATION.md under "Test-gate follow-up (2026-10-19)".
- **Expected behaviour:** CORE-21 and the file's own header require `.git-blame-ignore-revs` to list every commit that only reformats code, using reachable full object names, so that git blame and GitHub skip those commits.
- **Observed behaviour:** At f8d9a93, line 5 names 9df8ee6561efbe7878235da20ce360c088286642. That object does not exist in the repository; it is probably the hash the Prettier commit had before history was rewritten. The real reformat commit, 0dbf39d3b6b0fbb3c08a46a5b1414cc20871a470, is not listed. Git silently drops the unresolvable entry, so 2,307 lines at f8d9a93 are still blamed on the formatting commit (the claim says 2,302; the substance is the same). With the corrected entry, 1 line remains. The 163f7bb entry is valid.
- **Impact:** git blame with the documented configuration, and the GitHub blame view, attribute about 2,300 lines to a commit that only reformatted them. This hides the commits that actually wrote those lines and slows code review and audit tracing. Nothing reports the stale entry. There is no effect on runtime behaviour, secrets or release artifacts.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R8+R6; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Replace line 5 with 0dbf39d3b6b0fbb3c08a46a5b1414cc20871a470; commit 61e163b, made after the baseline, already does this. Optionally, add a check that every 40-hex line in `.git-blame-ignore-revs` resolves to a commit (`git cat-file -e <oid>^{commit}`), so a later history rewrite cannot leave stale entries without anyone noticing.
- **Required verification:** At the release commit, every entry in `.git-blame-ignore-revs` must resolve with `git cat-file -t <oid>` to "commit" and be an ancestor of the release commit. The probe in /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-BLD003/probe.sh, pointed at that commit, should report about 1 line blamed on 0dbf39d with the shipped file.

##### AUD-037-BLD004 — Low — No maintained gate runs the SSKR workspace's native Rust tests, including the only checks of the vendored secret-lifetime patches

- **Category:** BLD
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** fixed
- **Release blocking:** false: Shipped behaviour is not affected. The 18 native tests are recorded as passing in manual runs (REMEDIATION.md, AUD-035 remediation), and the native sources are unchanged since then. The gap is that no maintained gate re-runs them, which is a maintainability and regression-protection defect. It does not break a mandatory security, integrity or recovery guarantee, and no workspace rule makes Rust tests in a gate an explicit release precondition.
- **Rules:** CORE-26, CORE-32, CORE-43, CORE-47, RUST-08
- **Related:** AUD-035-SEC001, AUD-035-API002
- **Affected files and builds:** package.json:353 (check:rust), package.json:355 (check), package.json:360 (verify); Dockerfile.sskr:63 and :69-77 (cargo fetch, cargo build only); .github/workflows/ci.yml:39-44 (rustup clippy,rustfmt; pnpm check / pnpm verify); AGENTS.md:10-11 (documents check:rust as clippy and rustfmt, verify as the full tests); tests that never run in a gate: sskr-wasm/rust/src/lib.rs (9), sskr-wasm/rust/src/refusal.rs (2), sskr-wasm/rust/vendor/bc-shamir-0.13.0/src/recovery_secret.rs (3), sskr-wasm/rust/vendor/sskr-0.12.0/src/secret.rs (4); doctests in vendor/bc-shamir-0.13.0/src/lib.rs, src/shamir.rs and vendor/sskr-0.12.0/src/lib.rs
- **Reproduction:** I wrote a static, read-only probe, /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-BLD004/probe.sh, and ran it as `probe.sh <mnemocode> f8d9a93`. Output at f8d9a933ba9b:
- `check:rust` is `cargo clippy --locked --workspace --all-targets ... -D warnings && cargo fmt --all --check`. It contains no cargo test.
- `check` calls `pnpm check:rust`, and `verify` is `pnpm format:check && pnpm check && pnpm build && pnpm test`, where `test` is `vitest run`.
- `git grep 'cargo ... test'` over package.json, scripts, .github, test, Dockerfile.sskr, cloud and AGENTS.md finds "(none)".
- Dockerfile.sskr runs only `cargo install wasm-bindgen-cli`, `cargo fetch --locked` and `cargo build --target wasm32-unknown-unknown --release`.
- ci.yml installs the clippy and rustfmt components and runs `pnpm check` (push) or `pnpm verify` (tag or dispatch). executable.yml adds only `pnpm verify:sskr` (the WASM rebuild).
- `#[test]` count is 9 in src/lib.rs, 2 in src/refusal.rs, 3 in vendor bc-shamir recovery_secret.rs and 4 in vendor sskr secret.rs, total 18. Rust doc examples: 2 in bc-shamir lib.rs, 2 in shamir.rs, 1 in sskr lib.rs.

The vendored tests depend on `#[cfg(test)]` drop observers (RETIRED, `tests::record_drop`) that are compiled only in a native `cargo test`. The WASM-based Vitest suite and the self-test therefore cannot exercise them. The group-index refusal is the exception: it is covered through WASM by test/aud-035-native-self-test.test.ts.

Earlier audits only record manual cargo test runs (AUD-026: 7/7, AUD-030, AUD-032: rust-native, REMEDIATION: 18 tests for AUD-035-SEC001/API002). None of them, and nothing in REMEDIATION.md, records this gate gap as a finding.

Post-audit check: at 61e163b (main, after the audit HEAD) the same probe shows `check:rust` extended with `&& cargo test --locked --workspace --all-targets ...`. `git diff --stat f8d9a93 61e163b -- sskr-wasm .github Dockerfile.sskr` is empty. I ran no cargo, test suite or build, as the rules require.
- **Expected behaviour:** The repository's maintained gates should execute the SSKR workspace's native tests. CORE-43 counts "the tests" of the touched part among the fast gates, and the workspace rule requires the full test suite before a release. These tests include the only checks of the vendored zeroize-before-free patches behind AUD-035-SEC001, so at least one of pnpm check, pnpm verify or CI should run them.
- **Observed behaviour:** At f8d9a93, no gate runs the native tests.
- pnpm check:rust runs only clippy and rustfmt.
- pnpm check, pnpm verify, Dockerfile.sskr and both workflows never invoke cargo test.
- The 18 native unit tests (11 first-party bridge tests, 7 vendored secret-lifetime tests) and the vendored doctests run only when someone runs cargo test by hand.

The claim needs two corrections. Only 7 of the 18 tests guard the vendored patches. The documented contracts (RUST-02, AGENTS.md) require only clippy and rustfmt, so the gate does what it says; the defect is the missing test coverage under CORE-43 and the release-level full-suite rule, not a breach of RUST-02, RUST-08, CORE-26 or CORE-47.

Commit 61e163b, made after the audit HEAD, adds cargo test --workspace --all-targets to check:rust. Doctests still do not run, because --all-targets does not include doc tests.
- **Impact:** A future change to the vendored bc-shamir or sskr secret-lifetime code, or to the first-party bridge, could undo the native wiping of a failed-digest secret or of Secret clones. pnpm check, pnpm verify and CI would all stay green. AUD-035-SEC001 and AUD-035-API002 are marked Verified on manual native runs that no maintained check repeats. Current shipped behaviour is not affected.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R3; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Add `cargo test --locked --workspace --target-dir ../../node_modules/.cache/sskr-cargo` to pnpm check:rust, or a separate script that pnpm check and pnpm verify call, so CI runs it on push and on release. Do this for the audited release commit; 61e163b already does it for the unit tests. To cover the retained doctests too, drop `--all-targets` (the default target selection includes lib unit tests and doctests) or add a `cargo test --doc --workspace` step. Then update the AGENTS.md description of check:rust and the CI comment so they say the native tests run.
- **Required verification:** On the release commit, confirm that `pnpm check:rust` reports 18 passing unit tests across the three workspace crates, plus the vendored doctests if they are included. Make one vendored Drop observer assertion fail temporarily and confirm that pnpm check exits non-zero, then revert. Confirm that the CI push job and the release (pnpm verify) path both reach that step.

##### AUD-037-BLD005 — Low — Vitest discovered test files outside test/, including ignored local audit evidence (fixed after the audited commit in 61e163b)

- **Category:** BLD
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** verified
- **Release blocking:** false: The suite still runs all 209 maintained test files on every host. The extra files can only add tests or failures; they cannot suppress or skip a maintained test. No security, integrity or recovery guarantee depends on this, the ignored file is not shipped, and no release-precondition rule makes the suite's discovery scope a release gate. The defect is also already fixed on main in 61e163b.
- **Rules:** TS-14, CORE-32
- **Affected files and builds:** vitest.config.ts:12-17 at f8d9a93 (the test block has only testTimeout/hookTimeout and no include); package.json:358 "test": "vitest run" and :360 "verify" (both rely on Vitest's default discovery); .gitignore:22 docs/audits/*-evidence/ (the ignored local file that gets discovered)
- **Reproduction:** I read `git show f8d9a933:vitest.config.ts` and found no `include`. Vitest 5.0.3 (node_modules/vitest/dist/chunks/defaults.D2ip7f-X.js:5-6) uses defaultInclude ["**/*.{test,spec}.?(c|m)[jt]s?(x)"] and defaultExclude ["**/node_modules/**", "**/.git/**"], and it does not read .gitignore.

Probe: I copied the f8d9a93 test options, with the Vite cache moved, to /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-BLD005/vitest.f8.config.mjs. For comparison I copied the 61e163b options (include test/**/*.test.ts) to vitest.61e.config.mjs. From the repository root I ran `timeout 60 node node_modules/vitest/vitest.mjs list --filesOnly --configLoader runner --root <repo> --config /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-BLD005/vitest.<f8|61e>.config.mjs`. Output went to verify/AUD-037-BLD005/list-f8.txt and list-61e.txt; both exited 0.
- f8 config: 211 files, 209 under test/, plus docs/audits/AUD-008-harnesses/runner-probe.test.ts and docs/audits/AUD-025-evidence/runtime-probe.test.ts.
- 61e config: 209 files, all under test/.
- `git status --short` was unchanged afterwards (only the coordinator's untracked docs/audits/AUD-037-harnesses/).

Cited evidence: the grep on docs/audits/AUD-037-evidence/f8-test-full.log (command `pnpm exec vitest run --maxWorkers=4`, exit 0) prints exactly those two non-test/ files. The summary reads "Test Files 211 passed (211)" and "Tests 3001 passed (3001)". The grep finds 210 file lines rather than 211 because test/aud-033-working-memory.test.ts is printed after an ANSI cursor sequence; this does not affect the result.

Tracking status: `git ls-files` shows that runner-probe.test.ts is tracked and that AUD-025-evidence is untracked. `git check-ignore -v` attributes the AUD-025 file to .gitignore:22.

Clean hosts: CI runs `pnpm verify` (ci.yml:44) and `pnpm test` (ci.yml:85) on a fresh checkout, so it discovers 210 files.

Prior records: AUD-036 (md:87) already observed "211 files ... includes one ignored historical diagnostic" and reported a separate "210 files" project view, but it raised no finding. AUD-032 and AUD-035 assessed TS-14 as supported without noting this.

Fix after the audited commit: `git show 61e163b` (2026-10-19 17:44 UTC, after the audit's full run at 17:17-17:23 UTC) adds `include: ["test/**/*.test.ts"]`. It also records the change in docs/REMEDIATION.md under "Test-gate follow-up (2026-10-19)", with no audit ID.
- **Expected behaviour:** Per TS-14, MnemoCode's Vitest suite consists of the `*.test.ts` files in `test/`. `pnpm test` and `pnpm verify` should discover the same files on every checkout of a commit, independent of ignored local audit evidence.
- **Observed behaviour:** At f8d9a93, vitest.config.ts sets no `include`, so Vitest's default glob applies across the whole repository except node_modules and .git. On the owner's machine the suite discovers 211 files. Besides the 209 under test/, they are the tracked historical probe docs/audits/AUD-008-harnesses/runner-probe.test.ts and the Git-ignored local file docs/audits/AUD-025-evidence/runtime-probe.test.ts. A clean checkout such as CI discovers 210.
- **Impact:** The composition and outcome of the local test gate depend on untracked, ignored files that neither CI nor other hosts have. A failing or slow local diagnostic would fail `pnpm verify` without any tracked change, and file and test totals (211, 210 or 209) are not comparable across hosts or audits. Historical audit probes run as maintained tests outside test/, against TS-14. No shipped behaviour, secret handling or maintained test coverage is affected.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R6+coordinator; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Limit discovery to `include: ["test/**/*.test.ts"]` in vitest.config.ts and run historical audit probes only through their explicit recorded commands. Commit 61e163b on main already does this. Optionally add a check that the discovered file list equals the tracked `test/**/*.test.ts` files.
- **Required verification:** At the fix commit (61e163b or later), with the ignored docs/audits/AUD-025-evidence/runtime-probe.test.ts still on disk, `vitest list --filesOnly` lists only `test/` files (209 at 61e163b). A full `pnpm test` must report the same file count on the local machine and on a clean CI checkout. The AUD-008 runner probe must still pass when run by its explicit path.

##### AUD-037-BLD006 — Low — Lint and code-rules gates enforce less than the guide's Check lines claim

- **Category:** BLD
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: These gates check style and maintainability rules. No mandatory security, integrity or recovery guarantee depends on them, no legal distribution obligation is involved, and no release precondition says these particular checks must fail the build. At f8d9a93 the only violations that actually get past them are missing doc comments on public class and interface members. The other four gaps are latent: nothing in the tree currently exercises them.
- **Rules:** TS-12, TS-06, TOOL-02, CORE-15, CORE-20
- **Related:** AUD-024-BLD001, AUD-024-BLD002
- **Affected files and builds:** package.json:354 (lint script); scripts/verify-code-rules.mjs:165-168 (checkLength cuts the count at the first #[cfg(test)] line); scripts/verify-code-rules.mjs:425-426 (classesOf sees a parent only when "extends" directly follows the class name); scripts/verify-code-rules.mjs:186-192 (checkShellHeader checks only the header); scripts/verify-code-rules.mjs:513-534 (checkDocComments checks only top-level `export` declarations). Contracts: code-typescript.instructions.md TS-06, TS-11, TS-12; code-tooling.instructions.md TOOL-02; code-core.instructions.md CORE-15, CORE-20 and "Gates by name"; workspace docs/adr/0002. All of these files are unchanged between f8d9a93 and the working-tree HEAD 61e163b, except that the check:rust line in package.json gained a cargo test step.
- **Reproduction:** Lint probe (oxlint 1.88.0 from the repository, repository .oxlintrc.json). The fixture is /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-BLD006/lint/stale-directive.ts, which holds a `// oxlint-disable-next-line no-debugger -- probe of a stale exception` line above `export const value = 1;`.
- `oxlint -c <repo>/.oxlintrc.json --report-unused-disable-directives stale-directive.ts` printed "stale-directive.ts:1:1: warning: Unused oxlint-disable directive (no problems were reported)." and exited 0.
- The same command with `--report-unused-disable-directives-severity=error` printed the same line as "error" and exited 1.

Code-rules probe. In /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-BLD006/gate/ I placed verify-code-rules.mjs and source-tokens.mjs extracted with `git show f8d9a93:`, an empty pnpm-lock.yaml, and these files:
- src/errors.ts: generic `class Failure<Detail> extends Error` with no code, plus a control `class Plain extends Error {}`.
- src/members.ts: a documented exported class with an undocumented public method, plus a control undocumented `export const`.
- scripts/unquoted.sh: shebang, `set -euo pipefail`, then `rm -rf $1/build`.
- src/early.rs: 903 lines, `#[cfg(test)]` on line 2 above a helper function, then 900 non-test `pub fn` lines.
- src/control.rs: 901 lines with no cfg(test).

`node scripts/verify-code-rules.mjs` exited 1 and reported exactly four problems, all on the controls:
- control.rs:801 CORE-15
- Plain TS-06, twice (name and code)
- members.ts:8 TS-11 for the undocumented const

The generic error class, the 903-line early-cfg(test) Rust file, the unquoted shell expansion and the undocumented public method all passed.

R6's probes reach the same result. I diffed its copies of both scripts against f8d9a93 and they are identical.

State at f8d9a93:
- No stale directive: the coordinator's f8 check log shows the lint step with no output.
- No shell script outside the frozen docs/audits/.
- Largest first-party Rust file: 445 lines (sskr-wasm/rust/src/lib.rs).
- Only generic Error subclass: CodedError in src/core/error-catalog.ts, which is named correctly and has `readonly code`.
- Public members of exported classes do lack doc comments and pass the gate. Examples are EncodedBackupCheck's `get dated()`, `get wordCount()` and `async check()` at src/core/backup-check.ts:353, 357 and 416. R6's heuristic count is 23 class members and 340 interface members without /** */.
- **Expected behaviour:** Each gate named on a rule's `Check:` line should fail the build when that rule is broken. The guide says Check names how a rule is enforced, and it defines lint as running the guide's rules as errors. Specifically:
- A stale lint exception should fail `lint` (TS-12).
- code-rules should flag a misnamed or code-less error class whether or not the class is generic (TS-06, class names).
- code-rules should flag unquoted expansions in shell scripts (TOOL-02).
- CORE-15 should leave out only a Rust file's #[cfg(test)] test module from the line count.
- code-rules should flag undocumented public items of library modules (CORE-20).
- **Observed behaviour:** - oxlint reports a stale exception as a warning and exits 0, so `pnpm lint` and `pnpm check` stay green. This literally matches TS-12 and ADR 0002, which say only that the flag "reports" a stale exception, but nothing enforces it.
- code-rules ignores any class whose name is followed by type parameters, because it reads the parent only when `extends` immediately follows the name.
- code-rules checks only the `set -euo pipefail` header of shell scripts. Its own header and the guide's "Gates by name" scope it to script headers, while the TOOL-02 Check line names code-rules for the whole rule, quoting included.
- code-rules stops counting a Rust file at the first `#[cfg(test)]` line, even when that line marks a helper function and not the trailing test module.
- code-rules checks doc comments only on top-level `export` declarations. CORE-20 names code-rules for "every public item", yet undocumented public members of exported classes and interfaces pass.
- **Impact:** The gates are weaker than the guide's Check lines claim, so later changes can bring in the following violations with every gate still green:
- stale lint exceptions
- misnamed or code-less generic error classes
- unquoted shell expansions
- Rust files over 800 lines
- undocumented public members

At f8d9a93, only the last of these is actually present (missing doc comments on public members). The others are latent. No runtime behaviour, secret handling or release artifact is affected.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R6; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** - **Stale lint exceptions:** in the lint script, replace `--report-unused-disable-directives` with `--report-unused-disable-directives-severity=error`, or add `--deny-warnings`. Update the TS-12 and ADR 0002 wording to match.
- **Generic error classes:** in classesOf, skip a balanced `<...>` type-parameter list after the class name before looking for `extends`.
- **Rust test module:** in checkLength, cut the count only at a `#[cfg(test)]` line that is followed by a `mod` item, preferably the last one.
- **Shell quoting:** either add an unquoted-expansion check (or shellcheck SC2086) to code-rules, or change the TOOL-02 Check line to "code-rules (header), review".
- **Public members:** either extend TS-11/CORE-20 checking to public members of exported classes and interfaces, or narrow CORE-20's Check line for TypeScript to "code-rules (exports), review".

For each change, add a regression case to test/code-rules.test.ts.
- **Required verification:** - Rerun both probes under tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-BLD006/ against the fixed scripts. The lint probe should exit 1 on the stale directive. The code-rules probe should report Failure (TS-06), early.rs (CORE-15), and either unquoted.sh (TOOL-02) and the undocumented public method (CORE-20/TS-11) or the corrected Check lines in their place.
- Confirm the existing controls are still reported, and that `pnpm check` stays green on the real tree, or that real member-doc gaps are fixed in the same change.
- Confirm test/code-rules.test.ts covers each new case.

##### AUD-037-BLD007 — Low — The SSKR engine self-test refusal checks accept any error, so a decode or operational failure passes as the expected refusal

- **Category:** BLD
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: The shipped fixtures are sound, and the shipped engine refuses all three cases with the stable combination code. The startup gate works on the main path at f8d9a93. The defect weakens the gate's ability to detect a future fixture, host or engine regression. It does not break a mandatory security, integrity or recovery guarantee in 0.1.0, and per AUD-035-API002 the JS transport refuses impossible metadata before the engine sees it.
- **Rules:** CORE-28, CORE-27
- **Related:** AUD-035-API002, AUD-033-API003
- **Affected files and builds:** src/sskr/known-answers.ts:233-246 (/.+/u at line 244, with the comment "Hosts word engine errors differently" at line 243); src/core/self-test-check.ts:95-116 (expectRefused/expectRefusedLater contract); src/sskr/share-platform.ts:13-38 (ShareCombiner refusal contract, ShareCombineRefusalError code sskr.engine.combine); test/startup-edges.test.ts:207-239 (acceptance-only failing controls for two of the three cases); test/aud-035-native-self-test.test.ts:5-7 (positive-only)
- **Reproduction:** Probe /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-BLD007/probe.mjs runs against the dist/ built from HEAD. It rebuilds the fixtures exactly as known-answers.ts does. Command: cd /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-BLD007 && (ulimit -v 3000000; timeout 60 node probe.mjs). Output, saved in probe-output.txt:
(A) Real engine on the three shipped refusal cases. Incomplete quorum: code sskr.engine.combine, "SSKR shares did not contain enough groups". Damaged value: the same code and the same message. Impossible group: sskr.engine.combine, "SSKR group index is invalid".
(B) The impossible-group fixture with its CRC left stale: code sskr.engine.decode, "The SSKR share could not be decoded.", and expectRefusedLater(..., /.+/u, ...) passes.
(C) The real checkSskrKnownAnswers with a host whose combiner throws a code-less operational Error for the impossible-group set: the self-test passes.
(D) A copy of dist/sskr/known-answers.js run against a host that emulates the pre-AUD-035-API002 engine, which accepts a decodable impossible group. As shipped: "SSKR engine impossible group index was accepted." With only the CRC recompute line removed from the impossible-group fixture, the self-test passes.
probe-no-entry.mjs: a copy with the impossible-group entry deleted still resolves with nodeSharePlatform, so the aud-035 test cannot fail on that removal.
The reviewer probe r6/probe/impossible-group.mts was read and is sound. It is equivalent to parts A and B; it was not rerun because its .ts imports need a TS loader.
- **Expected behaviour:** Each engine refusal check should accept only the refusal it names. Per the ShareCombiner contract, that is a ShareCombineRefusalError, which has the stable code sskr.engine.combine. A decode, operational or initialization failure should fail the check as "refused for another reason", and each check should have a test showing it can fail both ways.
- **Observed behaviour:** All three engine refusal checks pass any thrown value whose text is not empty, because of /.+/u. A fixture that regresses to a decode error, or a host that throws an operational error, counts as the expected refusal. A JS failing control exists only for acceptance of the incomplete-quorum and damaged-value cases. The impossible-group check has none, and its only test asserts that the self-test resolves. A native Rust unit test of the engine's group-index refusal exists. At f8d9a93 it was not run by pnpm check or CI; it was added to check:rust only in the later commit 61e163b.
- **Impact:** The startup known-answer gate can silently stop exercising the property it names. If the impossible-group fixture regresses to a decode error, the gate still passes with an engine that accepts an impossible group index, which hides a reintroduced AUD-035-API002. The same applies to the damaged-value fixture. The impact is bounded: the shipped fixtures and engine behave correctly, the engine bytes are pinned and reproducibly checked, and the JS transport refuses impossible metadata first. No secret is exposed.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R6+R3; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** 1. Check the refusal kind instead of the message text. Require ShareCombineRefusalError.is(error) (code sskr.engine.combine) for the three engine cases, for example with an expectRefusedLater variant that takes an error predicate. Message text cannot tell the damaged-value case from the incomplete-quorum case, since both read "did not contain enough groups".
2. Correct the comment about hosts wording errors differently.
3. Add failing controls in test/startup-edges.test.ts:
   - a host that accepts the impossible-group set must fail with "was accepted";
   - a host that throws a decode-coded or code-less error for each case must fail with "was refused for another reason".
4. Rename or extend test/aud-035-native-self-test.test.ts so that its "only when" claim is tested.
- **Required verification:** The new controls must fail against the current /.+/u implementation and pass after the change:
- an accepting host for the impossible-group set;
- a decode-coded refusal and a code-less operational error for each of the three cases.
checkSskrKnownAnswers(nodeSharePlatform) must still resolve. Rerun verify/AUD-037-BLD007/probe.mjs: parts B, C and D must then report self-test failure. Confirm that the browser host's combiner throws the sskr.engine.combine code for all three cases before relying on the code check there.
