##### AUD-037-DOC001 — Medium — The bundled manual recovery guide never says where printed cards' unmarked decoys are or how many codes are real, so its tag check rejects every correct card set

- **Category:** DOC
- **Severity:** medium
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: The defect breaks no mandatory guarantee, legal obligation, main-path safety check or explicit release precondition. The main recovery path is the program. Its decode and restore read only the first N codes, and the printed cards keep the real codes intact as their numbered prefix. The heir sheet (heir-sheet-text.ts:142-156), README.md:123, docs/HEIRS.md:47 and docs/releases/v0.1.0.md:132 all state the first-N rule. The guide's step 1 already allows removing "cards explicitly identified as decoys", and the heir sheet's "the cards after them belong to nothing" provides that identification. The guide's colour check fails closed: none of the full printed sets I generated (5 lengths, k=1..16 decoys, 50 pools each) passed it. A Word Unicode list of wrong length is caught by the helper's BIP39 checksum (31/32 for 15 words) and by the section 8 address comparison. DOC-06 is checked by review and is not a release precondition. The manual guide is a fallback whose wording is incomplete, not a release gate.
- **Rules:** DOC-06, DOC-07
- **Related:** AUD-028-DOC001, AUD-021-FUN002, AUD-022-FUN003
- **Affected files and builds:** docs/MANUAL_RECOVERY.md:10-27 (the recovery pack keeps the mode, dates and passphrase but not the word or code count), :38-39 (the Word Unicode bullet has no decoy or count step), :49-51 (step 1: "real colors", remove only cards explicitly identified as decoys, do not guess), :55-58 (step 3: duplicate or missing tags mean the set is wrong); src/export/card-decoys.ts:1-8,33,82-91 (phraseColor takes a colour of a random phrase of the same length, so its tag is always a real order tag); src/export/card-plan.ts:41-64 and src/export/collection-layout.ts:502 (count starts at own+1 on sheets; separate cards keep exactly 1 decoy); src/export/card-codes.ts:62-69 (cards are numbered over all elements and never print the count); src/bundled-file-paths.ts:8 and src/cli/table-command.ts:33,75 (bundled and exported). Cross-check: README.md:123, docs/HEIRS.md:47 and src/export/heir-sheet-text.ts:142-156 do document the first-N rule.
- **Reproduction:** The repo HEAD is now 61e163b, two commits after f8d9a93. `git diff --quiet f8d9a93 HEAD -- docs/MANUAL_RECOVERY.md src/export docs/HEIRS.md src/cli src/bundled-file-paths.ts` returns 0. The only changed files are REMEDIATION.md, audits/README.md, package.json, vitest.config.ts and .git-blame-ignore-revs, and none of them mentions the guide or decoys. I read the guide, card-decoys.ts, card-plan.ts, card-codes.ts, backup-cards.ts, encoded-form.ts and heir-sheet-text.ts. Card forms are unicode (labels, word-code decoys), colors-unicode and colors; the colour forms print the RGB hex with no labels. I wrote an independent probe at /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC001-lens/lens.mjs. It implements guide RGB steps 1-4 from the guide text, not from program code, and applies them to dist/export/card-decoys.js output for 12/15/18/21/24 words (public abandon..about and abandon..art, counting indexes for 15/18/21), with k=1..16 kept decoys over 50 pools each. Results: the own set alone is ok for every length; 800/800 decoys per length carry a real order tag; zero full printed sets are accepted (each fails on count or duplicate tags); the first N printed codes decode correctly 800/800 for every length. For Word Unicode with 12 words, k decoys give 12+k codes, a valid phrase length at k=3, 6, 9 and 12, and every decoy is a 4-hex dictionary code. INDIVIDUAL_DECOYS=1. The first verifier's probes (r7/decoys.mjs, verify/AUD-037-DOC001/probe.mjs) were read and are sound. git log -S shows the step 1 decoy wording was added in 0d7898f (2026-10-15), after decoys arrived in 5cfb026 (2026-10-13). No AUD-001..036 finding or REMEDIATION.md entry concerns decoys in MANUAL_RECOVERY.md.
- **Expected behaviour:** The bundled "recovery without MnemoCode" guide should describe the cards the program prints (DOC-06). It should say that every printed set ends with at least one unmarked decoy: exactly one on separate cards, and enough to fill the rows on sheets. A colour decoy repeats a real order tag. The backup is the first N cards by printed number (8/10/12/14/16 colours, or the word count for Word Unicode). The guide should say how N is known (the heir sheet or a recorded word count), and the recovery pack should keep that count.
- **Observed behaviour:** The guide expects decoys ("real colors", "cards explicitly identified as decoys") but never says where they are or how to identify them. Its prepare-a-recovery-pack section never asks for the count. Step 3 rejects duplicate tags, and every colour decoy duplicates a real tag. The Word Unicode steps have no decoy step at all. Applied literally to a full printed set without an outside count, the guide rejects 100% of correct colour sets and turns Word Unicode decoys into extra words. The defect is narrower than the original claim: the guide is incomplete rather than unaware of decoys. With the heir sheet's "first N codes" statement it works as written, and the colour path fails closed.
- **Impact:** This affects an owner or heir who has the printed cards and the exported guide, but no working MnemoCode and no heir sheet. That is exactly the scenario the guide is for, and the guide tells them a correct card backup is incomplete or wrong. It forbids discarding the trailing cards. Word Unicode cards give 13+ words, or a wrong-length phrase that can pass BIP39 1/32 of the time for 15 words before the section 8 address check catches it. Recovery is blocked or delayed, not lost. The real codes are the numbered prefix, and the tag pattern itself reveals where the prefix ends. No secret is exposed and no wrong wallet is accepted silently if section 8 is followed. This is medium, not low: the defect is in the only software-independent recovery document, which ships inside the product, and it occurs on every printed card set, the main output of format 3/4/5.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R7; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json). A second, independent verifier checked severity and release blocking: agreed.
- **Recommended fix:** In docs/MANUAL_RECOVERY.md, add a printed-cards paragraph before step 1. It should say that printed sets end with unmarked decoys: one on separate cards, at least one on sheets. A colour decoy repeats a real order tag. The backup is the first N cards by printed card number: 8/10/12/14/16 colours for 12-24 words, or the word count for Word Unicode cards, as the heir sheet states ("the cards after them belong to nothing"). Discard the cards after the first N before sorting. Reword step 1 so that this rule is the explicit identification. Limit the duplicate-tag rule in step 3 to the remaining cards. Add the same first-N rule to the Word Unicode bullet. In "Prepare a recovery pack", tell the owner to keep the heir sheet or the word count with the cards. Keep the wording consistent with README.md:123, docs/HEIRS.md:47 and the heir-sheet text.
- **Required verification:** Re-read the revised guide against card-decoys.ts, card-plan.ts, collection-layout.ts, card-codes.ts and heir-sheet-text.ts. Rerun tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC001-lens/lens.mjs with the revised procedure (first N by printed number, then the tag checks). For all five lengths and k=1..16, every printed colour set should decode to the original numbers. For Word Unicode with 1, 3 and 12 decoys, exactly the original 12 codes should remain. Check that the guide, README.md:123, docs/HEIRS.md:47 and docs/releases/v0.1.0.md:132 describe the same rule. Run pnpm format:check and the docs checks after the edit.

##### AUD-037-DOC002 — Low — AUD-032/034/035 harness READMEs omit unpublished local inputs that their scripts require

- **Category:** DOC
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: The defect is confined to audit-support documentation and scripts. It does not change any product code path, secret handling, recovery result, release artifact or packaging gate. The workspace audit rule on rerunnable harnesses is not a stated release precondition.
- **Rules:** audits.instructions.md (harness README, rerunnable)
- **Related:** AUD-036-BLD003
- **Affected files and builds:** docs/audits/AUD-035-harnesses/README.md ("Prerequisites and inputs"); docs/audits/AUD-035-harnesses/inventory.mjs:12,41-47,76-78; AUD-035-harnesses/capture.mjs:70-94; coverage-v2.mjs:6-7; ledgers.mjs:44; date-preservation.mjs:6-7; structure.mjs:7; validate.py:7; docs/audits/AUD-034-harnesses/README.md; AUD-034-harnesses/inventory.mjs:12,41-86; capture.mjs:72-96; report.mjs:38; history.mjs:7 and validate.mjs:18 (embedded Python using jsonschema); docs/audits/AUD-032-harnesses/remediation.mjs:41 (not described in AUD-032-harnesses/README.md); docs/audits/AUD-035-remediation-harnesses/README.md (no commit); docs/audits/README.md (no AUD-033 row). Lower-weight context: AUD-029-harnesses/core-inventory.mjs:10, AUD-031-harnesses/inventory.mjs:100-103 and AUD-032-harnesses/contracts.mjs:14 use the scratch parser, but their READMEs declare it.
- **Reproduction:** All reads are at f8d9a93. The files involved are identical at the current HEAD 61e163b: between the two commits only REMEDIATION.md, the audit index, package.json, vitest.config.ts and .git-blame-ignore-revs changed.
(1) bash tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC002/inputs.sh greps the tracked AUD-029..036 harnesses for tmp/cancel-check and for other audits' -evidence paths, then counts how many files of each referenced input are published.
- It finds 52 references. Every input has 0 published files.
- The workspace repository's .gitignore rule "/*" ignores tmp/cancel-check, and its package.json pins only "typescript": "^5.9.3".
- AUD-033 has 0 report files and 0 index rows.
- There are 0 requirements files beside the AUD-034/035 jsonschema validators.
- The AUD-035 remediation README contains 0 commit-like hex strings.
(2) I extracted a clean tree with git archive f8d9a93 src scripts docs/audits/AUD-0{29,31,32,34,35}-harnesses sskr-wasm/integrity.json into tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC002/clone, then ran node docs/audits/AUD-029-harnesses/core-inventory.mjs. It printed "Error: Cannot find module '.../verify/AUD-037-DOC002/tmp/cancel-check/node_modules/typescript'" and exited 1.
- Control: with the existing scratch parser symlinked at tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC002/tmp/cancel-check, the same command exits 0 with parseDiagnostics 0. The unpublished parser folder is therefore the only missing input.
(3) In the same clone, with the parser linked and a stub of AUD-034's own snapshot.json in place, node docs/audits/AUD-034-harnesses/inventory.mjs printed "Error: ENOENT: no such file or directory, open 'docs/audits/AUD-033-evidence/remediation/initial-snapshot.json'" and exited 1.
(4) I read the READMEs.
- AUD-029, 030, 031 and 032 declare the scratch TypeScript 5.9.3 parser. AUD-031 also declares the AUD-030 snapshot, and AUD-030's inventory records a gap when the parser is absent.
- The AUD-034 README says only "already installed TypeScript 5.9 parser" and "admission of older semantic/canonical evidence".
- The "Prerequisites and inputs" section of the AUD-035 README says "All inputs are public fixtures" and lists "existing Python jsonschema" without a version.
- The AUD-032 README never mentions remediation.mjs.
- The AUD-035-remediation-harnesses README names no audited commit or base.
(5) The earlier records hold no tracked item on undeclared local harness inputs. AUD-036-BLD003 covered unpinned jsonschema for the AUD-036 validator only and said that "no wider audit of historical harnesses was performed". REMEDIATION.md records it as corrected through AUD-036-harnesses/requirements.md, which reuses the AUD-030 pins. AUD-008's README shows the expected practice: it names its maintainer-only evidence and says a reader cannot rerun those validators.
- **Expected behaviour:** The audit rule (audits.instructions.md) requires each harness folder's README to name its audit and reviewed commit and to list the inputs it needs, so that a reader can rerun the scripts. Where a script needs maintainer-only local evidence or a scratch tool, the README should name that input and say a reader cannot rerun the script, as the AUD-008 and AUD-031 READMEs do. Under TOOL-03, a Python validator should take third-party packages from an adjacent hash-pinned requirements file.
- **Observed behaviour:** Several AUD-032, AUD-034 and AUD-035 harness scripts fail on a clean tree because they read files that are never published. These are other audits' ignored evidence (AUD-032, AUD-033 and AUD-034) and the ignored workspace folder tmp/cancel-check, which holds a TypeScript parser installed from a caret range. AUD-033 has neither a published report nor an index row, so a reader cannot identify those inputs. The AUD-034 and AUD-035 READMEs do not list these inputs, and the AUD-035 README says all inputs are public fixtures. The AUD-032 README does not mention remediation.mjs, which needs AUD-033 evidence. The AUD-035 remediation README names no commit. The AUD-035 validate.py and the Python embedded in the AUD-034 validators import jsonschema without any pin. The AUD-029 to AUD-031 READMEs do declare their parser and snapshot inputs, so those parts of the original claim are overstated.
- **Impact:** A reader who follows the AUD-034 or AUD-035 instructions on a clean checkout hits MODULE_NOT_FOUND and ENOENT errors for inputs the README never mentions and cannot obtain. The published re-verification path for those audits' inventory, admission, coverage and ledger steps is therefore unavailable, and the README does not say so. The effect is limited to audit-support tooling. The reports still carry their hashes and results, and the AUD-035 product counterexample probes and recorder wrappers do not depend on these inputs. No product, secret, recovery or release artifact is affected.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R7; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Add a documentation-only addendum to the AUD-032, AUD-034 and AUD-035 harness READMEs. Leave the frozen scripts unchanged. The addendum should:
- name every other-audit evidence file the scripts read and the scratch parser path with its exact TypeScript version;
- state that these inputs are maintainer-only, and list which commands a reader can still rerun;
- correct the AUD-035 statement that all inputs are public fixtures;
- for jsonschema, point to the AUD-030 hash-pinned requirements file (or an adjacent requirements file).
Separately, name the base commit and source fingerprint in the AUD-035 remediation README. Also add an index row for AUD-033 marking it as local-only, or as a correction dossier without a report, so that references to it can be resolved.
- **Required verification:** Extract a clean tree of the release commit.
- Check that every corrected README lists each -evidence path and the tmp/cancel-check path that its scripts read. Rerunning the inputs.sh grep against the READMEs should find no undeclared reference.
- Run the self-contained commands each README marks as reader-runnable. They should run without those inputs.
- Confirm that the AUD-035 remediation README names a commit or fingerprint.
- Confirm that the AUD-034 and AUD-035 jsonschema usage points to a hash-pinned requirements file.
- Confirm that the audit index resolves AUD-033.

##### AUD-037-DOC003 — Low — docs/CLI.md wallet-check examples and date-search note use the refused --input and --dates options

- **Category:** DOC
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: The program refuses the raw options before it reads anything, and the error names the file or private-screen alternative. The documented file-based equivalent works, so no security, integrity or recovery guarantee is broken. No workspace rule makes runnable documentation examples a release precondition.
- **Rules:** DOC-06
- **Related:** AUD-022-DOC004, AUD-022-API002
- **Affected files and builds:** docs/CLI.md:178; docs/CLI.md:182-213 (lines 188-189, 196-197, 203-204, 209-210); context: docs/CLI.md:126, README.md:242 (links to the section), src/cli/arguments.ts:36-54, src/cli/command-line.ts:81. Minor wording only: src/cli/help-options.ts:139-140. Not a defect: CHANGELOG.md:199, docs/releases/v0.1.0.md:169.
- **Reproduction:** The cited files are unchanged between f8d9a93 and the working HEAD 61e163b (git diff --stat shows no change to them). A verbatim run of all four docs/CLI.md wallet-check examples against dist/mnemocode.js with the public STORED_PHRASE (/home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC003/run-examples.sh) printed "mnemocode: Secret material for --input must not be passed as a command-line value. Use --input-file PATH, --qr-file PATH or --ask-secrets." with exit=1 for each example. With --input-file stored.txt and the raw --dates "??-09-2026" kept, the command printed "Secret material for --dates must not be passed as a command-line value. Use --dates-file PATH or --ask-secrets." with exit=1. The refusal (PRIVATE_MATERIAL_FLAGS) came in with commit 4978a62. That commit rewrote the "Public command examples" in CLI.md to use files but left the wallet-check section unchanged. No test runs the CLI.md examples.
- **Expected behaviour:** Under DOC-06, documented commands use the options the program accepts. docs/CLI.md:126 itself says raw --input and --dates values are refused, so the wallet-check examples and the date-search note should use --input-file/--qr-file and --dates-file, or --ask-secrets.
- **Observed behaviour:** All four wallet-check examples pass the record with --input and the date pattern with --dates (8 refused uses), and each exits 1 at the argv refusal. CLI.md:178 says "Dates passed with `--dates` must be complete except in `recover-date`", naming an option that is always refused; the rule really applies to --dates-file. The CHANGELOG/v0.1.0 AUD-022 line is a historical remediation note. It is still true (raw --last-word is refused for a one-phrase backup), and CHANGELOG.md:26 in the same section states that all raw final-word arguments are refused, so it is not a mismatch. The --dates-file help prints "YYYY-MM-DD is also accepted" one paragraph before it names DD-MM-YYYY. This is an awkward order left over from moving the old --dates text, but the content is accurate.
- **Impact:** A person who follows README's "Reference options, supported coins and examples" link to this shipped guide (docs/*.md is in the npm package) gets a refusal on every wallet-check example. Someone who adapts an example with their own encoded record also puts that record and a date pattern into shell history before the refusal, which is the exposure the policy is meant to prevent. No secret is compromised by the program itself.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R7; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** In docs/CLI.md, rewrite the four wallet-check examples to write the public stored phrase and the date pattern to local files, as the "Public command examples" section already does, and pass them with --input-file and --dates-file. Change CLI.md:178 to say "Dates in --dates-file must be complete except in recover-date". Optionally reorder the --dates-file help details so the DD-MM-YYYY paragraph comes before "YYYY-MM-DD is also accepted". Leave the historical AUD-022 changelog line as it is.
- **Required verification:** Run each corrected wallet-check example verbatim against the built CLI with the public fixture and confirm exit 0 with the documented match. Example 1 should report 23-09-2026 at m/84'/0'/0'/0/0. Rerun the documentation option probe and confirm no refused material option appears in any documented invocation. Preferably add a test that extracts the CLI.md bash blocks and checks them against the argv policy.

##### AUD-037-DOC004 — Low — Help (encode and re-encode) and menu still say fewer SSKR shares reveal nothing (missed variant of AUD-030-DOC001)

- **Category:** DOC
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: The defect overstates what below-threshold shares hide, but it is wording only. Combining fewer shares than the threshold is still refused, and none of the hidden information is the phrase content. It breaks no mandatory security, integrity or recovery guarantee, no main-path safety check and no release-precondition rule. AUD-030 rated the same defect low and not blocking.
- **Rules:** DOC-07, AUD-030-DOC001
- **Related:** AUD-030-DOC001
- **Affected files and builds:** src/cli/help-options.ts:676 (--sskr details, shown by both `help encode` and `help re-encode`); src/cli/encode-questions.ts:126 (SHARES_EXPLANATION, shown with the menu's "Split it into Shamir shares?" question through askShareSet at encode-questions.ts:285). Built copies: dist/cli/help-options.js, dist/cli/encode-questions.js.
- **Reproduction:** Reading: git grep at f8d9a93 for absolute below-threshold wording outside docs/audits finds exactly these two strings: help-options.ts:676 "Fewer shares than --threshold reveal nothing about the phrase" and encode-questions.ts:126 "Fewer reveal nothing.". docs/SSKR.md:3 at f8d9a93 says fewer than N shares "still reveal the set identifier, group and member numbers, thresholds and phrase length". The current HEAD 61e163b changes no src file relative to f8d9a93. Runtime: `node dist/mnemocode.js help encode | grep -n -A1 'reveal nothing'` prints line 247 "--threshold reveal nothing about the phrase. ...". `help re-encode` prints the same text at line 332, and `help sskr-combine` does not contain it. Probe: /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC004/subquorum.mjs, run as `node subquorum.mjs <dist>`, log in subquorum.log. It splits the public 12-word and 24-word abandon phrases into 2-of-3 sets and reads one share with shareInfo. 12 words: {identifier:34561, groupThreshold:1, groupCount:1, groupIndex:0, memberThreshold:2, memberIndex:0, secretLength:16}. 24 words: secretLength 32. In both cases combineSskrShares([one share]) is refused with "Not enough SSKR shares to meet the recorded threshold." Source confirms that askShareSet shows SHARES_EXPLANATION with the Shares question. I did not drive the interactive menu in a PTY.
- **Expected behaviour:** Under DOC-07, and consistent with the AUD-030-DOC001 fix in docs/SSKR.md and docs/HEIRS.md, help and menu text should say only this: fewer shares than the threshold cannot restore the phrase, but each share still shows the set identifier, the thresholds and the phrase length.
- **Observed behaviour:** The help page for encode and re-encode says fewer shares than --threshold "reveal nothing about the phrase". The menu explanation says "Fewer reveal nothing." Yet one share below a 2-of-3 threshold exposes secretLength 16 or 32, which tells a 12-word phrase from a 24-word one, along with the set identifier, member index and threshold metadata.
- **Impact:** The help and menu promise more than the code delivers, so a user may think a single share is harmless and store it carelessly. The exposure is limited to metadata and phrase length. No phrase recovery, guessing attack or measurable loss of cryptographic strength is shown, and recovery below the threshold is correctly refused.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R7+R1; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Change both strings to match docs/SSKR.md. For example, help: "Fewer shares than --threshold cannot restore the phrase, but each share still shows the set identifier, thresholds and phrase length; keep every share private." Menu: "Fewer cannot restore it, but still show its length." Then search all user-facing text (src/cli, web pages, docs) for any remaining absolute below-threshold claims.
- **Required verification:** Rebuild, then check that `help encode`, `help re-encode` and the menu's Shares explanation no longer claim that fewer shares reveal nothing. Check that the new wording matches the shareInfo fields decoded from a single public-vector share, and that combining a single share is still refused. Add a regression test that rejects "reveal nothing" in SSKR help and menu text, and grep the whole user-facing tree for the same wording.

##### AUD-037-DOC005 — Low — help cards says separate cards end with as many decoys as the sheet (missed variant of AUD-028-DOC001)

- **Category:** DOC
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: This is a help-text mismatch only. The cards, the QR payload, the sheet for heirs and decode --words do not depend on it, so no mandatory integrity, isolation or recovery guarantee and no documented safety check fails.
- **Rules:** DOC-06
- **Related:** AUD-028-DOC001
- **Affected files and builds:** src/cli/help-pages.ts:755 ("help cards", section "What a card holds:"); contract side: src/export/card-decoys.ts:1-8,33,136-139; src/export/card-plan.ts:1-4,43-49; src/export/render.ts:70-79; docs/card-designs.md:10; README.md:133; CHANGELOG.md:162; docs/releases/v0.1.0.md:132; test/individual-card-plan.test.ts:115-160
- **Reproduction:** I checked that src/cli/help-pages.ts, src/export/card-decoys.ts, card-plan.ts and render.ts are the same at f8d9a93 and at the current checkout. The only changes after f8d9a93 are in REMEDIATION, the audits README, package.json and vitest.config.ts. (1) `node dist/mnemocode.js help cards` saved to /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC005/help-cards.txt prints: "A set ends with a few random codes that belong to nothing, at least one, so that every row of a sheet is full and the cards are as large as the page allows; separate cards end with as many." (2) `timeout 60 node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC005/plan.mjs` plans zero-entropy public phrases with plannedCardJob and separateCardJob from dist, using the business-it design. Output is saved to plan.out. For 24-word Unicode, the a6/a5/a4 sheet has 28 cards with 4 decoys; separate cards (business size, or the same sheet plan converted) have 25 cards with 1 decoy. For 12-word Unicode the sheet has 15 cards with 3 decoys and separate cards have 13 with 1. For 24-word colours the sheet has 18 cards with 2 decoys and separate cards have 17 with 1. INDIVIDUAL_DECOYS = 1. (3) `git log -S` shows the help sentence came in with 5cfb026 (2026-10-13). The one-decoy rule INDIVIDUAL_DECOYS came in with 0d7898f (2026-10-15), and the help text was not updated with it. (4) Audit history: AUD-028-DOC001 ("Curated release notes retain the obsolete separate-card decoy count", docs/releases/v0.1.0.md) is recorded as fixed in AUD-028, where only the release notes were changed. No AUD-001..036 record or docs/REMEDIATION.md entry tracks help-pages.ts:755 as open. The only other match is an untracked, git-ignored AUD-036 evidence worklist that copies this AUD-037 candidate as "not-undertaken", which is not a tracked finding. A git grep of the tracked tree finds no other text with the stale claim.
- **Expected behaviour:** "mnemocode help cards" should say that a sheet ends with at least one decoy and then as many as fill its rows, and that separate cards end with exactly one decoy after the real codes. That is what the code (INDIVIDUAL_DECOYS = 1, keptIndividualCards), its header comment, docs/card-designs.md, README.md, CHANGELOG.md, the curated release notes and the regression tests say.
- **Observed behaviour:** The help says separate cards "end with as many" random codes as a set or sheet. The program always gives separate cards exactly one decoy: for a 24-word Unicode backup the sheet has 28 cards (4 decoys) but separate cards have 25 (1 decoy), and separate cards made from a sheet plan are cut down to one decoy.
- **Impact:** The built-in help gives a wrong card and decoy count for separate business cards. Someone checking a printed set against the help may expect more cards than were made. Recovery is not affected: the QR code and the sheet for heirs carry only the real codes or the word count, and decode --words reads only those codes. This is the same stale claim that AUD-028-DOC001 fixed in the release notes, missed in the CLI help, against DOC-06.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R7; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Change the sentence in src/cli/help-pages.ts so it says that separate cards end with exactly one decoy, for example "separate cards end with exactly one, after all real codes". Rebuild dist so `help cards` shows the new text. Consider a help-text assertion so the wording cannot drift from INDIVIDUAL_DECOYS again.
- **Required verification:** After the fix and rebuild, `node dist/mnemocode.js help cards` should state exactly one decoy for separate cards and no longer contain "separate cards end with as many". Rerun test/individual-card-plan.test.ts and the help or documentation consistency tests. A git grep should find no remaining text that gives separate cards the sheet's decoy count.

##### AUD-037-DOC006 — Low — Release notes promise unqualified memory wiping of QR images and saved data, and unconditional owner-only export folders

- **Category:** DOC
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: This is a documentation overstatement. The wipe and mode behaviour it describes is implemented as far as the runtime allows. SECURITY.md, the normative security policy, already qualifies erasure (line 41) and owner-only access (line 47). No mandatory security, integrity or recovery guarantee is broken, and no release-precondition rule is violated. The fix is release-note wording.
- **Rules:** DOC-07, TS-07
- **Related:** AUD-018-SEC001, AUD-005-SEC001
- **Affected files and builds:** CHANGELOG.md:117,122,123,139; docs/releases/v0.1.0.md:87,92,93,109; src/export/private-file.ts:23-41 (doc comments); supporting code: src/cli/qr-input.ts:16-22, src/cli/encode-export.ts:156, src/cli/sskr-output.ts:205-208. Removed from the proposal: CHANGELOG.md:30, CHANGELOG.md:129, docs/releases/v0.1.0.md:99, src/cli/help-options.ts:108, SECURITY.md:40 and the terminal-choice.ts scrollback citation.
- **Reproduction:** All cited files are identical at f8d9a93 and at the working HEAD 61e163b (git diff f8d9a93 61e163b shows no change in them); I read them with git show f8d9a93:<path>.

Runtime probe: /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC006/qr-copies.mjs, run with `cd /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC006 && timeout 30 node qr-copies.mjs`. It writes a QR PNG of a public MNC1 record of the 'abandon … about' phrase, then wraps pngjs 7.0.0's lib/filter-parse-sync.process to keep references to its inflated input and unfiltered output. Next it calls decodeQrPngFile from the HEAD-built dist/cli/qr-input.js, and finally compares the unfiltered copy with a fresh decode. Output (exit 0):
- "decoded equals public text: true | typeof result: string"
- "pngjs inflated: 179988 bytes, 3059 nonzero after decodeQrPngFile returned"
- "pngjs unfiltered: 179776 bytes, 130048 nonzero after decodeQrPngFile returned"
- "unfiltered copy byte-identical to the decoded pixels: true"

Source reading:
- qr-input.ts:16-22 states that pngjs's compressed, inflated and unfiltered buffers, jsQR's working copies and the returned text cannot be wiped.
- encode-export.ts:156 builds the saved record from the JS string backup.record(), and sskr-output.ts:207 builds the share file from lines.join(); only the derived byte buffers are wiped.
- private-file.ts relies only on POSIX modes 0o600/0o700 (writeFile mode, mkdir mode, chmod), with no ACL handling (git grep for icacls/ACL in src/export finds nothing). Node's fs documentation (@types/node fs.d.ts:2577) says that on Windows only the write permission can be manipulated. Release notes v0.1.0.md:16 confirm that a Windows executable ships.
- SECURITY.md:41 ('as far as it can … text in JavaScript memory cannot be erased') and SECURITY.md:47 ('where the system supports this') are qualified. CHANGELOG.md:29 qualifies only retained results, wallet checks, dates and card plans. docs/releases/v0.1.0.md contains no erasure qualifier at all.

Refuted sub-claims:
- terminal-choice.ts:220-223 and 260-267 describe the menu's answer record, which menu.ts:711-738 runs on the main screen and which holds no secrets. Secret commands run inside withPrivateScreen (private-screen.ts:80-111) on the alternate screen (?1049h). The scrollback admission therefore does not contradict help-options.ts:108, SECURITY.md:40 or CHANGELOG.md:129. Behaviour of individual terminal emulators was not reproducible here.
- For CHANGELOG.md:30, the engine's zeroize/memzero use (sskr-wasm/rust/src/lib.rs, vendored sskr and bc-shamir) and the glue's fill(0) support the claim; the candidate offered no counter-evidence.

Duplicate search: grep of docs/audits/*.md, the JSON records and docs/REMEDIATION.md for these phrases, DOC-07 findings and the file names finds no open or fixed item covering these overclaims.
- **Expected behaviour:** DOC-07 says release notes and help claim no stronger guarantee than the code provides. TS-07 says texts never present wipe() as a guaranteed erasure and that JavaScript strings cannot be erased. The changelog and release notes should qualify memory clearing as best-effort, limited to MnemoCode-owned byte buffers. They should state owner-only access only where POSIX modes apply, as SECURITY.md already does.
- **Observed behaviour:** CHANGELOG.md:117 and v0.1.0.md:87 state without qualification that MnemoCode clears its temporary copies of the phrase data after use.

CHANGELOG.md:122 and v0.1.0.md:92 say a QR input image 'is wiped from memory … both the file's bytes and its decoded pixels'. In fact pngjs keeps an unfiltered buffer that is byte-identical to the decoded pixels, plus an inflated copy, and both stay nonzero after decodeQrPngFile returns. The decoded text is a string.

CHANGELOG.md:123 and v0.1.0.md:93 say the saved record and share text are 'wiped from memory'. Only the derived byte buffers are wiped; their source strings are not.

CHANGELOG.md:139 and v0.1.0.md:109 say the share-card folder 'can be read only by your user account, like the other export folders' without condition. The code only sets POSIX modes, which Windows does not apply beyond the write bit and FAT/exFAT mounts ignore. The private-file.ts doc comments repeat the unconditional owner-only claim.

The scrollback and native-engine parts of the proposal are not supported.
- **Impact:** Readers of the release text could believe that QR images, records and shares leave no copy in process memory, or that exports on Windows or removable FAT media are private to their account. They might then skip the precautions SECURITY.md recommends (offline live system, encrypted swap, folder permissions). There is no direct disclosure: the code wipes what it owns, and the normative security policy is correctly qualified. The impact is limited to misleading documentation.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R7+R1; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Reword CHANGELOG.md:117,122,123 and the matching release-note lines 87, 92 and 93 to the SECURITY.md wording. For example: MnemoCode wipes its own byte copies after use, as far as the runtime allows. Copies held by libraries (pngjs, jsQR) and JavaScript strings, such as the record text and the decoded QR text, cannot be erased.

Qualify CHANGELOG.md:139 and v0.1.0.md:109 as 'on Linux and macOS file systems that keep Unix permissions'. State that Windows ACLs and FAT/exFAT media are not restricted by MnemoCode. Optionally make SECURITY.md:47 name the same exceptions instead of 'where the system supports this'.

Qualify the owner-only doc comments in src/export/private-file.ts the same way.
- **Required verification:** Re-read the reworded CHANGELOG.md and docs/releases/v0.1.0.md lines. Confirm that no unqualified 'wiped from memory', 'clears its temporary copies' or unconditional 'only by your user account' claim remains in either file or in the private-file.ts comments. Run `git grep -n -i -E "wiped from memory|clears its temporary copies|only by your user account"` over CHANGELOG.md, docs/releases/ and src/, and check that each hit carries a qualifier consistent with SECURITY.md:41,47 and TS-07. Rerun pnpm format:check and pnpm check.

##### AUD-037-DOC007 — Low — README claims the private screen accepts the BIP39 passphrase; only --bip39-passphrase-file exists

- **Category:** DOC
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: This is a documentation inaccuracy. The supported input, --bip39-passphrase-file, works, and the same README sentence, docs/CLI.md:223 and --help all name it. No mandatory security, integrity or recovery guarantee is broken, no legal distribution obligation is affected, and no documented safety check is bypassed.
- **Rules:** DOC-07, DOC-06
- **Related:** AUD-014 (BIP39 passphrase file help wording; different defect), AUD-033-UI002 (candidate-list passphrase prompt role; different defect)
- **Affected files and builds:** README.md:242; src/cli/bitcoin-options.ts:218-223 (bip39Passphrase reads only --bip39-passphrase-file); src/cli/decode-command.ts:258-267 (comparedPassphrase: the private screen compares "without a BIP39 passphrase" or with that of the file); src/cli/menu.ts:417-427 (the only passphrase question in the menu is for candidate-list encryption, not BIP39)
- **Reproduction:** Checked at f8d9a93. The working tree is at 61e163b, but README.md and src/ are identical between the two commits: git diff --stat f8d9a93 HEAD touches only REMEDIATION, the audits README, package.json, vitest.config.ts and .git-blame-ignore-revs.

Source checks:
- `git show f8d9a93:README.md` line 242 reads "supply it through the private screen or `--bip39-passphrase-file`".
- `git grep -n -i "bip39.passphrase|BIP39 passphrase" f8d9a93 -- src`: the only BIP39 passphrase input is bip39Passphrase() in bitcoin-options.ts:218, which reads the file option or returns "".
- No askSecret or askSecretUntil call anywhere asks for it.
- The menu's "A passphrase" (menu.ts:422) is the candidate-list encryption passphrase.

Probe: sh /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC007/probe.sh. Output is saved in probe-output.txt beside it.
- Grepping the built dist/ for secret prompts that mention BIP39 found only "Seed phrase (English BIP39 words):".
- The full list of askSecretUntil prompts holds only the encoded phrase, shares, backup, dates, last word, seed phrase and the two candidate-list passphrase prompts.
- The private-screen wording in dist is only "compares it without a BIP39 passphrase." / "compares it with the BIP39 passphrase of --bip39-passphrase-file.".
- `decode --help` and `recover-date --help` list only --bip39-passphrase-file ("The BIP39 passphrase of the wallet, if it has one; the file is secret.").

History:
- git blame dates README:242 to 0d7898f (2026-10-15, a README condensing pass).
- `git log -S`/`-G` finds no commit that ever added a BIP39 passphrase prompt. The only passphrase prompts in history are the candidate-list ones.

Earlier records: grepping docs/audits/AUD-0*.md/json and docs/REMEDIATION.md for "through the private screen", "README.md:24x" and BIP39 passphrase finds no earlier finding on this sentence. Related but different items are AUD-008-API005, AUD-014 (help wording for the passphrase file), AUD-026-SEC002 and AUD-033-UI002 (list passphrase prompt role).
- **Expected behaviour:** README describes only the BIP39 passphrase inputs that exist. Per DOC-06, code and documents agree.
- **Observed behaviour:** README.md:242 offers two alternatives: "the private screen or --bip39-passphrase-file". Neither the private screen nor the menu ever asks for a BIP39 passphrase; --bip39-passphrase-file is the only input. It can be combined with --ask-secrets, and decode accepts it only there. When no file is given, the private screen compares wallets with an empty passphrase. Decode and re-encode say so in their wallet question; recover-date, sskr-restore and the menu do not.
- **Impact:** A user whose wallet has a BIP39 passphrase may follow the README into the menu or private screen and wait for a passphrase prompt that never comes. Every wallet check then runs with an empty passphrase, so the right candidate is reported as not matching, which can be misread as wrong dates or codes. No secret is exposed, the documented file option works, and the same sentence names it, so the practical impact is bounded.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R1; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Reword README.md:242. Suggested text: "If the wallet uses a BIP39 passphrase, give it with `--bip39-passphrase-file` (also together with `--ask-secrets`); the private screen and the menu do not ask for it, and without the file wallet checks use an empty passphrase." Alternatively, if a private-screen prompt is intended, implement it as a secret question and document it in docs/CLI.md and --help.
- **Required verification:** Grep README.md and docs/ for any remaining claim that the private screen or the menu takes the BIP39 passphrase. Confirm the new wording matches bip39Passphrase() and comparedPassphrase(). Run pnpm format:check and any documentation or help consistency tests.

##### AUD-037-DOC008 — Low — The --ask-secrets help and release notes say Escape does nothing while an answer is typed, but at visible values it goes back and drops the typed text

- **Category:** DOC
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: The defect is wrong wording about a keyboard convenience. Only non-secret visible values are dropped: fingerprints, addresses, file names and address counts. The phrase, dates and other secrets keep their text, and the earlier answers are kept. No security, integrity or recovery guarantee and no documented safety check is affected, so shipping 0.1.0 with it breaks no release precondition.
- **Rules:** DOC-06, DOC-07
- **Affected files and builds:** src/cli/help-options.ts:109 (--ask-secrets help, shipped as dist/cli/help-options.js:69); CHANGELOG.md:127 and docs/releases/v0.1.0.md:97 (the same claim in general form: "Escape does nothing while an answer is typed"); behaviour in src/cli/ask.ts:296-305 (askValueUntil passes back: true and leave: "back"), src/cli/ask.ts:250-258 (turnOfReading), src/cli/ask.ts:156-190 (readAnswer) and src/cli/terminal-input.ts:585-591 (readLine returns undefined at Escape when escapeGoesBack, whatever was typed). Callers that take visible values: ask.ts:608 (wallet fingerprint, address and address count on the private screen), backup-questions.ts:234, missing-codes-search.ts:202, save-retry.ts:69 and share-repair.ts:279.
- **Reproduction:** Read the files with git show f8d9a93:<path>. src/cli and scripts are the same at the checked-out HEAD 61e163b, and dist/ contains the same help text and the same back: true path. Command run: python3 /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC008/probe_escape_value.py. It drives dist/mnemocode.js in a PTY through scripts/terminal_session.py with `decode --ask-secrets --mode seedshift`, the public README masked example and the public fingerprint 73c5da0a. Output: "[secret] Escape inside typed dates kept the text: dates accepted, wallet list shown". Next, "[value] Escape with '73c5' typed went back to the wallet list". The fingerprint prompt shows no key hint mentioning Escape; only the earlier list shows "Esc goes back". Choosing Fingerprint again and typing 'da0a'+Enter gives "A master fingerprint must be eight hexadecimal characters.", which proves the typed '73c5' was dropped. The run ended with exit 130 after Ctrl+C. R4's probe (/home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/r4/probe_escape_value.py) also passes ("Escape in the typed fingerprint went back to the wallet list; typed text dropped.", exit 130), but it prints the drop without checking it; the verify probe checks it. From the source (not run): withAddressCount (ask.ts:576-588) returns undefined when Escape is pressed at the address count. askWalletEvidence then shows the wallet list again, so an address that was already accepted is dropped too.
- **Expected behaviour:** The --ask-secrets help, CHANGELOG and v0.1.0 release notes should describe Escape as it behaves. It does nothing in a secret answer. At a visible value (fingerprint, address, address count or file name) it goes back to the question before and drops the value typed there. Otherwise the code should match the documented promise that a slip loses nothing.
- **Observed behaviour:** The help says: "While an answer is typed, Escape does nothing, so that a slip loses nothing; in a list of choices it does what the key hint says." The CHANGELOG and release notes say: "Escape does nothing while an answer is typed." On the private screen, Escape at a visible-value prompt goes straight back, even with text typed, and that text is lost. Escape at the address count also drops the address already accepted. These prompts are not lists of choices and show no key hint. Secret answers do ignore Escape as documented. The comment at the top of ask.ts documents the go-back behaviour for values as intended, so the user-facing text is the side that is wrong.
- **Impact:** A user who trusts the help can press Escape by mistake at a fingerprint, address, address-count or file-name prompt. They are taken back one step and must type that value again, plus the address when Escape is pressed at the address count. No secret is exposed or lost, the phrase, dates and other earlier answers are kept, and Ctrl+C behaves as documented. The impact is a limited documentation inaccuracy (DOC-06 mismatch; DOC-07 promises more than the code does).
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R4; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Settle the mismatch under DOC-06 in favour of the documented code behaviour in ask.ts. Reword help-options.ts:109, CHANGELOG.md:127 and docs/releases/v0.1.0.md:97. Suggested wording: while a secret is typed, Escape does nothing; at a visible value such as a fingerprint, an address or a file name, Escape goes back to the question before it and what was typed there is dropped; in a list of choices it does what the key hint says. Alternatively, if the promise is the intended behaviour, make askValueUntil ignore Escape once text has been typed, or show a key hint at value prompts. Then update the ask.ts header and the PTY checks to match.
- **Required verification:** Check that the rendered `mnemocode decode --help` (and the other commands that show --ask-secrets) and the release notes describe Escape the same way as the code. Add a PTY check to scripts/terminal_secret_checks.py for the documented behaviour: Escape with text typed in a secret keeps the text, and Escape with text typed at a visible value (fingerprint or file name) does whatever the new text says. Rerun the help snapshot tests and verify-terminal-input.py.

##### AUD-037-DOC009 — Low — User-facing help, messages and documents describe the program's development (approval, older names, old records, remediation)

- **Category:** DOC
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: DOC-03 is a review-checked writing rule, not a stated precondition of a release. The wording breaks no security, integrity, recovery, legal or safety-check guarantee, and every command still behaves correctly.
- **Rules:** DOC-03
- **Affected files and builds:** src/export/templates.ts:135; src/export/cards-self-test.ts:59; src/cli/preview-command.ts:54-56; src/export/render.ts:25,91 (public via mnemocode/cards renderCards); src/cli/help-share-pages.ts:200-202; docs/SSKR.md:29; README.md:94; docs/CLI.md:108,126; docs/ROADMAP.md:28 (the ROADMAP claim was missing from the proposed locations)
- **Reproduction:** Read the cited lines at f8d9a93. HEAD is now 61e163b, but the two later commits change only docs/REMEDIATION.md, docs/audits/README.md, package.json, vitest.config.ts and .git-blame-ignore-revs, so every cited file is identical. Probes are in tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC009/ and ran against dist built from f8d9a93.
(1) `node dist/mnemocode.js preview --list` (preview-list.txt): row 8 prints "business-mixed  Mixed design card collection  One identity across all approved designs; A6, A5 or A4 sheets or individual cards."; exit 0.
(2) `node dist/mnemocode.js sskr-split --help` (sskr-split-help.txt) prints "Older name for encode --sskr" and "Kept for compatibility. It splits the phrase like encode --sskr ...".
(3) dist/export/cards-self-test.js:47 holds detail "approved templates, fonts, artwork and single-page A6 PDF". src/cli/self-test.ts:201,204 adds cardExportCheck to the CLI self-test and prints row.detail; the AUD-021 transcript (md:602) shows that line printed.
(4) `node empty-jobs.mjs <repo>` (empty-jobs.txt) calls the public renderCards with an empty job list: it prints "installed templates: 13" and then "code: export.render.no-approved-card-templates-installed-yet | message: No approved card templates are installed yet."
(5) preview-command.ts:55 ("... Each design will be added after individual approval.") runs only when the frozen 13-entry catalog is empty, so the shipped CLI never shows it.
(6) README.md:94 ("old Private Use symbol records remain readable") and docs/CLI.md:108 ("older symbols are also accepted"): src/core/colors.ts colorsToUnicode writes only hex digits, and colorUnicodePoints also accepts the raw Private Use characters. Color Unicode is described as MnemoCode's own format, and package.json is 0.1.0 with CHANGELOG "0.1.0 (unreleased)", so "old" can only mean pre-release builds.
(7) docs/CLI.md:126: "Help identifies these old forms as refused".
(8) docs/ROADMAP.md:28: "These applications are not part of the current TypeScript remediation."
(9) A grep of docs/audits and docs/REMEDIATION.md (f8d9a93 and HEAD) for these phrases and for DOC-03 findings returns only rule-coverage rows and an AUD-021 self-test transcript line. No finding was recorded for this.
- **Expected behaviour:** DOC-03 (docs.instructions.md; the writing guide covers help and messages) requires text written for the program's reader that leaves out how the program was developed. It names "approved templates" as an example of forbidden wording. A first release should describe designs, aliases and accepted input forms as they are now, without approval steps, earlier names, older records or remediation phases.
- **Observed behaviour:** Several user-facing texts describe the development process:
- `preview --list` prints "approved designs".
- The self-test prints "approved templates".
- The public renderCards API refuses an empty job list with "No approved card templates are installed yet." even though 13 templates are installed. The text is wrong for the real cause as well as off-rule.
- The `sskr-split` help says "Older name" and "Kept for compatibility", and docs/SSKR.md says "an older name", in a release that has no earlier version.
- README and docs/CLI.md call raw-symbol Color Unicode input "old ... records" and "older symbols", and docs/CLI.md calls the refused raw arguments "these old forms".
- docs/ROADMAP.md refers to "the current TypeScript remediation".
The preview-command empty-catalog message cannot be reached in the shipped CLI.
- **Impact:** Presentation only, with bounded effect:
- Readers see development-process vocabulary they cannot interpret, such as approval steps, older names, old records and remediation.
- The "older symbols" wording does not say what input is actually accepted (the raw Private Use characters).
- A library caller who passes no jobs gets a misleading reason for the refusal.
No secret, integrity or recovery behaviour is affected.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R7; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** - Remove "approved" from the template description, the self-test detail and both empty-catalog messages.
- For renderCards with no jobs, give the real cause (for example "No cards to render.") under a stable refusal code.
- Describe `sskr-split` as an alias of `encode --sskr` whose `--format` selects the share format, without "older name" or "kept for compatibility".
- Say directly that Color Unicode also accepts the Private Use characters themselves.
- Describe the refused raw arguments without "old forms".
- Replace "current TypeScript remediation" in ROADMAP with a reader-facing scope statement, for example "the current command-line release".
- Update the tests that assert these strings.
- **Required verification:** - Grep src, README.md and docs (audits excluded) for "approved", "older name", "kept for compatibility", "older symbols", "old Private Use", "old forms" and "remediation" in user-facing strings, and confirm none remain.
- Rerun `preview --list`, `sskr-split --help` and the self-test card row, and confirm the new wording.
- Call renderCards with an empty job list and confirm a refusal that states the real cause.
- pnpm check, pnpm format:check and the affected help, preview and render tests pass.

##### AUD-037-DOC010 — Low — The 0.1.0 changelog and release notes file user additions among test and audit lines, bundle unrelated changes, and the release notes omit later user additions

- **Category:** DOC
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: This is a presentation and completeness defect in the release text. The release notes exist as the tag workflow requires, and no reviewed line makes a false safety, integrity or recovery claim. No mandatory guarantee, legal obligation or explicit release precondition is broken. It should still be corrected before the draft release text is published.
- **Rules:** CHECK-DOC-001, DOC-03
- **Related:** AUD-019-DOC001, AUD-028-DOC001, AUD-004-API002
- **Affected files and builds:** CHANGELOG.md:5-11 (unheaded 0.1.0 list), CHANGELOG.md:13-39 (Audit follow-up; line 37 names SSKR bridge 0.1.7), CHANGELOG.md:191-199 (Audit remediation); docs/releases/v0.1.0.md:5-12 (Audit follow-up; line 12), docs/releases/v0.1.0.md:161-169 (Audit remediation); sskr-wasm/rust/Cargo.toml:3 (version 0.1.8); sskr-wasm/NOTICE.md:7 (0.1.8)
- **Reproduction:** Read CHANGELOG.md, docs/releases/v0.1.0.md and sskr-wasm/rust/Cargo.toml with `git show f8d9a93:<path>`. `git diff f8d9a93 HEAD` shows these three files unchanged at the current HEAD 61e163b. Ran `python3 -I /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC010/probe.py <mnemocode repo> f8d9a93` (output in the same folder as probe.out). It reported: no local tags; Cargo.toml version 0.1.8; CHANGELOG:37 says "bridge 0.1.7"; the release notes never mention the bridge. The release notes lack seven user changes that CHANGELOG has: --recovery-guide, --word-lists-dir, --table-format (the table/lookup exports), "Enter repeats", "local text file path", "multiline paste" and "original wallet phrase beside shares". Test and audit wording in user bullets: CHANGELOG:10 and 15 ("terminal regressions ..."), 27 ("Integrity regressions use declared child-only read faults"), 28, 33 ("Terminal tests close their PTY descriptors"), 34 ("stale regression contracts", "Mixed-set tests"), 38 ("Corrected regression/mutation fixtures and added 47 focused tests"), and 32 and 35 ("Fix AUD-028", "Fixed the 22 AUD-026 findings"). In the release notes, line 12 ends a user bullet with "Integrity tests inject declared child-only read faults". The probe's 'test' hits on self-test lines were excluded by hand. `git blame` shows CHANGELOG lines 6-11 came from 0d7898f (2026-10-15), but the release notes' Audit follow-up section (4978a62, 7facfcc) carries only line 5. One `git ls-remote --tags origin` was issued by mistake. It returned no output and nothing here relies on it.
- **Expected behaviour:** The audit standard's CHECK-DOC-001 sets the rule. Release notes keep user additions apart from developer, refactoring and fix changes, put each distinct change on its own line, and do not treat a source version as a published release. DOC-03 says text for readers leaves out how the program was developed. mnemocode/AGENTS.md says the release notes are written from the version's CHANGELOG section. AUD-019-DOC001 expects the release notes to carry the release's changes.
- **Observed behaviour:** In the 0.1.0 CHANGELOG, user-visible additions sit under an "Audit follow-up" heading. Among them are new options (--dates-file, --old-dates-file, --last-word-file) and the refusal of raw phrase arguments. They share that heading with lines that only describe tests, fixtures, mutation work, PTY cleanup and audit IDs. Several bullets bundle unrelated changes; line 31, for example, covers terminal byte storage, artwork scratch, font errors and package contents. Many lines are deltas against development states that were never released ("no longer", "Fix AUD-028", "Fixed the 22 AUD-026 findings"), although no tag exists and the notes call this the first release. The release notes leave out the table/lookup exports and their table options, Enter-to-repeat lookup, file paths in restore prompts, the multiline-paste decode fix, and Encode's offer of the original phrase beside shares. They also end a user bullet with a sentence about integrity tests. CHANGELOG:37 names an intermediate internal bridge version 0.1.7, but the release ships 0.1.8, and nothing in the 0.1.0 text says 0.1.8. Corrections to the proposal: the --format 6 to 4 renumbering notice was kept on purpose on AUD-004-API002's recommendation and is not a defect. The 0.1.7 label is historically true for the commit that introduced it, so it is an unexplained intermediate version rather than a false current claim. Most audit lines are under their own "Audit remediation" heading; the mixing defect is in the "Audit follow-up" sections and the unheaded top list.
- **Impact:** Users reading the draft GitHub release do not learn about several new features, such as the recovery-guide and word-list exports and file-path input in restore prompts. A new option and a breaking refusal of raw phrase arguments appear in an audit section next to test-maintenance lines, so they are harder to find. The bridge version in the text does not match the shipped engine. The impact is bounded: the release notes link to the full CHANGELOG, and no safety or recovery statement was found to be wrong.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R7; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Restructure the 0.1.0 section as the first release. List user-visible features and behaviour, including the options and the refusal now under Audit follow-up, in the user sections, with one change per line. Move or drop the test, fixture, mutation, PTY and audit-ID lines into a clearly separate developer or audit section, or leave them out of the release notes. Reword fixes of never-released behaviour as plain descriptions of current behaviour, but keep the deliberate --format renumbering notice. Add the missing user additions from CHANGELOG lines 6-11 to the release notes. Either drop the internal bridge version or state that the release ships SSKR bridge 0.1.8, without rewriting historical audit records.
- **Required verification:** Read the revised CHANGELOG 0.1.0 section and docs/releases/v0.1.0.md. User sections should contain no test, fixture or audit-ID lines, and each bullet should carry one logical change. Every user addition in the CHANGELOG should appear in the release notes or be covered by an explicit summary line. Any bridge version named should match sskr-wasm/rust/Cargo.toml. Then run pnpm check (Markdown list rules) and pnpm format:check.

- **Rules:** DOC-06
- **Related:** AUD-030-DOC002
- **Affected files and builds:** docs/HEIRS.md:33; docs/RELEASING.md:5-17,88; docs/card-identities.md:23; README.md:8,36-38,114-145; same-cause variants: .github/workflows/executable.yml:128 (comment), docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:23, src/export/heir-sheet-text.ts:202 (comment), and more loosely docs/SSKR.md:88 and docs/card-designs.md:3
- **Reproduction:** I read the cited lines at f8d9a93. git diff f8d9a93 HEAD shows README.md, docs/HEIRS.md, docs/RELEASING.md, docs/card-identities.md and docs/CLI.md unchanged since then. git history shows commit 0d7898f cut README.md from 708 to 290 lines; AUD-026 records the cut. The old README held the gpg/sha256sum/gh attestation steps under 'One executable file' and listed --card-name ... --card-location and --card-slogan/--card-subtitle/--card-footer/--card-reference-label. All of these moved to docs/CLI.md (Download verification, Card options). Probe: `python3 -I /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC011/probe.py <repo> f8d9a933ba9bde8290da39d42fa3a380de78385a`. Output is in /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC011/probe-output.txt. Results: HEIRS.md -> README#one-executable-file: anchor exists, present=[], missing=['gpg --verify','sha256sum --check'], all found in docs/CLI.md. RELEASING.md 'README shows the same steps': none of gpg --show-keys/--verify, sha256sum --check or gh attestation verify appear anywhere in README; all are in docs/CLI.md. card-identities.md -> README#cards-and-qr-codes: none of the six --card-* identity options. card-identities.md -> README#collection-sheet-identity: none of the four sheet-text options; all are in docs/CLI.md. executable.yml:128 '(README)': gh attestation verify is not in README. The README line 8 text is 'This README describes version 0.1.0, which has not been released.' RELEASING.md never mentions it, and git grep finds no script or test that checks it. RELEASING.md mentions Windows and macOS (true) but not browser, multi-chain or regroup (all false). One loose end: the probe's 'mod' check for ARCHITECTURE.md is a false positive on the docs/CLI.md side. The modulus explanation now lives in SEEDSHIFT_CONSTRUCTION.md, and the README section really does lack it.
- **Expected behaviour:** Each cross-reference should send readers to the place that holds the content it describes. Under DOC-06, when the README was shortened, the documents that cite its sections should have been updated in the same change. The release procedure should list every versioned statement that must change at release, including README line 8.
- **Observed behaviour:** Three documents send readers to the README for content that now lives only in docs/CLI.md. HEIRS.md:33 says the README 'shows how to check the file and the signature'. RELEASING.md:88 says 'the README shows the same steps'. card-identities.md:23 says 'The README lists the options' for personal details and sheet text. The cited README sections hold only a link to docs/CLI.md, plus the fingerprint for HEIRS.md and a few option names for the collection-sheet section. The same root cause leaves stale README pointers in the executable.yml:128 comment, ARCHITECTURE.md:23 ('its own modulus') and the heir-sheet-text.ts:202 comment (the worked 'abandon' to 'wool' example). RELEASING.md's Release notes and Version steps change CHANGELOG.md, docs/releases, package.json and src/version.ts, but not README line 8. A tag cut by following the procedure would publish a README that says 0.1.0 'has not been released'. One part of the claim is wrong: RELEASING.md:46 does describe Windows and macOS acceptance, because the tag workflow builds and runs each executable and drives its menu on all three systems. Another part is not shown to be a defect of RELEASING.md: the browser-parity and regroup preconditions are set by the workspace rules, and REMEDIATION.md already tracks them as pending pre-release work.
- **Impact:** Readers take an extra, unexpected hop, and the statements in three documents are inaccurate. The heir path is sheet, then README, then HEIRS.md, then README#one-executable-file, then docs/CLI.md. That is an extra step for a non-technical reader, but the integrity check stays reachable and correct. If the procedure is followed as written, the released tag's README would wrongly say that 0.1.0 is unreleased. No secret, recovery result or verification outcome is affected.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R7+R8; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Point HEIRS.md:33 and RELEASING.md:88 at docs/CLI.md#download-verification, or keep the steps in the README. Point card-identities.md:23 at docs/CLI.md#card-options. Fix the README references in the executable.yml:128 and heir-sheet-text.ts:202 comments and in ARCHITECTURE.md:23. Check whether the README's 'Cards and QR codes' section still fits the wording 'describes the options' in SSKR.md:88 and card-designs.md:3. Add a RELEASING.md step that updates or removes the README line 8 status sentence, or add a check that ties it to the CHANGELOG heading. Separately, consider adding docs/CLI.md to KEY_DOCUMENTS in scripts/release-key.mjs. It now carries the release key fingerprint for download verification, but pnpm check does not check it.
- **Required verification:** For every Markdown link and comment that names a README section, confirm that the target section holds the specific content claimed, not just that the anchor exists. Rerun the AUD-037-DOC011 probe and expect present lists that match each claim. Confirm that RELEASING.md names the README status line, and that a tag-time or check-time rule fails when that line disagrees with the CHANGELOG release state. If docs/CLI.md is added to KEY_DOCUMENTS, confirm that pnpm check fails when the docs/CLI.md fingerprint is mutated.

##### AUD-037-DOC012 — Low — Release documents use "boundary" alone and names for the self-tests that are not in the glossary

- **Category:** DOC
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: This is a wording nonconformance with CORE-17, a rule checked by review. No security, integrity or recovery guarantee, no legal obligation and no documented safety check changes. Every protection and check is described correctly in substance. No workspace rule makes glossary wording a precondition of a release.
- **Rules:** CORE-17
- **Affected files and builds:** CHANGELOG.md:136; docs/releases/v0.1.0.md:106; SECURITY.md:42; README.md:252,254; docs/CLI.md:233,256. Related, not cited: the help text in src/cli/help-pages.ts:654 ("smaller core self-test") and code comments in src/cli/command-options.ts:151 and src/core/backup-check.ts:420 ("quick self-test"). Dropped from the proposal: SECURITY.md:36 ("permission boundary") and SECURITY.md:38 (the heading "local boundaries").
- **Reproduction:** Read the cited lines with git show f8d9a93:<path>. Between f8d9a93 and HEAD 61e163b only docs/REMEDIATION.md, docs/audits/README.md, package.json, vitest.config.ts and .git-blame-ignore-revs changed, so the cited files are the same at both commits. Read the workspace GLOSSARY.md. Its "Trust boundary" entry has _Avoid_: boundary (alone), seam; "Seam" has _Avoid_: boundary; "Startup self-test" has _Avoid_: quick test, boot check; "Full self-test" has _Avoid_: long self-test, complete test. CORE-17 says: "Identifiers, messages and documents use the term the glossary gives ... A word listed under _Avoid_ stays out of new code and text." Both the glossary and CORE-17 arrived in workspace commit 96798aa at 2026-10-14 01:15 UTC +0000, and neither has changed since. docs.instructions.md applies CORE-17 to release notes. Probe: tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC012/scan.sh <mnemocode> f8d9a93 (output in tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC012/scan.out). It printed CHANGELOG.md:136 and docs/releases/v0.1.0.md:106 "Each boundary is confirmed before it is named"; SECURITY.md:42 "rather than a boundary against malicious code"; SECURITY.md:36 "permission boundary"; SECURITY.md:38 "## Secret lifecycle and local boundaries"; docs/CLI.md:233 "runs quick checks"; and docs/CLI.md:256 "The full test includes the quick checks". README.md:252 reads "Quick known-answer and refusal checks run before secret operations", and README.md:254 calls the full self-test "the fuller installation check". First appearance of each phrase, from git log -S (output in tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC012/origins.out): "Each boundary is confirmed" in 45c9f86 at 2026-10-14 11:28 UTC, after the rule; "Quick known-answer and refusal checks" in 0d7898f at 2026-10-15, after the rule; the CLI.md "quick checks" and "The full test" text in 5cfb026 at 2026-10-13, then moved from README into docs/CLI.md by 0d7898f after the rule; "a boundary against malicious code" in 27c491a at 2026-10-04, before the rule; "permission boundary" and "local boundaries" in c53944c at 2026-09-27, before the rule. A grep of AUD-001 to AUD-036 and docs/REMEDIATION.md found no CORE-17 or glossary-word finding. AUD-031, AUD-032, AUD-034 and AUD-035 mark CORE-17 as supported at their recorded scope and state that they made no prose vocabulary scan.
- **Expected behaviour:** Under CORE-17, the 0.1.0 documents use the glossary terms "trust boundary", "startup self-test" and "full self-test". Text written after the glossary does not use the avoided word "boundary" on its own.
- **Observed behaviour:** CHANGELOG.md:136 and docs/releases/v0.1.0.md:106 were written after the glossary and say "Each boundary is confirmed before it is named", using "boundary" alone. SECURITY.md:42 calls the same thing "limits". SECURITY.md:42 also uses "a boundary against malicious code" where the concept is a trust boundary; this text predates the glossary. README.md:252/254 was written after the glossary and docs/CLI.md:233/256 was moved after it. They call the startup self-test "quick checks" or "Quick known-answer and refusal checks", and the full self-test "the full test" or "the fuller installation check". The help text calls the startup self-test a "core self-test", so one concept has four names. "quick checks" and "full test" are not literally on the _Avoid_ lists, but they break CORE-17's first sentence. "permission boundary" (SECURITY.md:36) and "local boundaries" (SECURITY.md:38) are qualified terms that predate the rule, so they are not "boundary" alone and are not defects.
- **Impact:** The impact is wording only. Release documents name one concept in several ways that differ from the workspace glossary, which makes cross-referencing harder. Every described protection and check is accurate, and no behaviour or security property is affected.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R7; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** In CHANGELOG.md and docs/releases/v0.1.0.md, change "Each boundary is confirmed" to "Each limit is confirmed" to match SECURITY.md. In SECURITY.md:42, write "a trust boundary against malicious code". In README.md and docs/CLI.md, call the checks that run before secret operations the "startup self-test" and the result of `mnemocode self-test` the "full self-test"; for example, "The full self-test includes the startup self-test plus ...". Leave the existing section headings and their anchors alone. Changing the help text and stable message wording ("core self-test") is optional and can be a separate change.
- **Required verification:** At the fix commit, rerun tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC012/scan.sh. Expect no "boundary" on its own in CHANGELOG.md, docs/releases/v0.1.0.md or SECURITY.md:42, and expect README.md and docs/CLI.md to use "startup self-test" and "full self-test". Run pnpm format:check and confirm that the README links to SECURITY.md#secret-lifecycle-and-local-boundaries and docs/CLI.md#installation-checks still resolve.

- Tracked files at f8d9a93 that cite AUD-033: docs/REMEDIATION.md, docs/audits/AUD-034-2026-10-18.json, docs/audits/AUD-035-2026-10-18.{md,json}, docs/audits/README.md, src/core/candidate-encryption-checks.ts and test/aud-035-share-input.test.ts.
