- **Expected behaviour:** Under audits.instructions.md, every numbered audit report lives in the repository under docs/audits/, is indexed in docs/audits/README.md, and has its harness scripts committed in docs/audits/AUD-NNN-harnesses/. AUDIT_STANDARD.md requires each new report to be added to the chronological index. CHECK-DOC-003 requires a consistent index and requires machine-local evidence paths to be labelled as local. If a report is deliberately withheld, the index should at least say so, as the AUD-023 row does.
- **Observed behaviour:** The AUD-033 report and its JSON companion exist only in the git-ignored evidence folder, marked "Local only — do not commit or publish". The index skips from AUD-032 to AUD-034 with no row or explanation, and no AUD-033 harness folder is published. Despite this, REMEDIATION.md, AUD-035, AUD-034 JSON, the index's AUD-035 row, a test name and a source comment cite 16 AUD-033 finding IDs and AUD-033 evidence paths. The source comment does not say that its oracle path is local-only, and several published harnesses of AUD-032, AUD-034 and AUD-035 read files under the ignored AUD-033-evidence folder.
- **Impact:** A reader of the repository or the package cannot find the original statement, severity or evidence behind 16 finding IDs that the remediation register reports as corrected. Cited evidence and the oracle provenance in shipped source cannot be resolved, and the published harnesses of later audits cannot be rerun as their READMEs claim. The impact is limited to documentation and traceability, with no effect on product behaviour or secret handling.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R7+R8+coordinator; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Either publish the AUD-033 report pair under docs/audits/ with an index row and an AUD-033-harnesses folder that has a README, after the privacy anonymization the rules require; or, if the owner keeps it private, add an AUD-033 index row that says the report is withheld and why, and summarise the cited AUD-033 findings in a published record. In both cases, label the AUD-033 evidence paths cited in source comments, AUD-034 JSON and published harnesses as machine-local, or replace them with published equivalents.
- **Required verification:** - `git ls-tree -r HEAD docs/audits` either lists an AUD-033 report pair and harness README, or the index has an AUD-033 row explaining the withholding.
- Every tracked citation of an AUD-033 finding ID resolves to a published record.
- `grep -rn 'AUD-033-evidence' src test docs` finds no unlabelled machine-local path.
- `pnpm check` (audit privacy and package listing gates) still passes.

##### AUD-037-DOC014 — Low — Audit index table is split by a blank line, so the AUD-035 and AUD-036 rows render as plain text

- **Category:** DOC
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: The defect is a rendering and consistency problem in the audit index. The AUD-035 and AUD-036 records, their links and their content are all published and reachable. No security, integrity or recovery guarantee, legal distribution obligation, documented main-path safety check or explicit release precondition depends on how the index renders.
- **Rules:** audits.instructions.md, DOC-02, CHECK-DOC-003
- **Related:** AUD-024-DOC002, AUD-037-DOC013
- **Affected files and builds:** docs/audits/README.md:42 (blank line inside the index table; rows at lines 43-44 follow it), docs/audits/README.md:34-44 (link-cell formatting differs between rows). The JSON part of the proposal (docs/audits/AUD-030-2026-10-15.json, AUD-031-2026-10-15.json, AUD-032-2026-10-16.json, scope.*) is not a defect.
- **Reproduction:** Both probes were read-only and ran on git objects in memory.
(1) python3 -I /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC014/render_index.py <repo> f8d9a93, and again at 61e163b. This renders docs/audits/README.md with markdown-it 3.0.0 using the gfm-like preset. Output at both commits: "pipe lines 7-44, blank lines inside: [42]"; "tables rendered: 1"; "rows rendered as table cells: 33 (AUD-001..AUD-034)"; "ids rendered outside the table: {'AUD-035': 'paragraph(raw pipes)', 'AUD-036': 'paragraph(raw pipes)'}". The blank line arrived in 4978a62 together with the AUD-035 row. The Markdown gate (scripts/verify-markdown.mjs) checks only blank lines inside lists, so it does not catch this.
(2) python3 -I /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC014/json_breakdown.py <repo> f8d9a93 <paths>. Results:
- AUD-031: 13,511,450 bytes; scope 92.3% (fileLedger 33.2%, interfaceContextAdmission 19.4%, reviewedContracts 19.3%, publicInventory 8.2%, supplements 5.6%).
- AUD-032: 6,171,814 bytes; scope 86.0% (fileLedger 49.9%, operationCoverage 29.8%).
- AUD-030: 2,704,239 bytes; scope 85.4%.
So the reported sizes are accurate. The output is in out-breakdown.txt and out-render.txt in that folder.
(3) Contract reading:
- AUDIT_STANDARD.md:85 says JSON reports carry the envelope fields and that "additional evidence fields are allowed".
- audits.instructions.md keeps evidence files local, but says the report itself carries "the commands, results and hashes a reader needs".
- AUD-031.md:30 points readers to "the exact per-file methods and context admissions in the JSON companion".
- AUD-026-harnesses/README.md:58 validates "the 882-file ledger" inside the JSON.
- package.json "files" deliberately ships docs/audits/AUD-*.json, and mnemocode AGENTS.md requires every audit file to be in the package listing.
- Since AUD-034, the companions no longer embed ledgers (AUD-034/035/036 JSON are 68 KB, 209 KB and 347 KB).
- No rule sets a size limit.
- **Expected behaviour:** CHECK-DOC-003 covers the audit index. The audit skill says every audit appends a row to the docs/audits/README.md table. DOC-02 asks for one consistent look. So every audit row should render inside the single index table, and the link cells should follow one form.
- **Observed behaviour:** At f8d9a93, and still at 61e163b, a blank line at docs/audits/README.md:42 ends the GFM table after the AUD-034 row. The AUD-035 and AUD-036 rows have no header or delimiter row, so they render as one paragraph of raw pipe-separated text, and the index table shows only AUD-001 to AUD-034. The link cells also vary between rows: commas or middle dots as separators; "harness scripts", "Harness" or "Harnesses" as the label; and a folder, README.md or RERUN.md as the target. The JSON size claim holds as a fact: AUD-030, AUD-031 and AUD-032 together hold 22.4 MB, mostly scope ledgers. But it breaks no stated rule. The standard allows extra evidence fields in JSON, and the reports name the JSON as the home of the per-file ledgers.
- **Impact:** On GitHub and in any GFM viewer, readers of the audit index (which also ships in the npm package) do not see the two newest audits as table rows. They see a run of pipe-delimited text instead, which makes the latest audit status hard to find and read. Nothing is lost: the records and links stay reachable. The bulky historical JSON companions make the repository and npm package larger (top-level audit JSON totals 28.2 MB at f8d9a93) but cause no defect. This is an informational observation only.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R7+R8+coordinator; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Remove the blank line at docs/audits/README.md:42 so that AUD-035 and AUD-036 are rows of the single table. Pick one link-cell form and use it for new rows, without rewriting the substance of historical rows. Optionally, extend scripts/verify-markdown.mjs to refuse a blank line between pipe-table rows, or a pipe row outside a table, in published Markdown. Do not strip ledgers from the historical AUD-030/031/032 JSON. If package size is a concern, the owner can decide to change the package "files" policy instead.
- **Required verification:** Rerun render_index.py, or render with any GFM parser, on the corrected README: one table that contains every indexed audit row, from AUD-001 to the latest, with no AUD id outside a table cell. pnpm check and pnpm check:audit-privacy pass. If the Markdown gate is extended, a negative fixture with a blank line inside a table is refused.

##### AUD-037-DOC015 — Low — AUD-036 Markdown and JSON disagree on CHECK-DOC-003 (Markdown left "in-progress"), and recent audit records contain run-together prose

- **Category:** DOC
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: The defect is in published audit records only. No program output, wallet derivation, recovery path, security guarantee, licence notice or release-precondition gate is affected. The AUDIT_STANDARD rule that paired records must agree is a precondition for publishing a report, not for the 0.1.0 release.
- **Rules:** CHECK-DOC-003, AUDIT_STANDARD
- **Affected files and builds:** docs/audits/AUD-036-2026-10-19.md:1 (H1 title), :37, :45, :54, :60, :64 (CHECK-DOC-003 "in-progress"), :85, :87, :112, :158; docs/audits/AUD-036-2026-10-19.json (.title, .checks[31] CHECK-DOC-003 "qualified", run-together assessment strings); docs/audits/README.md:44 (AUD-036 index title); run-together prose also in AUD-026, AUD-029, AUD-030, AUD-031, AUD-034 and AUD-035 Markdown and JSON, and AUD-009/AUD-012 earlier
- **Reproduction:** Read at f8d9a93 with `git show f8d9a93:<path>`. HEAD is now 61e163b, but `git diff --quiet f8d9a93 61e163b -- docs/audits/AUD-0*` confirms no audit record changed.

- 32 checks on each side.
- "OUTCOME MISMATCH CHECK-DOC-003 md= in-progress json= qualified".
- Markdown outcome vocabulary includes 'in-progress'; the JSON vocabulary does not.
- Three titles: MD "Precommit CODE review and release follow-up"; JSON "Precommit code-quality review and local release-verification follow-up"; README "Precommit changed-code review and local release follow-up".
- Finding severities agree. JSON keeps original status "open" and remediation status "verified", which matches the Markdown.

(2) Strict prose probe (JSON string values only, keys and commands ignored; Markdown with code and links stripped): `SHOW=30 python3 -I /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC015/glued.py docs/audits/AUD-036-2026-10-19.md docs/audits/AUD-036-2026-10-19.json`.
- AUD-036 MD: total=25. Examples: 'Fresh18 native tests', 'No copies of50tokens or more in306src/scripts', '**2986passed,1failed,0 skipped', 'PTY;132 current', 'bytes:0 pass/2 fail', 'pass/0fail after.29 name-filtered', 'two40/80-column', 'baseline648-input'.
- AUD-036 JSON: total=20. Examples: 'exact20-file', 'current132', 'after83green', 'from9ade4a', '32pins/four'.

(3) The same probe over all records gives:
- Markdown: AUD-026=41, AUD-029=19, AUD-030=7, AUD-031=12, AUD-034=14, AUD-035=29.
- JSON: AUD-031=503, AUD-030=31, AUD-035=22.
- AUD-014..025, AUD-027 and AUD-032 Markdown: 0 each.
- AUD-031 JSON has 29 unique strings with 480 occurrences. One sentence, 'Current29 report pairs ... all26 command/log bindings validate.', is repeated 416 times.

(4) R7's probe `/home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/r7/runon2.py` gives AUD-036 MD=21 and AUD-031 JSON=3415. Its AUD-031 hits are mostly camelCase JSON keys such as priorFileSha256 and sha256Observed, which are false positives.

(5) REMEDIATION.md at f8d9a93, lines 45 and 100-103, says the owner "subsequently authorized pushing", and that the AUD-036 section "retains the status recorded before the later push authorization".

(6) Earlier records: grep and JSON scans of AUD-001..036 and REMEDIATION.md find no finding about MD/JSON check-outcome disagreement or run-together prose. The nearest DOC findings (AUD-002-DOC001, AUD-003-DOC001, AUD-018-DOC002, AUD-024-DOC002) are different defects.
- **Expected behaviour:** AUDIT_STANDARD requires the Markdown and JSON pair to describe the same review and to agree on metadata, statuses and results, and it requires this to be checked before publishing. FULL_AUDIT_GUIDE limits check outcomes to passed, failed, blocked, skipped, not-run and not-applicable. It also says a report is complete only when every planned area has a recorded outcome. Published records should therefore give one final CHECK-DOC-003 outcome in both formats. Their prose should also be readable, with no words and numbers run together.
- **Observed behaviour:** The published AUD-036 Markdown records CHECK-DOC-003 as "in-progress", stating that pair, index, schema, privacy and evidence reconciliation "is finalized only after all current receipts are available". The JSON companion records the same check as "qualified" and says it was reconciled. The Markdown row is therefore stale or unfinished, and the pair disagrees on a result.

The H1, JSON title and README index title are three different paraphrases. This is a minor metadata inconsistency, and 12 earlier pairs show similar title elaborations.

AUD-036 contains about 25 run-together prose defects in the Markdown and about 20 in the JSON, such as "Fresh18", "of50tokens or more in306src" and "2986passed,1failed,0 skipped". The same kind of defect recurs in AUD-026..035, including 29 unique strings in AUD-031 JSON that are repeated to 480 occurrences.

Corrections to the claim:
- The push-status "contradiction" does not hold. REMEDIATION.md explicitly labels its AUD-036 section as status recorded before the later push authorization, and historical reports are intentionally left unchanged.
- The claimed counts are inflated. "~280" is not the AUD-036 Markdown count: R7's noisy probe gives about 286 summed across the AUD-026..036 Markdown records. "~600" for AUD-031 JSON is inflated by JSON key names.
- **Impact:** A reader of the shipped audit records sees an unfinished records-consistency check in the Markdown and a different result in the JSON. This weakens trust in the pair-consistency claim. Run-together counts make several results, such as "after.29" and "two40/80-column", harder to read and easy to misquote. No program behaviour, wallet output, secret handling or release artifact is affected. The records are excluded from formatting, so no gate catches this.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R7; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Add a dated documentation-only correction to AUD-036, as AUDIT_STANDARD allows for documentation edits:
- Set the Markdown CHECK-DOC-003 row to the same final outcome and assessment as the JSON. Use the guide's outcome vocabulary, or record the existing extra outcome terms as an explicit convention.
- Align the H1, JSON title and README index title, or state that the index uses a short form.
- Insert the missing spaces in the AUD-036 prose.

Optionally:
- Correct the recurring run-together strings in AUD-026..035, recording these edits as documentation edits.
- Extend the existing audit-record checks to compare check outcomes across each Markdown/JSON pair and to flag glued word-number prose in string values.
- **Required verification:** - Rerun /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC015/pair.py on the corrected AUD-036 pair. It should report no outcome mismatch and a single title, or a documented short form.
- Rerun /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC015/glued.py on the corrected records. It should report zero hits, or only reviewed intentional tokens.
- Confirm that the correction is recorded as a dated documentation edit, without changing original execution evidence.

##### AUD-037-DOC016 — Low — Remediation register records dispositions without standard statuses or fix/verification commits

- **Category:** DOC
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: This is a gap in how fixes are recorded. It does not break a security, integrity or recovery guarantee. The corrected tests and fixes are in the signed history, and later full suites pass (2,988 at 4978a62; CI 3,000/0 failed on Linux at f8d9a93). No workspace rule makes the wording of the register a precondition for release.
- **Rules:** AUDIT_STANDARD remediation status
- **Related:** AUD-010-DOC006, AUD-002-DOC001, AUD-022-DOC006, AUD-032-BLD001, AUD-032-BLD002, AUD-035-SEC001, AUD-035-API002, AUD-035-DOC001
- **Affected files and builds:** docs/REMEDIATION.md:168-181 (the AUD-035 corrections table: a "Current state" column and no Fix commit / Verification commit columns); docs/REMEDIATION.md:214 (AUD-032-BLD001/BLD002/DOC001 carried forward with no status); docs/audits/AUD-032-2026-10-16.md:253-254,265 and the JSON remediation[] (BLD001/BLD002 recorded as fixed, fixCommit and verificationCommit null); docs/audits/README.md:40; docs/audits/AUD-029-2026-10-15.md:216,220 and the JSON remediationAddendum.lifetimeCheckpoint.initialReview.confirmedFindings[0] (priority "P2", sub-ID AUD-029-SEC001-review-001). docs/audits/AUD-035-2026-10-18.md:165-172 is NOT a defect site.
- **Reproduction:** I read the files with git show f8d9a93:<path> and wrote a read-only probe: /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC016/status_probe.py, run as `timeout 30 python3 -I status_probe.py f8d9a93` (exit 0). Output at f8d9a93:
- The AUD-035 JSON gives all five IDs status open with fixCommit/verificationCommit None.
- The AUD-032 JSON gives BLD001 and BLD002 as ('fixed', None, None) and DOC001 as ('verified', None, None). The findings themselves are open, medium, releaseBlocking True.
- The header of the REMEDIATION.md AUD-035 table is ['Finding ID / variant','Cause and required behavior','Current state','Regression / verification','Independent review']. Its ten state values are 'Verified for current Linux snapshot' (9) or 'Verified for current scoped follow-up' (1). None is one of the eight standard status values.
- REMEDIATION.md mentions AUD-032-BLD only in the 'Earlier corrections carried forward' row, with no status.
- No AUD-034/035/036 record mentions AUD-032-BLD001 or BLD002.
- AUD-029 Markdown has P1-P3 labels on lines 216 and 220; the AUD-029 JSON priority values are ['P2'].
Running the probe at HEAD 61e163b gives the same output for the REMEDIATION.md table, the AUD-032 references and AUD-029, so the gap persists. git log shows the AUD-035 and AUD-032 fixes entered history in 4978a62. git grep finds the corrected 28-label inventory and the new QR wording in the tests at f8d9a93. REMEDIATION.md:150 names 4978a62 only as the 'signed implementation checkpoint', not per row.
- **Expected behaviour:** AUDIT_STANDARD requires remediation and verification tables to use Finding ID, Status, Fix commit, Verification commit and Evidence columns. Status values must come from open/fixed/verified/deferred/accepted/not-reproduced/withdrawn/duplicate. A `verified` status names the verification commit, and AUD-010-DOC006 set the precedent that rows name fix and verification commits once those exist. P1/P2/P3 must not stand in for an ID or a severity, and an optional priority uses urgent/normal/deferred.
- **Observed behaviour:** - **AUD-035 record:** it correctly keeps its five baseline findings open with no commits. The standard says a historical report's status refers to its own snapshot, so this part of the claim is not a defect.
- **Remediation register:** the later follow-up, docs/REMEDIATION.md, records the ten AUD-035 variant dispositions only as free-text states such as 'Verified for current Linux snapshot'. It has no per-row fix or verification commit, although fix commit 4978a62 exists.
- **AUD-032-BLD001/BLD002:** these medium, release-blocking findings are last recorded as `fixed` with null commits, pending a repeat of the mandatory full suite. The index still says so. The register carries them without any status, and no later record marks them verified, although the required full suite has since passed.
- **AUD-029:** the record labels the BOM regression found during remediation as 'P2'. The Markdown prose calls it a P2 regression and the JSON gives "priority": "P2" under a non-canonical sub-ID.
- **Impact:** A reader of the published records cannot find the fix and verification commits of the AUD-035 corrections, and cannot see whether the two release-blocking AUD-032 test findings were ever verified. A release gate that reads the records would still show them as unverified. The impact is limited to records: the code fixes exist and later suites pass, and no wallet output or secret handling is affected.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R7; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Add a standard remediation table to docs/REMEDIATION.md, or a dated addendum to the AUD-035 and AUD-032 pairs, with these columns: Finding ID, Status, Fix commit, Verification commit, Evidence. It should:
- record each AUD-035 variant as `verified` with fix commit 4978a62 and its actual verification commit;
- record AUD-032-BLD001/BLD002 as `verified` at the first commit whose full mandatory suite passed (or keep them `fixed` with the reason);
- align the docs/audits/README.md index rows.
For AUD-029, add an addendum note that maps 'P2' to legacyPriority or a standard priority and severity. Do not rewrite the historical prose. Leave the AUD-035 baseline statuses unchanged.
- **Required verification:** Re-read the updated register or addenda. Every row should use a standard status value and name fix and verification commits that exist in `git log` and whose recorded suites actually ran there. The Markdown and JSON pairs and the docs/audits/README.md index should agree. Rerun /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC016/status_probe.py and confirm it no longer prints NON-STANDARD states for the AUD-035 rows.

##### AUD-037-DOC017 — Low — The bundled card artwork has no recorded origin or license in the notices or the executable license file

- **Category:** DOC
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: No license breach is shown. Nothing shows that the images are third-party or that their terms require a notice. The defect is a missing provenance record in the legal materials, so it breaks no mandatory security, integrity or recovery guarantee, and no proven distribution obligation. It would become blocking only if the owner found that the images come from a third party under terms that require attribution or forbid redistribution.
- **Rules:** CHECK-DOC-002, CHECK-BLD-003
- **Affected files and builds:** assets/images/* (12 files); THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md (sections for fonts and the world map, none for the images); scripts/executable-notices.mjs:1-5 and :29 (ASSET_FILES lists only fonts/DejaVuSans-NOTICE and maps/NOTICE); scripts/build-executable.mjs:67-81 (bundledFiles walks all of assets/ into the executable); package.json "files" includes "assets"; docs/card-designs.md:29-31 ("Artwork" section, which calls them photographs and gives no origin); README.md:290-292 (the claim is on line 292; line 290 is the heading)
- **Reproduction:** All reads were at f8d9a93; assets/, NOTICE, THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md and scripts/executable-notices.mjs are byte-identical at the current checkout (git diff --quiet f8d9a93 -- assets returned 0). (1) Ran /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC017/doc_probe.out commands. git ls-tree lists 12 files under assets/images. The only notice/license files under assets are assets/fonts/DejaVuSans-NOTICE and assets/maps/NOTICE. package.json:327 lists "assets" in files. executable-notices.mjs:29 sets ASSET_FILES = ["fonts/DejaVuSans-NOTICE", "maps/NOTICE"]. The built licenses file release/local-7facfcc/mnemocode-0.1.0-linux-x64-licenses.txt (built from identical notice inputs) has 0 matches for images/|photograph|artwork, and its sections are MnemoCode, Node.js, the npm packages, the SSKR engine, and "Fonts and map data". (2) git grep for the 12 file stems or assets/images across NOTICE, THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md, README.md, LICENSE, docs/*.md, docs/releases/*.md and CHANGELOG.md found only template-ID usages such as --template business-it. No match states an origin or license. A wider search for photograph|artwork|stock|generated|license near the image names found only docs/card-designs.md:31, which says photographs "are stored in the program", and release-note lines about cropping the photographs. Searches of the git history of THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md, NOTICE and docs/card-designs.md, and of the commit messages that added the images (a6627fd, be33cc4, 5cfb026), found no earlier provenance statement. (3) python3 -I /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC017/image_metadata.py assets/images (output in image_metadata.out). The PNGs have no ancillary chunks (no tEXt, iTXt or eXIf). The JPEGs have only a 16-byte JFIF APP0. There are no keyword hits for copyright, author, source, license, stock, generator or C2PA, so the files carry no embedded provenance either. (4) Viewed two images. material-kitchen.jpg is a photorealistic material-board mock-up. business-architect-v1.png combines a photographic city skyline with card graphics. Their origin cannot be told from the content.
- **Expected behaviour:** Like every other bundled non-code input (the DejaVu font with a pinned source and license, the Natural Earth map with its NOTICE, the BIP39 word lists, and the SSKR crates), the 12 bundled card images should have a recorded origin and license basis. This can be a third-party notice with source and license, or a statement that they are first-party works under the project's MIT license. The record should reach THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md and the executable license notices.
- **Observed behaviour:** 12 raster artwork files (7 business-*.png and 5 material-*.jpg, about 2.2 MB) are listed in renderAssets. The package file list ships them under "assets", and every single executable embeds them. No document, notice file, release licenses file or embedded metadata records who made them or under what license. docs/card-designs.md calls them photographs. README.md:292 says the third-party notices cover only dependencies and word-list origins.
- **Impact:** A reviewer or downstream redistributor of 0.1.0 cannot establish the right to redistribute the photographic artwork in the executables and package. The release's license notices are silent on 12 embedded files. Nothing shows that the files are unlicensed copies, so the impact is limited to documentation and provenance, with no security effect.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R8+R7+coordinator; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Record the origin of each image file: the creator or the generating tool, the source URL or that it is the project's own work, and the license with any attribution text. Add a "Card artwork" section to THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md, or a notice file beside the images such as assets/images/NOTICE added to ASSET_FILES in scripts/executable-notices.mjs. Do this so that the executable license notices carry it. If the images are first-party, state that they are covered by the project's MIT license. Optionally pin each file's SHA-256 next to that statement.
- **Required verification:** The owner confirms the real origin and license of each of the 12 files. THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md (or an assets/images notice) names each file or the group with its origin and license. A rebuilt *-licenses.txt from pnpm build:executable contains that statement. README.md:292 no longer limits the notices to dependencies and word lists, or it links the artwork statement.

##### AUD-037-DOC018 — Low — docs/CLI.md says no search has more than 2^31 attempts, but Decode's joint search of marked codes and dates has no total ceiling

- **Category:** DOC
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: The search is not secretly unbounded. Its size and estimated time are shown, a long one runs only after a yes on the private screen, and Ctrl+C stops it. No secret, integrity or recovery guarantee breaks, so this is a wrong statement in the documentation, not a broken release precondition.
- **Rules:** DOC-07, CORE-50
- **Affected files and builds:** docs/CLI.md:174 (identical at f8d9a93 and 61e163b); src/core/missing-codes-search.ts WalletAndDatesWay.plan (count = codes.expected * dates.combinations, compared with no ceiling); src/core/date-search.ts:80 and 212-216 (HARD_MAX_COMBINATIONS = 2^31, enforced per DatesAnswer only); src/cli/missing-codes-search.ts started()/byWallet() (asks, never refuses); src/cli/date-search.ts:110 (--max-candidates max = 2^31)
- **Reproduction:** I read the reviewer's probe and found it sound: it calls only the dist core API (built from the audited source; src is unchanged between f8d9a93 and 61e163b), the wallet check never matches, and it uses public counting codes. Command: `node --max-old-space-size=256 /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/r2/probe_joint_limit.mjs`. Output: {"codesExpected":262144,"dateCombinations":6674031,"planCount":1749557182464,"overDateCap":true,"size":"262,144 candidates with 6,674,031 date combinations each","ask":true,"seconds":818294554}. Peak memory was 87 MB and the run took 0.2 s.

I also wrote my own probe. Command: `node --max-old-space-size=256 /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC018/probe_joint_cap.mjs`. Output:
(1) {"step":"small-run","expected":128,"dateCombinations":10,"planCount":1280,"walletChecksInRun":1310,"result":"none"}. A real run makes one wallet check per code candidate per date combination (131 candidates times 10), so the attempts multiply.
(2) {"step":"large-plan-with-max-limit","hardMax":2147483648,"expected":262144,"dateCombinations":36525,"planCount":9574809600,"ratioToHardMax":4.5,"ask":true,"question":"Search 262,144 candidates with 36,525 date combinations each, about 47 days?"}. This plan was made with the largest limit `--max-candidates` accepts (2^31). It is not refused; the program only asks.

From the source: WalletAndDatesWay.plan never compares its count with HARD_MAX_COMBINATIONS. The only check is in the DatesAnswer constructor, and it covers one date answer. The CLI's started() asks and never refuses.

Earlier records: I grepped docs/audits AUD-001 to AUD-036 (.md and .json) and docs/REMEDIATION.md for the 2^31 figure, HARD_MAX_COMBINATIONS, the doc sentence, "date combinations each" and "Date search details". There is no open or fixed item for this defect.
- **Expected behaviour:** The documentation should state only bounds the code enforces (DOC-07). docs/CLI.md:174 says without qualification that "No search has more than 2,147,483,648 attempts". Decode's own help counts each code candidate with every date combination it is tried with, so under that statement a joint search of marked codes and dates should also stay at or under 2^31 attempts. Otherwise the documentation should limit the statement to a single date search.
- **Observed behaviour:** The 2^31 ceiling is enforced only on the date combinations of one date answer. Decode's joint search of marked codes and dates multiplies code candidates by date combinations without any ceiling. One reproduced plan had 1,749,557,182,464 attempts (about 26 years). Another, planned with the largest `--max-candidates` value (2^31), had 9,574,809,600 attempts (about 47 days) and was offered as a question rather than refused. The joint search is reachable only on the private screen, because dates with `?` are accepted on the command line only by recover-date. A total above 2^31 can therefore run only after the person answers yes, never through `--max-candidates`. The reviewer's phrase "bounded only by the 12-hour question or --max-candidates" needs that correction. README.md:234 ("no date search exceeds") and the CHANGELOG and release notes ("up to 2,147,483,648 date combinations") are worded per date search and stay accurate. The unqualified sentence in docs/CLI.md is the inaccurate one.
- **Impact:** A reader of docs/CLI.md is promised a hard ceiling on search work that does not exist for Decode with codes and dates both marked. The ceiling's rationale in the code ("about a month") does not hold for this search. The person is still shown the size and estimated time, has to confirm, and can stop the search with Ctrl+C. The effect is limited to a misleading statement of how far work is bounded; no secret is exposed and no wrong result is produced.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R2; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** There are two options; choose one and keep the code and documents consistent. Documentation option: in docs/CLI.md, limit the sentence to one date search (for example, "No date search has more than 2,147,483,648 date combinations"). Then add that Decode tries each candidate for marked codes with every date combination, so the total can be larger and is always asked for on the private screen. Code option: if 2^31 is meant as a safety ceiling for all work, make WalletAndDatesWay.plan refuse a count above HARD_MAX_COMBINATIONS (or a stated combined ceiling, such as the share search's 2^40) with a stable error code, and document that ceiling.
- **Required verification:** Rerun /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-DOC018/probe_joint_cap.mjs and the r2 probe against a fresh dist. With the documentation fix: docs/CLI.md, README.md:234, the decode help's `--max-candidates` text and the release notes describe the same bound, and the joint plan still asks. With the code fix: a regression test in test/missing-codes-search.test.ts shows that a joint plan above the ceiling is refused with its error code, and one at the ceiling is planned. pnpm check and pnpm format:check pass.

(3) node /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/r6/doc-errors.mjs <repo> 200 → "182; doc names a failure: 114; not: 68" (reproduced). Of the 68, 10 are members of non-exported classes (CodeSearchInput.assertLive, WalletAndDatesWay.run, ListWay.run, six card-drawing.ts internals, FarthestColours.first).

The 59 are the reviewers' list minus the 10 internal members plus ShareCardSet.documents.

- **Observed behaviour:** Three comments name identifiers that do not exist at f8d9a93. Only assertStillRunning was actually removed; FILE_NAME_FOR_FOLDER and THREE_PHRASES never existed. The comment at missing-codes-search.ts:569 says the nested date search stops at its first match, but it checks every date combination (10 of 10 at runtime) and only keeps one shown. The printCodes doc points to terminalCodes, which printCodes no longer calls and nothing else calls. sskr-restore-command.ts:732 still calls the codes "numbered". By keyword heuristic, about 59 of 180 throwing exported functions and public methods of exported classes in library modules, plus 16 of 19 throwing public constructors, have doc comments that do not name their failures, including several published parsers. The proposed count of 68 of 182 includes members of non-exported classes.
- **Impact:** Maintainers and host integrators are misled, but no runtime behaviour is affected. A host reading parseRecord's doc expects undefined for unreadable input, not coded exceptions. A maintainer trusting line 569 may think the nested wallet-and-dates search is cheaper than it is. Stale names send readers to code that does not exist. No secret exposure, wrong result or failed check follows.

Reword missing-codes-search.ts:569 to say the nested search keeps its first match but checks every date combination; change behaviour only as a separate, tested decision. Point the printCodes doc to codeLines/joinedCodeLines and remove or reuse the unused terminalCodes. Drop "numbered" from sskr-restore-command.ts:732. Add the refusal conditions or error codes to the doc comments of the throwing public functions, methods and constructors, starting with the published parsers (parseRecord, serializeRecord, parsePageSize, parseOrientation, parseImageFormat, parseDocumentFormat, parseColors, unicodeToColors, parseMissingWord, readShare).
- **Required verification:** Rerun stale-facts.sh: every identifier named in the corrected comments resolves to code. Rerun throwing-docs.mjs and check its remaining list by hand, since the keyword test is only a heuristic. Run pnpm check and pnpm format:check. No test-suite change is needed for comment-only edits; rerun nested-date-search.mjs only if the search's behaviour is changed instead of the comment.

#### UI

##### AUD-037-UI001 — Medium — Private-screen step redraw erases the encoded fingerprint before the dates, and other pre-question lines

- **Category:** UI
- **Severity:** medium
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** true: The docs promise the encoded-fingerprint check of the written codes or shares before the dates. README.md:96 says it "checks the written codes without dates". docs/SSKR.md:44 says that on the private screen, and in Restore in the menu, "you find out before typing the dates". The headers of decode-command.ts and recover-date-command.ts say the same. The check runs on the main recovery paths of the first release: the menu's Decode (spawns `decode --ask-secrets`, menu.ts:219/264) and Restore (`sskr-combine --ask-secrets --mode seedshift`, menu.ts:319). Interactive use always goes through the private screen, and there the fingerprint is erased in the same write burst, so it can never be read. For complete codes nothing compares it automatically: `--encoded-fingerprint` serves only the missing-code '?' search (missing-codes-search.ts). Shipping 0.1.0 would therefore defeat a documented safety/integrity check on a main path, which is one of the listed release-blocking conditions. This holds only for the fingerprint part. The other erased lines (record-header notice, 'Detected input format', re-encode heading and info notice, Protection line) are low on their own and would not block release.
- **Rules:** CORE-39, DOC-06, DOC-07, CORE-33
- **Related:** AUD-026-UI002, AUD-027 (result-retention correction of the same PrivateSteps owner)
- **Affected files and builds:** src/cli/private-steps.ts:24-31 (begin() redraws whenever not showing a result); src/cli/private-screen.ts:87-92 (redraw = CLEAR_SCREEN + title + remembered warnings only); begin() callers src/cli/ask.ts:273-274 (askUntil), src/cli/terminal-choice.ts:322 (choose), :483 (askLineOnce), src/cli/terminal.ts:131-135 (terminalWork); only warning-kind notices are remembered: src/cli/terminal.ts:143-145; src/cli/backup-questions.ts:134-146 (showEncodedFingerprint -> reportFingerprint, src/cli/fingerprint-report.ts:49-55, terminalStatus/terminalHint or plain console.error, never remembered); src/cli/decode-command.ts:4-5,353-362; src/cli/recover-date-command.ts:230-235,253-262; src/cli/sskr-restore-command.ts:296-305; src/cli/backup-questions.ts:467-477 (record-header notice before choose); src/cli/input.ts:185-186; src/cli/re-encode-command.ts:153-157,192-199,225-227; src/cli/command-line.ts:143-144 with src/cli/private-screen.ts:53-58 (Protection line); docs: README.md:96, docs/SSKR.md:44; menu entry points src/cli/menu.ts:216-219,264-265,315-319; test gap: test/encode-decode-questions.test.ts:674-691 (asserts console.error call order only).
- **Reproduction:** The working tree is at 61e163b, not f8d9a93. `git diff --stat f8d9a93 61e163b` touches only .git-blame-ignore-revs, docs/REMEDIATION.md, docs/audits/README.md, package.json and vitest.config.ts. src/ is unchanged, and dist/cli/private-steps.js begin() matches the source. The new REMEDIATION.md text does not mention this issue.

Own probe: /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-UI001-lens/probe_fp_color.py. It uses a raw pty, not the project harness, at 40x120 with TERM=xterm-256color, and runs once with NO_COLOR=1 and once with colour enabled. SGR codes are stripped before matching. Data is public: the phrase "abandon x11 about" masked with the README date, and the public shares from scripts/terminal_answers.py. Run with `timeout 60 python3 -I`.
- `decode --ask-secrets --mode seedshift`: in plain mode the fingerprint 0d05289e is at offset 387, the last ESC[2J before the prompt at 429, the prompt at 458. In colour mode: 352, 439 and 468. In both runs the text after the last erase is exactly 'ESC[2J ESC[H MnemoCode · decode\r\n\r\nDates ('.
- `sskr-combine --ask-secrets --mode seedshift` with two shares: in plain mode the fingerprint is at 488, the erase at 530, the prompt at 565. In colour mode: 453, 540 and 575. The text after the erase is the title plus 'Dates ('.
Both the plain-line and colour branches of reportFingerprint are therefore erased.

Code reading:
- begin() clears unless a result is showing.
- remember() is reached only through warning-kind terminalNotice and validation warnings, so status, hint and info lines are not carried over.
- The menu's Decode and Restore spawn the same --ask-secrets commands.

History: before 0d7898f, CLEAR_SCREEN in terminal-choice.ts was used only by eraseAnswersSince (the menu's answer record), and ask.ts had no clear. private-steps.ts first exists in 0d7898f, the AUD-026-UI002 remediation, recorded as fixed in AUD-026 line 433/543.

Duplicate search: AUD-027 to AUD-036 Markdown and JSON, and REMEDIATION.md, have no item, open or closed, about the fingerprint or notices being erased by the step redraw. The only nearby item is AUD-027's result-retention fix, which is a different symptom.

The unit test at test/encode-decode-questions.test.ts:674-691 compares only the invocation order of console.error and the stderr prompt write, so a clear between them still passes.
- **Expected behaviour:** Once the codes or shares are checked, the encoded fingerprint should be readable on the private screen while the dates are asked. This is what README.md:96 and docs/SSKR.md:44 promise ("a share is wrong, and you find out before typing the dates"), and what the decode and recover-date contracts promise ("a mistyped code shows before any date is typed" or "before a long search"). CORE-39 allows this as long as the line is shown as part of the dates step. Lines that explain the next question should appear with that question: the no-record-header notice before the Seedshift question, and the re-encode confirmation notices that the code comment says 'stay at the top' of each new-backup screen.
- **Observed behaviour:** Every question (askUntil, choose, askLineOnce) and terminalWork calls PrivateSteps.begin(). Outside a displayed result, begin() writes ESC[2J ESC[H, the title and only the remembered warnings. The encoded fingerprint is written by terminalStatus/terminalHint, or by plain console.error under NO_COLOR, and is immediately followed by that clear and the Dates prompt, with no key or pause in between. It is never readable on the alternate screen, and nothing reaches scrollback. The same redraw drops:
- the 'no MNC1 record header' info notice before the Seedshift question;
- 'Detected input format';
- the re-encode 'New backup' heading and info-kind confirmation notice;
- the Protection line, both at screen open and after re-encode's questions.
Warning-kind notices, such as the swap and cloud warnings and the re-encode fingerprint-filter warning, are retained.
- **Impact:** The documented early check of written codes or shares by encoded fingerprint does not work on any interactive path, menu Decode and Restore included. The CLI has no other way to see that fingerprint for complete codes before entering dates.

Practical scope is bounded. Most typing errors are caught first by the BIP39 checksum or the SSKR/UR checks. The fingerprint is the documented check for errors that pass those: a wrong code that still satisfies the checksum, or a wrong but structurally valid share.

In those cases the user types the secret dates against wrong material. In recover-date, or decode with '?', a possibly long date search runs to no match. Without the encoded fingerprint, the user also cannot tell code errors from date errors when the final original fingerprint does not match.

No secret is disclosed and no wrong wallet is silently accepted, because the original fingerprint shown with the result and the wallet evidence still disagree. The other erased lines are bounded presentation losses.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R4; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json). A second, independent verifier checked severity and release blocking: agreed.
- **Recommended fix:** Give PrivateSteps a step-context slot, separate from warnings. Lines that belong to the next question would be redrawn by begin() under the title until that step is answered or newPage() runs. These lines are the encoded fingerprint (status line plus disclosure hint), the no-record-header notice, the re-encode heading and confirmation notices, and the post-question Protection line where intended. An alternative is to pass them as the question's own 'above' text.

This keeps CORE-39 (each step's screen holds the title and that step, including its context) and keeps plain, redirected and TERM=dumb output sequential.

Do not resolve it by deleting the promise from README.md and docs/SSKR.md. The pre-0d7898f behaviour, the code comments and the existing unit test all show that the visible fingerprint is the intended behaviour (DOC-06). If any of the minor lines is deliberately dropped instead, update its code comment so code and comment agree.
- **Required verification:** Add PTY regressions that check the visible frame, using Screen(...).lines() after the last ENTER_ALTERNATE_SCREEN rather than transcript order. Each should fail at f8d9a93/61e163b and pass after the fix:
- the encoded fingerprint visible on the Dates frame for `decode --ask-secrets --mode seedshift`, `recover-date --ask-secrets` and `sskr-combine --ask-secrets --mode seedshift`, with NO_COLOR and with colour;
- the same through the menu's Decode and Restore entries;
- the no-record-header notice on the Seedshift-question frame;
- the re-encode confirmation notices on the first new-backup question frame.

Change test/encode-decode-questions.test.ts:674-691 to assert that no screen clear occurs between the fingerprint and the dates prompt, or to assert the rendered frame. Rerun the AUD-027 result-retention cases, the AUD-026-UI002 fresh-step and warning carry-over PTY scenarios, the cancellation/exit-130 cases, and the redirected and TERM=dumb sequential-output checks.

##### AUD-037-UI002 — Medium — Unpaged SSKR share sets overflow the 24-row colour private screen: the pager ignores the header, fingerprints and backup question around the shares, so a whole share (even a required one in 3-of-3) scrolls away

- **Category:** UI
- **Severity:** medium
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: None of the release-blocking conditions holds. No secret is disclosed and no wrong or corrupted share set is produced. The documented backup check (the default "Check the backup now?" question) still works as designed: in 2-of-3 it correctly accepts shares 2 and 3, which do restore the wallet; in 3-of-3 it cannot pass without share 1, so it fails safe rather than certifying an incomplete backup. The remaining shares are labelled "share 2 of 3", so the gap is visible. The person still holds the source phrase they just typed, and file export is a workaround. The precedent AUD-028-UI001 has the same consequence class: shares 1-10 of a 16-of-16 set, all required, were unreachable, and it was recorded as release-blocking false. No workspace rule makes complete on-screen share display, or the exact CHANGELOG wording, an explicit release precondition. The overstated CHANGELOG and v0.1.0 claims are a documentation inaccuracy to correct, not a legal obligation of distribution. The defect does affect a primary path (the default colour TTY, default 80x24, an ordinary 2-of-3 or 3-of-3 set, the default backup question), which is why it is medium rather than low. Affecting a main path is not enough on its own to make it release blocking.
- **Rules:** CORE-39, CORE-10, AUD-028-UI001
- **Related:** AUD-028-UI001, AUD-033-UI001, AUD-026-UI002, AUD-028-UI002
- **Affected files and builds:** src/cli/private-result.ts:26-30 (fit test is #wrapped().length + #reserved() <= terminalRows(); counts only its own lines plus 4 navigation rows); src/cli/sskr-output.ts:166-178 (printShares passes only the share block lines, from 7facfcc); src/cli/sskr-split-command.ts:190-225 (colour-only "SSKR export" header, notices, optional masked words or original copy, printFingerprints and offerShareCheck printed around the shares, none counted; no clear before the unpaged print); src/cli/backup-check.ts:206-221 (redraw via show() only after a failed check's "Show the result again"; a No answer or a passing 2-of-3 check never redraws); src/cli/screen-fit.ts:14 (beside-aware linesFitPrivateScreen not reused); src/cli/terminal.ts:109-115 (header lines exist only with colour, which is why NO_COLOR fits); scripts/terminal_session.py:48 (PTY suite forces NO_COLOR=1); test/private-result.test.ts:178-191 (colour regression measures printShares in isolation only); CHANGELOG.md:5,10; docs/releases/v0.1.0.md:7
- **Reproduction:** Snapshot: HEAD is now 61e163b (= origin/main), two commits after the stated f8d9a93. `git diff --stat f8d9a93 61e163b` touches only .git-blame-ignore-revs, docs/REMEDIATION.md, docs/audits/README.md, package.json and vitest.config.ts. No src/ or build input changed, so the dist under test is equivalent.

I wrote my own probe, /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-UI002-lens/lens_probe.py. It runs dist/mnemocode.js `encode --sskr --ask-secrets` in a real PTY with TERM=xterm-256color and NO_COLOR, CLICOLOR_FORCE and CLICOLOR removed, so colour follows the default TTY rule. It types the public phrase abandon x11 about and stops at the first "Check the backup now?", clear prompt or pager. It rebuilds the bounded frame with the repository's own Screen emulator (scripts/terminal_session.py:192, which drops rows off the top at finite height and models wrap, cursor up, erase and clear). It then counts share titles and code rows on that frame. Log: lens1.log beside the probe.

Results at 80x24:
(1) `color 24 80 -- --mode direct --threshold 2 --shares 3 --share-format words`: paged False; 41 rows since the clear; titles in render [1,2,3]; titles on frame [2,3]; share 1 code rows 2, on frame 0; question on frame True. Frame row 1 is "MnemoCode · SSKR share 2 of 3".
(2) Default UR format, 2-of-3 direct: titles on frame [2,3]; share 1's single ur:sskr row is on frame row 1 without its title.
(3) `--threshold 3 --shares 3 --share-format words` (all three shares required): titles on frame [2,3]; share 1 code rows on frame 0; question on frame True.
(4) 3-of-5 words: paged True, so every share is reachable.
(5) The NO_COLOR control for 3-of-3 words: 26 rows, titles on frame [1,2,3].

I read the first verifier's ask_no_probe.py critically: it strips only SGR and 1049/25/2004 modes and uses the same emulator. Its log shows the frame after answering No still starting at "share 2 of 3", with share 1 never redrawn.

Source: backup-check.ts:206-221 shows that show() runs on a cleared screen only after a check ends in "Show the result again", which is reachable only after a mismatch. A No answer, or a 2-of-3 check that passes with shares 2 and 3, therefore never redisplays share 1.

`git show 7facfcc` shows that before that commit printShares measured an even shorter compact summary, so this overflow predates 7facfcc and is not introduced by it. grep shows no open UI item covering it: AUD-028-UI001 and AUD-028-UI002 are recorded fixed, and AUD-036-UI001 is unrelated.
- **Expected behaviour:** CHANGELOG.md:5 says every SSKR share stays reachable on a 24-row private screen. CHANGELOG.md:10 says Shamir shares stay visible beside follow-up questions. docs/releases/v0.1.0.md:7 says the complete printed layout determines when pages are needed. AUD-028-UI001 expects every screen-only share to stay accessible until the person finishes copying or checking it. A share set that does not fit beside its heading, notices, fingerprints and the backup-check question should be paged or drawn on a cleared screen.
- **Observed behaviour:** On a colour 80x24 terminal (the default TTY), ordinary unpaged sets overflow the alternate screen, which keeps no scrollback, because PrivateResult measures only the share block.

- 2-of-3 Bytewords, direct mode, default backup question: share 1 is gone entirely, title and both code rows, while the question is shown, and it stays gone after answering No.
- 3-of-3 Bytewords: share 1 is gone even though all three shares are required.
- Default UR: share 1 loses only its title row.
- The candidate's seedshift example with backup check no: share 1's codes remain and only its title, the export facts and the dates-and-mode notice are lost.
- The heading is not lost for every unpaged set: a 2-of-2 UR set keeps it, per the first verifier.
- Under NO_COLOR the same sets fit, because the colour-only result headers are absent.
- The PTY suite always sets NO_COLOR=1, and the colour unit regression from 7facfcc measures printShares in isolation, so neither sees this layout.
- **Impact:** A person copying a screen-only share set on a default-sized colour terminal cannot see one whole share. That share is required in 3-of-3, and there is no page-back. In 2-of-3 the default check passes on shares 2 and 3 and never redisplays share 1. Getting it back means running the command again, which makes a new and incompatible random set, or exporting to files. The numbered titles make the gap noticeable. No secret is disclosed and no wrong share is produced. The release notes and CHANGELOG overstate the AUD-028-UI001 and 7facfcc fixes.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R4+coordinator; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json). A second, independent verifier checked severity and release blocking: agreed.
- **Recommended fix:** Make the page decision from every row that shares the private screen with the shares. That means the rows already printed since the last clear (input echo, the "SSKR export" header and its notices) and the rows that follow (masked words or original copy, fingerprints, the backup-check question or clear prompt). Reuse the existing linesFitPrivateScreen(lines, beside) rule instead of PrivateResult's share-only threshold (CORE-10). Alternatively, clear the screen and render the whole result through PrivateResult. Apply the same sizing to other PrivateResult callers that print rows beside their result. Correct the CHANGELOG and v0.1.0 wording to match the behaviour.
- **Required verification:** Add a real PTY regression at 80x24 with colour enabled (NO_COLOR unset or CLICOLOR_FORCE=1). Cover 2-of-3, 3-of-3 and 2-of-2 sets in Bytewords, UR and Unicode, in direct and seedshift modes. Cover the default backup-check question, the frame after answering No, --backup-check no, --show-original words and --show-masked-words. Each case asserts that every share title and code row is on the current frame or reachable through pages. Repeat at a narrow size such as 32x12. Confirm that NO_COLOR, redirected stdout and the existing 16-of-16 paging checks are unchanged. Rerun the 7facfcc regressions.

##### AUD-037-UI003 — Low — A list-overflow refusal ends decode with exit 1, losing typed codes and dates

- **Category:** UI
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: This breaks a coding-guide rule (CORE-42), but that rule is not a stated release precondition. No secret is exposed, no wrong result is produced, and recovery is still possible: the person can run decode again and pick the wallet or encoded-fingerprint way. The probe showed the private screen is left before the refusal is printed, so the typed codes do not stay on the main screen.
- **Rules:** CORE-42
- **Related:** AUD-037-FUN002, multi-chain-wallet-tools:AUD-034-FUN005, AUD-028-UI002
- **Affected files and builds:** src/cli/missing-codes-search.ts:270-276 (listed() calls ran(way) and catches nothing); src/cli/missing-codes-search.ts:122-151 (ran() only maps Ctrl+C "stopped" to CANCELLED); src/cli/missing-codes-search.ts:159-178 (the way loop goes back to the question only when an outcome is undefined); src/core/missing-codes-search.ts:680,685 (ListWay.run throws core.missing-codes-search.list-overflow); src/cli/decode-command.ts:429 (searchMissingCodes is awaited with no catch). The source is identical at f8d9a93 and the current checkout e28d81e, which changes only docs.
- **Reproduction:** Driver: /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-UI003/pty_run.py, a 120x40 PTY with NO_COLOR=1, running node dist/mnemocode.js. dist was built from f8d9a93.
(1) Cited probe. `decode --ask-secrets --input-file tmp/aud037/r2/direct-17.txt --mode direct --format english --list-candidates`. The file holds abandon x11 followed by 17 checksum-valid last words joined by |. Output: "Code 12 cannot be read: 17 combinations, about 1 candidate." then "mnemocode: More than 16 candidates fit these codes and dates. Narrow the codes or use a wallet check instead of listing them." EXIT: 1.
(2) Menu path with ordinary marks. `decode --ask-secrets --mode direct --format english`, type "legal winner t* ye* wave sausage worth useful legal winner thank yellow" (a public vector), then press 2 = "List every candidate". The screen says "Codes 3 and 4 cannot be read: 242 combinations, about 15 candidates." and the list is offered. Result: the same refusal, EXIT: 1, and the way question is never shown again.
(3) Masked path. `decode --ask-secrets --mode seedshift --format english`, type "la* wo* tired wire weekend secret will valve liquid weird thought clown" (found by verify/AUD-037-UI003/masked_case.mjs: 200 combinations, about 13 candidates), press 3 = "List every candidate", then type the date 23-09-2026. Result: the same refusal, EXIT: 1, so both the typed codes and the typed dates are lost.
(4) Frequency. verify/AUD-037-UI003/overflow_rate.mjs marks two places of the vector phrase with 1- and 2-letter prefixes. 20 of 66 pairs are offered the list way, and 1 of those 20 ends in list-overflow, so ordinary input can reach this path.
(5) verify/AUD-037-UI003/raw_tail.py shows the alternate-screen exit (ESC[?1049l) comes before the refusal text. No secret is left on the main screen.
- **Expected behaviour:** Per CORE-42, an unusable answer is explained in one line and asked again, keeping earlier answers; a command ends only on Escape, Ctrl+C or a critical self-test failure. When the actual list exceeds 16 candidates, decode should show the refusal as a warning and return to "How can MnemoCode tell the right codes?". That is what it already does for the "none" and "several" outcomes, and it is what the refusal message itself suggests ("use a wallet check instead").
- **Observed behaviour:** The list-overflow refusal thrown by ListWay.run passes uncaught through ran(), listed(), searchMissingCodes() and runDecode. The command prints "mnemocode: More than 16 candidates fit these codes and dates. ..." and exits with status 1. The marked codes typed on the private screen are discarded, and so are the dates in masked modes. This happens whether the list way was picked from the menu or given by --list-candidates.
- **Impact:** A person recovering a damaged backup who picks an offered way loses everything typed in the session, including the secret codes and dates, and has to restart decode and type them again. The wallet way they are told to use is not reachable from the session they were in. There is no wrong result, no leak and no permanent loss. The refusal's limit (MAX_LISTED = 16) was added for multi-chain-wallet-tools AUD-034-FUN005 (AUD-030 addendum), and the CLI host was never adapted to it.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R2; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** In listed() in src/cli/missing-codes-search.ts, catch the core refusal by its catalog code core.missing-codes-search.list-overflow. Show its message with terminalNotice(..., "warning") and return undefined, so searchMissingCodes asks for the way again; the given option is already cleared after the first pass. Keep or wipe the dates answer under the existing using/dispose rules, ideally offering it again to the wallet way. Rethrow every other error unchanged.
- **Required verification:** Add a PTY test. Decode "legal winner t* ye* wave sausage worth useful legal winner thank yellow" (direct, english) and choose "List every candidate". The warning must be shown and the way question shown again, with the process still running. Choosing the wallet way must then still work, and Escape must type the codes again. Repeat the test in seedshift mode with typed dates, and with --list-candidates given on the command line. Add a unit test that every non-overflow error from ListWay.run is still rethrown. Run the existing missing-codes-list-boundary tests unchanged.

##### AUD-037-UI004 — Medium — Paged share output clears the result heading, save confirmations, notices and the requested complete backup printed before it, and leaves stale page controls after Enter

- **Category:** UI
- **Severity:** medium
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: Every share stays complete and reachable through the pages. Recovery needs only the shares, plus the dates and mode that the person typed. The backup check (offerShareCheck) still runs afterwards. Nothing leaves the alternate screen, and redirected output is unchanged. What is lost is the result heading, the save confirmations, the safety and keeping notices, and the extra complete-backup copy that --format alone requests. Notices are not checks, so no mandatory integrity, recovery or isolation guarantee fails and no documented safety check on a main path fails. No legal obligation or workspace release precondition is involved; neither AGENTS.md makes UI conformance a release gate. On the menu, the main path of 0.1.0, --format is passed only together with --show-original (encode-questions.ts:349-359). That path loses only the heading, the save confirmation and the notices, not the complete backup.
- **Rules:** CORE-35, CORE-36, CORE-39
- **Related:** AUD-028-UI001, AUD-033-UI001, AUD-037-UI001, AUD-037-UI002
- **Affected files and builds:** src/cli/private-result.ts:11 (NAVIGATION, unstyled); :39-53 (draw: CLEAR_SCREEN at :45 on the alternate screen, then only the page lines, 'Page n/m' and NAVIGATION); :60 (Enter returns without removing the footer). src/cli/sskr-output.ts:171-177 (printShares hands PrivateResult only the share lines with STYLE_CODES stripped). src/cli/sskr-split-command.ts:178-211 (saveShares confirmations and keeping notes, terminalResultHeader 'SSKR export', the holds and dates notices, printEncodedBackup and its warning, all written before show() at :213-223); :219 (printMaskedWords is skipped as 'already shown'). src/cli/terminal.ts:42,64 (CLEAR_SCREEN = ESC[2J ESC[H inside the ESC[?1049h alternate screen, so nothing is left in scrollback). The documented contract is in src/cli/help-options.ts:606 and docs/SSKR.md:25.
- **Reproduction:** HEAD is now 61e163b. `git diff --stat f8d9a93 HEAD` touches only .git-blame-ignore-revs, docs/REMEDIATION.md, docs/audits/README.md, package.json and vitest.config.ts, so src/ is the same as at f8d9a93. No REMEDIATION change mentions the pager.

I wrote my own probe at /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-UI004-lens/probe_lens.py. It uses the repository's scripts/terminal_session.py with dist/mnemocode.js in a Linux PTY at 80x24, the public phrase abandon x11 about, and the date 23-09-2026.

(1) `C28L="seedshift 2 3 unicode 1 --format english"` (colour forced). Result: paged: True. Written before the pager's clear:
- 'MnemoCode · SSKR export' with Threshold 2 of 3, Mode seedshift, Layout text
- 'The shares hold the masked phrase (decoy).'
- 'The shares do not store the dates or the mode; keep them yourself.'
- 'MnemoCode · Encoded result' with Mode, Format and Content
- 'wool abuse actual … congress'
- '! This is the complete backup: no share is needed, but the original dates are still required.'

None of these lines is on the pager frame. Page 1 ends with the orphaned heading 'MnemoCode · SSKR share 3 of 3', then 'Page 1/2' and both navigation lines. The final frame after End and Enter shows 'Page 2/2', '↑/PgUp previous · ↓/PgDn next' and 'Home/End · Enter done · Esc cancel' above '✓ Original fingerprint 73c5da0a' … 'Press Enter to clear this screen.'. Also printed: 'SGR inside first pager frame: False'. Exit 0.

(2) `C28L="seedshift 7 7 unicode 0 --format english --output <probe>/out/t.txt"` (NO_COLOR). Result: paged: True. Written before the clear: 'Saved SSKR records: …/t.txt …', "All 7 shares are needed to restore the masked phrase (decoy), which the owner's dates turn back into…", 'SSKR: 7 of 7 shares required. Mode: seedshift.', the holds and dates notices, 'English BIP39 words of the masked phrase (decoy):', the masked words and 'This is the complete backup …'. None of them is on the pager frame or the final frame. Page 1 also splits share 7's codes across the page boundary. The final frame keeps 'Page 2/2' and both navigation lines above the fingerprints. Exit 0. I then removed both output files and the out folder.

(3) Control runs.
- `seedshift 2 3 unicode 0` (NO_COLOR) and `direct 2 3 words 1` do not page.
- In the colour direct run, the heading and the whole phrase scroll off the 24 rows instead. That is the AUD-037-UI002 mechanism, not this candidate.

The menu offers 'Unicode codes' share format (encode-questions.ts:87-90). Its share run adds --format only after --show-original (encode-questions.ts:349-359), so on the menu path the complete-backup loss does not occur, but the heading, save confirmation and dates notice are still cleared.

Precedent and record checks:
- AUD-028-UI001 (Medium, release blocking false, fixed by introducing PrivateResult) asked for 'result/check retention'.
- AUD-028-UI002 was rated low.
- No REMEDIATION or audit entry records the pager clearing earlier result lines or leaving its footer behind.
- **Expected behaviour:** The help text (help-options.ts:606) and docs/SSKR.md:25 say that with --sskr, --format alone 'shows the complete encoded backup beside the shares'. CORE-36 says a result starts with its 'MnemoCode · <what>' heading and facts. docs/CLI.md:122 says private result pages stay until 'Enter when finished copying'. CORE-35 says hints are grey. So every line of the result should stay reachable on some page or appear after paging ends:
- the export heading and its facts
- the save confirmations and keeping notes
- the holds and dates notices
- any requested complete backup with its warning

When Enter ends paging, the 'Page n/m' counter and the key hints should be gone.
- **Observed behaviour:** Whenever a share set needs pages, PrivateResult.show writes ESC[2J ESC[H on the alternate screen and draws only the share lines, 'Page n/m' and two unstyled navigation lines. This happens with colour for an ordinary 2-of-3 Unicode split, and with NO_COLOR for larger sets such as 7-of-7. Everything encode --sskr wrote before it is erased and never drawn again:
- the SSKR export heading and facts
- 'Saved SSKR records: …'
- the share-keeping note
- the holds and dates notices
- with --format alone, the complete encoded backup or the whole phrase and its warning

printMaskedWords is skipped as 'already shown', so the masked words appear nowhere. After Enter, the last 'Page n/n' and the key hints for keys that no longer act stay above the fingerprints and the clear prompt. All styling is removed inside the pager, and a page break can fall inside a share's code block.
- **Impact:** The documented option `encode --sskr --format N` without --show-original silently fails to show the complete backup the person asked for whenever the shares page on an interactive terminal. The backup is written and cleared at once, and nothing says it is missing.

On the menu path, the summary is erased. So are the save location and the Seedshift reminder that the shares do not hold the dates or the mode. The stale page controls misdescribe the active prompt.

The defect is bounded:
- The shares stay complete and reachable.
- In Seedshift mode the complete backup carries the same masked phrase the shares hold.
- In direct mode it is the phrase the person typed.
- Redirected output, a taller terminal, or a run without --sskr gives the copy.
- No secret leaks and no recovery path is broken.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R4; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed-with-changes); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json). A second, independent verifier checked severity and release blocking: agreed.
- **Recommended fix:** Make the paged view the whole result. Pass the heading and facts, the save confirmations and keeping notes, the holds and dates notices, and any complete backup with its warning to PrivateResult as the opening lines, measured the same way as printed. Alternatively, print them again after the pager returns, before the fingerprints. Fix the unpaged scroll case (AUD-037-UI002) together with this one, because it loses the same lines.

On Enter, redraw the last page without the 'Page n/m' and navigation footer, or clear it, before writing the fingerprints and clear prompt.

Strip owned styles only to measure widths and keep them when drawing. Paint the page counter and key hints with STYLE.muted, as terminal-choice.ts does. Prefer page breaks that keep a share heading with its codes.
- **Required verification:** Add real PTY checks at 80x24, both forced-colour and NO_COLOR, for:
- encode --sskr seedshift 2-of-3 unicode
- 7-of-7 unicode
- 16-of-16 words
- direct 2-of-3 unicode

Run each with and without --format english and with and without --output. They must show that each of these can be reached on some page, or is visible after paging:
- the 'SSKR export' heading and facts
- 'Saved SSKR records'
- the keeping note
- the holds and dates notices
- the complete backup and its warning

After Enter, no 'Page' or navigation line may stay above the fingerprints. A forced-colour check must find the muted SGR on the key hints and the heading style on share titles.

Also cover the menu path: Unicode shares with no original copy must still show the export summary and the dates notice. Redirected stdout and stderr must stay byte-for-byte unchanged. The existing private-result and terminal suites must still pass.

##### AUD-037-UI005 — Low — The page view ignores Home and End sent as ESC [1~ / ESC [4~

- **Category:** UI
- **Severity:** low
- **Status:** open
- **Release blocking:** false: Only a navigation convenience key fails. Up/Down and PageUp/PageDown still reach every page, nothing is hidden, lost or leaked, and no security, integrity or recovery guarantee, legal obligation or release-precondition rule depends on Home/End.
- **Rules:** CORE-10
- **Related:** AUD-028-UI001
- **Affected files and builds:** src/cli/terminal-input.ts:459-468 (readKey: only CSI 5~/6~ and final H/F); src/cli/key-sequences.ts:181-183 (editKeyOf: 1~/7~ as home, 4~/8~ as end); src/cli/private-result.ts:11, 64-65 (on-screen "Home/End" hint and handling); docs/CLI.md:122 (documented Home/End page controls)
- **Reproduction:** Source at HEAD: the working tree is at e28d81e, a docs-only commit after f8d9a93, and git diff f8d9a93 HEAD touches only docs/REMEDIATION.md and docs/audits/README.md. In readKey, a CSI sequence ending in ~ maps only parameters "5" and "6", and any other sequence with parameters returns "other". Home/End are recognised only as final H/F without parameters. editKeyOf, used for line editing, also maps 1/7 ~ to home and 4/8 ~ to end. dist/cli/terminal-input.js has the same logic. Local terminfo (infocmp -1): screen, screen-256color, tmux, tmux-256color and linux all have khome=\E[1~ and kend=\E[4~; rxvt and rxvt-unicode have \E[7~ and \E[8~; xterm has \EOH and \EOF. The R4 probe is unreliable: its page() reads one PTY chunk, and the redraw comes as several writes, so readings lag one key. Its run printed "after ESC [ 1 ~ (Home): Page 3/3" and "after ESC [ H (Home): Page 1/3", which is ambiguous. Corrected probe: `timeout 90 python3 -I /home/user/Documents/bip_tools/tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-UI005/probe_pager_home_end.py`. It drains output until quiet and uses SSKR 16-of-16 word shares of the public phrase at 24x80. Output: "start: Page 1/3"; "ESC[4~ / ESC[1~: End from 1/3 -> 'Page 1/3' (redraw False); Home from 3/3 -> 'Page 3/3' (redraw False)"; "rxvt ESC[8~ / ESC[7~: End from 1/3 -> 'Page 1/3' (redraw False); Home from 3/3 -> 'Page 3/3' (redraw False)"; "xterm ESC[F / ESC[H: End from 1/3 -> 'Page 3/3' (redraw True); Home from 3/3 -> 'Page 1/3' (redraw True)"; "ESCOF / ESCOH: ... 3/3 ... 1/3 (redraw True)"; "PageDown ESC[6~: Page 2/3"; "exit 0". End to end in GNU screen 4.09.01 with SCREENDIR under verify/AUD-037-UI005, `timeout 40 python3 -I probe_screen_bytes.py` prints "screen set outer keypad-transmit (ESC[?1h ESC=): True True", "xterm Home ESC O H -> inner receives ['1b 5b 31 7e']" and "xterm End ESC O F -> inner receives ['1b 5b 34 7e']". `timeout 60 python3 -I probe_screen_home_end.py` runs the pager inside screen: an outer ESC O F leaves "Page 1/3", and after two PageDowns an outer ESC O H leaves "Page 3/3", while ESC O A (Up) moves to "Page 2/3". tmux is not installed here. Its behaviour is inferred from its terminfo (khome=\E[1~, kend=\E[4~). For PuTTY, the inference comes from its documented default Home/End setting ("Standard": ESC[1~ / ESC[4~), not from a run. Windows was not examined.
- **Expected behaviour:** The private result pages advertise Home/End on screen and in docs/CLI.md. These keys should reach the first and last page whatever standard sequence the terminal sends for them, as typed-answer editing already does through editKeyOf: CSI H/F, SS3 H/F, CSI 1~/7~ and CSI 4~/8~.
- **Observed behaviour:** readKey recognises Home/End only as CSI or SS3 H/F, and discards CSI 1~, 4~, 7~ and 8~ as "other" without a redraw. The Linux console, GNU screen and tmux send ESC[1~/ESC[4~, and so does PuTTY by default; rxvt sends ESC[7~/ESC[8~. In these terminals Home and End do nothing on the page view, while the same keys work while editing a typed answer. Inside GNU screen this holds even when the outer terminal is xterm.
- **Impact:** On common terminals and multiplexers, the advertised Home/End controls silently fail, and the documented navigation is misleading. Every page stays reachable with Up/Down or PageUp/PageDown, and no result is hidden, altered or exposed. The impact is a limited interaction defect.
- **Evidence:** Proposed by reviewer R4; verified adversarially at f8d9a93 (confirmed); the verifiers' full reasoning is kept in the local evidence (verification/workflow-result.json).
- **Recommended fix:** Keep one classifier for escape sequences, as CORE-10 requires. readKey should take its home/end decision from editKeyOf or a shared helper in key-sequences.ts rather than its own H/F check, so the pager and line editing accept the same Home/End forms. Add unit tests in test/private-result.test.ts for ESC[1~, ESC[4~, ESC[7~ and ESC[8~, and type ESC[1~ / ESC[4~ in terminal_result_checks.py.
- **Required verification:** Rerun tmp/aud037/verify/AUD-037-UI005/probe_pager_home_end.py and probe_screen_home_end.py against a rebuilt dist. Every form (ESC[1~/4~, ESC[7~/8~, ESC[H/F, ESC O H/F) must move from page 1 to the last page and back with a redraw. Inside GNU screen, outer ESC O H/F must reach the first and last page. New unit and PTY checks for the tilde forms must pass, and the existing PageUp/PageDown, arrow and Ctrl-modified-key behaviour must stay unchanged.
